### Incident: `runDedupe` had not run since 2026-09-06, leaving 3,277 real active hr.ge listings with no canonical opportunity (found 2026-09-15, corpus repaired, recurrence only partially closed)

Found by Codex during an independent design review of an unrelated landing-page plan (the plan's "live proof" numbers were the trigger — it asked Codex to verify them against the real database before any code was written, per this project's established plan-review-before-code convention). Codex queried the live corpus directly rather than trusting the plan's own evidence table, and found the numbers were already stale: **3,705 real source listings existed (3,395 hr.ge, 310 jobs.ge) against only 409 canonical opportunities**, with **3,277 active hr.ge listings holding zero live opportunity membership** — invisible to `/opportunities`, `/ranked`, `/saved`, everywhere except the raw `/listings` view.

**Root cause, confirmed independently against the database, not taken on Codex's word alone:** the newest live `opportunity_source_memberships` decision was dated **2026-09-06T13:28Z** — nine days stale. Every listing hr.ge's growth (100 → 3,395 listings, driven by the full re-crawls recorded under Phase 3C-2 below) added since that date had been crawled, classified, and made browsable as raw listings, but never passed through cross-source canonicalization at all. This is a pure operational gap — `npm run dedupe` was never re-run after the corpus grew — not a code defect.

**Fixed:**
1. `npm run dedupe` (no `--auto-link`, the safe default): scored 3,705 listings, 40,743 pairs, wrote 267 candidates (224 `needs_review`, 37 `confirmed_same`, 6 `probable_same`) — but created **zero** opportunities. Traced why rather than assumed a bug: singleton canonicalization (a listing with no duplicate becoming its own opportunity) is *also* gated behind `--auto-link` in `run-dedupe.ts`, not only cross-source merging. Recorded here because it is a real, non-obvious gap between "the safe default" and "actually closes the gap" that cost a round-trip.
2. Explicit user confirmation obtained before the second, consequential run (a real write to the live corpus at this scale), then `npm run dedupe -- --auto-link`: **3,262 opportunities created, 3,295 memberships created**, in 35.5s. The 37 `confirmed_same` pairs auto-merged (the scorer's own high-confidence, multi-signal restriction — this project's designed production path, not a shortcut); the 224 `needs_review` + 6 `probable_same` stayed queued exactly as candidates, resolvable through the existing `/review` screen (Phase 3C-1), nothing auto-decided among them.
3. **Verified against the live database, not just the CLI's own summary line:** zero active listings on either board now lack a live opportunity membership. Total opportunities: 409 → 3,671. `npm test`: 736/736 still green (test data is disposable and untouched by this).

**Not actually fixed by the above: the recurrence.** A first version of this entry called the incident closed after step 3 — wrong, and caught by the per-commit Codex gate before it could commit that claim. Steps 1–3 repair the corpus as it stood on 2026-09-15; they do nothing to stop the same gap reopening the moment the corpus grows again, since nothing in this repo had ever called `runDedupe` automatically after a crawl, and the CLI's own `--auto-link` defaults off. Left as "fixed," this entry would itself have become exactly the kind of stale status claim this file exists to prevent.

4. **Partial fix for the recurrence:** `scripts/run-jobs-ge-crawl.ps1` — the one wrapper an actual Task Scheduler registration (`register-jobs-ge-schedule.ps1`) would drive — now chains `run-dedupe.js --auto-link` after every crawl attempt, success or failure, and folds a dedupe failure into the wrapper's own exit code so it cannot pass silently (concept §19.1). **Two real limits, stated rather than glossed over:** `Get-ScheduledTask` confirms no such task is actually registered on this machine today, so this wrapper is not currently running unsupervised at all — the fix is real but dormant until someone runs `register-jobs-ge-schedule.ps1`. And **hr.ge has no equivalent wrapper or scheduled task of any kind** — every hr.ge crawl so far, including both full-coverage runs earlier this same day, has been triggered manually via `npm run crawl:hr-ge`, and this fix does not touch that path. Until an hr.ge wrapper exists, **run `npm run dedupe -- --auto-link` by hand after any manual hr.ge crawl** — the same discipline this incident exists to replace, still required on that one path.

**Consequence for the in-flight landing-page plan (Phase 3E, below):** every "live proof" number that plan cited (310 open vacancies, 410 listings tracked) was computed against the stale, pre-fix corpus and is now wrong at a large margin (the corrected open-vacancy count alone is **3,627**, not 310) — revised before any landing-page code was written, per the same review that caught it.

### Incident: both scheduled crawls silently stopped after their first run (2026-09-16), corpus stale for a week (found 2026-09-23, root cause fixed, recovery still pending)

Found by `npm run health:check` while closing out a Phase 7A follow-up (the taxonomy-classification fix, PR #18): both `hr-ge` and `jobs-ge` reported **critical** `run_overdue` (no crawl in 6 days), **critical** `unlinked_active_listings` (**129** hr-ge, **401** jobs-ge — 530 active listings invisible outside the raw `/listings` view), and **warning** `run_possibly_stuck` (a run "running" for 6 days).

**Root cause, confirmed against the live database and Task Scheduler, not assumed:** `crawl_runs` shows both sources' very first scheduled run (2026-09-16 16:10 UTC, the day the schedules were registered) still `status: 'running'`, `reconciled_at: null`, six days later. `schtasks /query /fo list /v` on both tasks showed **`Power Management: Stop On Battery Mode, No Start On Batteries`** and a `Last Run Time` of only 2026-09-18 15:04:59 (a single `-StartWhenAvailable` catch-up two days late, itself recorded `Last Result: 1`) — no log file exists in `logs/` for either source after 2026-09-18 at all, meaning Task Scheduler never even launched the wrapper on any later trigger, not that it launched and failed quietly. `register-crawl-schedule.ps1`'s `New-ScheduledTaskSettingsSet` call never overrode `DisallowStartIfOnBatteries`/`StopIfGoingOnBatteries`, both `$true` by default: the first run was almost certainly killed mid-crawl when this machine switched off AC power, which explains the stuck `running` row (killed processes don't get to write `status: 'failed'`), and every later trigger then either refused to start on battery or was blocked outright by the CLI's own exclusivity guard (`src/cli/run-hr-ge-crawl.ts` / `run-jobs-ge-crawl.ts`) seeing the stuck row and refusing to start a second run for the same source — two independent reasons stacked, either one sufficient to explain a week of silence.

**Fixed:** `register-crawl-schedule.ps1` now explicitly disables both battery restrictions (`-DisallowStartIfOnBatteries:$false -StopIfGoingOnBatteries:$false`) — an unattended crawl on a laptop cannot assume AC power. Committed directly to `main` (governance/ops script, per this file's own git-workflow allow-list).

**Not yet fixed — needs the project owner to run directly, blocked in this session by the harness's own write-safety sandboxing (direct live-database writes and re-registering a live scheduled task are both outside what this session's tool permissions allow, correctly so given this project's DB-safety history):**
1. Reconcile the two stuck rows so they stop blocking every future attempt: `update crawl_runs set status = 'failed', finished_at = now(), reconciled_at = now() where id in ('2d030dcf-69a8-41a2-b756-439c487381e0', '77c999c9-0e6b-4452-9f49-abbd2ebd92c4');` (jobs-ge, hr-ge respectively).
2. Re-register both schedules with the fix: `./scripts/register-crawl-schedule.ps1 -Source jobs-ge` and `-Source hr-ge`.
3. After a real crawl completes on each (chained dedupe + taxonomy backfill will run automatically), confirm `npm run health:check` clears both sources' criticals.

**Not addressed, deliberately out of scope here:** self-healing for a stuck `running` row is not built — `health:check` surfacing it (which it did, correctly, the moment someone looked) is this project's current design, not a gap this incident calls for closing on its own; that would be a `Phase 7B` "supervised repair" concern, not a same-day fix.

## Current phase: Phase 8B — surfaces and admin boundary

**Phase 8A is merged (PR #19)**, concluding with an honest lexical-first recommendation for its one tested candidate (below, now "Earlier phase"). Phase 8B was chosen next by explicit project-owner decision — its own precondition (change.md §2: Phase 6 merged, or a clean worktree off reviewed `main`) is satisfied, same as 8A's was, and it does not depend on 8A's candidate-model outcome at all (change.md §16 defers "winning multilingual model" as a non-blocking decision).

**Scope, per `change.md` §13/§18's Phase 8B description and concept §30.2/§30.4/§9:** a validated `local|public|admin` runtime-surface split, real auth on the admin surface, the three already-built operational screens (`/health`, `/review`, `/taxonomy-review`) migrated under `/admin/*` with authorization enforced at the data layer (not just hidden nav), a public-safe query boundary, database role separation, and an admin audit trail. **No new public-facing product surface** — `/`, `/opportunities`, `/listings` already exist and keep their current URLs; Phase 8D (browser CV Ranked) is what actually builds new public UI, gated on 8B's boundary existing first.

### Stage plan (written 2026-09-23, before any code)

**Grounded in what's actually in this repo today, checked live rather than assumed:**
- Current routes, all served from one undifferentiated surface: `/`, `/opportunities`, `/opportunities/[id]`, `/listings`, `/profile`, `/profile/[id]`, `/ranked`, `/saved`, `/review`, `/taxonomy-review`, `/health`, `/drafts`, `/drafts/[id]`, `/drafts/new` — no route groups, no auth, no per-surface config anywhere in `web/app/`.
- **Next.js 16 renamed `middleware.js` to `proxy.js`** (confirmed against this repo's own bundled `node_modules/next/dist/docs/`, per `web/AGENTS.md`'s own warning that this version differs from training data) — the optimistic surface-routing layer below is a `proxy.ts`, not a `middleware.ts`.
- **Auth.js v5** (`next-auth@beta`, confirmed via `npm view` to support `next@^16.0.0` and `react@^19.0.0`) is the auth library, with the GitHub provider — **provider choice made by explicit project-owner decision** (GitHub, since this project's own GitHub account and 2FA already exist; the alternative offered was Google or a swappable stub). Confirmed against Auth.js's current docs (Context7): env vars auto-infer as `AUTH_GITHUB_ID`/`AUTH_GITHUB_SECRET`/`AUTH_SECRET`, callback path `/api/auth/callback/github`, and its own docs already show the v5 `proxy.ts` integration pattern (`export const proxy = auth(...)`) — not stale for the middleware→proxy rename.
- `src/db/client.ts` today is one memoized `Pool` off one `DATABASE_URL` — no per-role connection exists yet; that is genuinely new infrastructure, not a config toggle.
- An `audit_events` table already exists (Phase 6A, `src/db/schema/outreach.ts`) but its `audit_action` enum is closed to outreach-lifecycle values (`draft_generated`, `draft_approved`, …) — reusing it for admin actions would mean growing outreach's own enum with unrelated values. A separate `admin_audit_events` table, same "ids/hashes only, never content" discipline, is cleaner than overloading it.

**Needs the project owner directly, before Stage 3 can be tested end-to-end:** a registered GitHub OAuth App (github.com Settings → Developer settings → OAuth Apps → New OAuth App), homepage URL `http://localhost:3000`, callback URL `http://localhost:3000/api/auth/callback/github` for local dev. The Client ID and Client Secret go straight into `.env` as `AUTH_GITHUB_ID`/`AUTH_GITHUB_SECRET` (never pasted into chat) — this session cannot register an OAuth App on someone's account itself. `AUTH_SECRET` (`openssl rand -base64 32` or equivalent) is needed regardless of provider.

**Stages, reordered and expanded across multiple Codex review rounds against this plan itself, all findings folded in before any code — see the discharge note after the stage list:**
1. **`XTELO_SURFACE` + route-group reorganization, no behavior change.** Add a validated `XTELO_SURFACE=local|public|admin` env var (fail closed on anything else). Move every existing route into `web/app/(local)/...` unchanged — a purely mechanical, URL-preserving move — so this refactor is reviewed and browser-QA'd on its own before any new surface logic touches it. Full suite + a manual pass over a handful of existing pages is this stage's own gate.
2. **Guard every existing local-only Server Action, not just the two on soon-to-be-public pages.** Any exported Server Action is independently reachable by a direct request regardless of which page nominally renders it — proxy/layout checks and `XTELO_WRITES_ENABLED` are not authorization for it, the same principle this project already applies everywhere else. Every export in `web/app/drafts/actions.ts`, `web/app/profile/actions.ts`, `web/app/profile/[id]/actions.ts`, `web/app/saved/actions.ts`, `web/app/opportunities/[id]/actions.ts` (`detachFromOpportunity`), `web/app/review/actions.ts` and `web/app/taxonomy-review/actions.ts` gets an `assertLocalSurface()` guard (same shape as `web/lib/writes.ts`'s existing `assertWritesEnabled()`) at its top, throwing unless `XTELO_SURFACE === 'local'`. **One deliberate, stated exception:** `web/app/actions.ts`'s `toggleLocale` sets a display-language cookie, mutates no data, and is reasonable to leave reachable everywhere. The review/taxonomy-review actions keep this same-surface-only guard even though Stage 8 later gives admin its *own*, separate wrapper actions calling the same underlying `src/` logic — not these Server Actions directly (see Stage 8).
3. **Public database role — created, wired and verified before any public route is reachable, not four stages later.** A `SELECT`-only Postgres role on explicit public views, and the public process's `DATABASE_URL` pointed at it. Sequenced here specifically because Stage 6 below turns on public routing: if that happened before this role exists and is actually wired in, a public-configured process would serve real traffic while still holding the current broad, write-capable credential — a defense-in-depth gap independent of whether every mutation and query happens to be correctly guarded elsewhere. Same real-DDL-against-the-live-database caveat as every role stage in this file: written here, run and verified (`\du+`, plus a real query that should fail under this role) by the project owner.
4. **Public-safe query boundary — surface-aware, not a blanket swap, and respecting source-republishing policy.** A dedicated, narrow query module for public-surface pages, excluding shortlist decisions, source-health data, quarantined listings and internal dedupe evidence by construction (an explicit column/table allowlist). **Selected by `XTELO_SURFACE` at render time, not substituted for the existing query wholesale** — `/opportunities` and `/opportunities/[id]` keep serving `local` visitors their full current view (shortlist state, dedupe evidence, the detach/undo workflow intact), and only switch to the restricted module when `XTELO_SURFACE === 'public'`; an earlier draft of this stage would have silently regressed local's own "full navigation and workflow preserved" requirement by pointing the shared route files at the restricted module unconditionally. Also enforces `display.mayRepublishFullContent` (`src/policies/jobs-ge.ts` and `hr-ge.ts` both currently set this **`false`** — confirmed live, not assumed): the public DTO omits or truncates full source description text whenever the owning source's policy says republishing isn't yet cleared, independent of whatever else that DTO already excludes. Covers `/` (`getSourceHealth`), `/opportunities`, `/opportunities/[id]` (`decisionsByOpportunity` plus dedupe evidence) and `/listings`.
5. **Public layout and navigation.** A public-specific layout (concept §30.1's own defined nav: `Browse | Listings`, `CV Ranked` withheld until Phase 8D actually builds it) replaces the shared `SiteHeader` for the public surface — that header's links to `/profile`, `/ranked`, `/drafts`, `/review`, `/taxonomy-review`, `/health` would all 404 once Stage 6 allow-lists routes, and it also renders the `scraplify_qa`/"writes on" database chip, operational detail a public visitor has no business seeing.
6. **`proxy.ts`: optimistic surface allow-listing — Auth.js invoked only on admin paths.** Public allows `/`, `/opportunities*`, `/listings*`; admin allows `/admin*` and `/api/auth/*`; local (today's default) allows everything. Everything else 404s — **except** the exclusions Next.js's own bundled Proxy guide requires (confirmed against `node_modules/next/dist/docs/.../proxy.md`, per `web/AGENTS.md`'s scoped instruction to read it first): Proxy runs on `_next/static`, `_next/image` and public asset requests too, so the `matcher` config must exclude them the same way Next's own authentication-guide example does, or a default-deny breaks CSS/JS/fonts/images on every surface. **Not** the simple `export const proxy = auth(async function proxy(req) {...})` wrapper Auth.js's own docs show as the default pattern — that would run Auth.js's own initialization (which validates `AUTH_SECRET` and the provider credentials) on *every* request, including `public` and `local`, which by design (concept §30.2: "the `public` process holds neither an admin secret nor a write-capable/admin database credential") never have those env vars set at all. `proxy.ts` branches on the request path *before* touching Auth.js: `public`/`local` paths run plain surface/asset matching with no Auth.js involvement whatsoever; only `/admin*` requests reach the `auth(...)`-wrapped check. By the time this stage runs, Stages 2-5 mean turning it on is safe: mutations are guarded, the public DB role is isolated, queries are surface-aware and policy-compliant, and the layout is ready. Per Next.js's own authentication guide (Context7): this stage itself is still only a UX optimization reading a cookie, never a database — the real boundary is Stage 8.
7. **Auth integration.** `auth.ts` (full config) + `auth.config.ts` (edge-compatible, no adapter, for `proxy.ts`), GitHub provider, a `jwt`/`session` callback stamping `isAdmin: true` only when the GitHub numeric `id` (immutable, unlike a login name) is in an explicit allowlist (`ADMIN_GITHUB_IDS`). Fails closed: absent env vars block all admin access rather than silently allowing it. Route handler at `web/app/api/auth/[...nextauth]/route.ts`.
8. **Real authorization at the data layer — the actual boundary — plus the admin shell itself.** A `requireAdmin()` DAL helper (the pattern Next.js's own authentication guide recommends), called first in every admin page, Server Action and Route Handler, independent of the Stage 6 proxy check. Also builds what change.md §5/§13 actually asks for: an `/admin` dashboard root and its own layout/nav. **`/health`, `/review` and `/taxonomy-review` are *not* moved** — change.md's own requirement that the local surface preserve current URLs and full navigation unchanged means those paths keep working exactly as they do today, unauthenticated, on a `local`-configured instance, with their existing Server Actions unchanged apart from Stage 2's guard. New route files at `/admin/sources`, `/admin/duplicates`, `/admin/taxonomy` (change.md §5's own route table — `/admin/matching` and `/admin/operations` are not built here, since nothing backs them yet) import the same underlying **data-fetching** functions from `src/browse/queries.ts` for reads. For the duplicate/taxonomy **mutations**, admin gets its own new Server Action wrapper files (e.g. `web/app/(admin)/admin/duplicates/actions.ts`) calling the same underlying `src/dedupe/*`/`src/taxonomy/*` business logic the local actions also call, but with their own `requireAdmin()` guard and their own `redirect`/`revalidatePath` targets pointed at `/admin/duplicates`/`/admin/taxonomy` — **not** the existing `web/app/review/actions.ts`/`taxonomy-review/actions.ts` Server Actions directly, which hardcode `redirect('/review')` and `revalidatePath('/taxonomy-review')` (confirmed live, not assumed) and would send a successful admin mutation to a URL the admin allow-list itself 404s.
9. **Remaining database roles.** Concept §30.2's other three: admin (only the mutations Stage 8 implements), **worker** (crawl/dedupe/taxonomy/embedding/publication writes — today's existing CLI tools, currently on the same broad `DATABASE_URL` as everything else) and **migration** (DDL at deploy time only) — leaving worker and migration on the shared credential would mean the worker role still carries migration-level privileges. Same real-DDL/owner-verifies pattern as Stage 3.
10. **Enforcement tests — crafted actions AND the authorized path, correctly split by what each guard actually checks.** Route-level proof (an unauthenticated `/admin/*` request redirects/404s; a `local`-only path 404s under `XTELO_SURFACE=public`; local surface still serves everything; static assets still load under every surface, closing the Stage 6 gap) is necessary but not sufficient: a `requireAdmin()` accidentally omitted from one mutation would still pass every route-level assertion — and a `requireAdmin()` that rejects *everyone*, including a genuinely allowlisted admin, would pass every denial test too while shipping an unusable dashboard. The two guard types check different things, so their tests differ: Stage 2's `assertLocalSurface()`-guarded actions authorize by **`XTELO_SURFACE`, not user identity** — each gets a test invoking it directly under `public`/`admin` (asserting throw, no database row changed) **and** a positive test under `local` confirming it still succeeds unauthenticated, exactly as today; there is no "non-allowlisted user" variant for these, since inventing one would misdescribe what the guard does and risks pushing an implementation toward adding identity checks the local workflow was never meant to need. Stage 8's `requireAdmin()`-guarded admin actions **do** authorize by identity — each gets the full matrix: unauthenticated (rejects), authenticated-but-not-allowlisted (rejects), and allowlisted (succeeds, mutation genuinely commits, redirect lands on an admin URL that itself resolves) — change.md §14's own test-gate line requires auth coverage for every admin page, action and handler, not only its rejections. **Plus one CSRF-specific case** `docs/THREAT_MODEL.md` names explicitly: a forged cross-origin POST against an admin action, with an allowlisted session cookie attached, using the `Origin`/`Host`/`X-Forwarded-Host` header shape the real admin reverse-proxy deployment will actually produce (not just same-origin `localhost`) — asserting Next's own Server Action CSRF check rejects it, since the header comparison it depends on is exactly what a reverse proxy in front of `admin.<domain>` can perturb if misconfigured.
11. **Admin audit foundation — all three outcomes durable, not just two.** `admin_audit_events` (actor GitHub id, entity type/id, action, **outcome** — `succeeded` | `refused` | `failed`, not assumed-success — timestamp, `details` jsonb restricted to ids/hashes, same discipline as the existing outreach audit table). Three distinct cases, three distinct write paths: a **successful** mutation's audit row commits inside the *same* transaction as the mutation itself, so the two rise or fall together; a **refusal** (`requireAdmin()` rejects before anything starts, so no mutation transaction exists yet) gets its own immediate write; a mutation that **starts** (passes `requireAdmin()`, opens its transaction) and then genuinely throws gets its transaction rolled back same as any failed transaction — an audit insert placed inside that same transaction would vanish with it, so the `failed` outcome is written in a **separate** statement issued from the `catch`, after the rollback has already happened, not from inside the transaction that just failed.

**Exit gate (8B), as change.md itself states it:** unauthenticated/public requests cannot read or mutate local/admin resources by direct URL or crafted action; local workflows remain intact.

**This plan's own Codex review findings, across five rounds, are recorded here rather than silently folded in, since it is unusual for a review to target a planning document rather than code:**
- Round 1: stale "Upcoming phases" entries (fixed below), mutation isolation needed sequencing before public exposure, crafted-action tests, audit-outcome persistence, and the missing admin-shell stage.
- Round 2: the public-safe query boundary was still sequenced *after* the stage that makes public routes reachable, leaving a real read-leak window between them (now Stage 3, before Stage 5's allow-listing, not Stage 9 as first drafted); the `proxy.ts` stage's "everything else 404s" would have also blocked `_next/static`/`_next/image`/public assets without an explicit matcher exclusion; the audit-atomicity fix from round 1 over-corrected (a *successful* mutation now audits inside its own transaction, atomic with it; only a *refusal*, which has no mutation transaction to attach to, gets a separate write); a public layout/nav stage was missing for the same "shared header 404s and leaks the writes chip" reason the admin shell needed one; and this file's own "Phase 7 is fully closed" wording (below) contradicted the same paragraph's "7B remains deferred" — corrected to name 7A and its follow-up specifically.
- Round 3: Stage 3's public-safe query boundary still missed two of the four routes Stage 5 actually exposes (`/` calls `getSourceHealth`; `/opportunities/[id]` calls `decisionsByOpportunity` and renders internal dedupe evidence) — both added. Stage 7 said "migrate"/"move" `/health`, `/review`, `/taxonomy-review`, which would have deleted those URLs from the local surface, directly contradicting change.md's own "local preserves current URLs and full navigation" requirement — corrected to duplicate the route (new `/admin/*` files reusing the same underlying functions) rather than move it, so local keeps working unchanged. Stage 8 built only 2 of the 4 database roles concept §30.2 actually names (worker and migration were missing, leaving the worker credential with migration-level privileges) — both added, plus gating completion on the owner verifying privileges, not just running the SQL. Stage 9 tested only denial paths, which a `requireAdmin()` that rejects everyone would also pass — added the authorized-admin-succeeds side. Stage 10's audit design still lost a `failed` (as opposed to `refused`) outcome to its own mutation's rollback — added a third, separate post-rollback write for that case specifically.
- Round 4: the public database role was still sequenced four stages after the proxy stage that exposes public routes — a real window where a "public" process would serve traffic on the current broad, write-capable credential — moved to its own stage immediately before public exposure, with the other three roles kept later since they don't gate it. The mutation-guard stage covered only 2 of ~15 exported Server Actions across the app; broadened to every one of them (one stated, reasoned exception: `toggleLocale`, which mutates no data). The query-boundary stage would have swapped the shared `/opportunities` route files to the restricted module unconditionally, regressing `local`'s own full shortlist/dedupe-evidence workflow — corrected to select the query by `XTELO_SURFACE` at render time. The same stage was also missing enforcement of `display.mayRepublishFullContent` (confirmed `false` on both live source policies), a real source-content republishing constraint distinct from the internal-data exclusions already planned. The admin-shell stage would have reused `web/app/review/actions.ts`/`taxonomy-review/actions.ts` directly, whose hardcoded `redirect('/review')`/`revalidatePath('/taxonomy-review')` (confirmed live) would send a successful admin mutation to a URL the admin allow-list itself 404s — corrected to give admin its own wrapper actions with their own redirect/revalidate targets. Two stale cross-references outside this file (`AGENTS.md`'s "Phase 8 not yet started", `docs/scraplify-concept.md` §30's "Phase 8 has not started") were also still contradicting this file's own current-phase declaration — both fixed in the same change. A STATUS.md line claiming "nothing about embeddings... has been implemented" contradicted 8A's own real embedding record — narrowed to what's actually still missing (a production pipeline, not the infrastructure).
- Round 5: the `proxy.ts` stage's endorsed Auth.js pattern (`export const proxy = auth(...)`) would have run Auth.js's own secret/provider validation on *every* request, including `public` and `local`, which by design never carry `AUTH_SECRET` at all — corrected to branch on path before touching Auth.js, so only `/admin*` requests reach it. The enforcement-tests stage asked for "unauthenticated and non-allowlisted-user" variants of Stage 2's `assertLocalSurface()` tests, which authorize by `XTELO_SURFACE` and have no user-identity concept at all — corrected to the actual matrix (reject under `public`/`admin`, succeed unauthenticated under `local`), with identity-based variants correctly reserved for Stage 8's `requireAdmin()` actions; also added the one CSRF case `docs/THREAT_MODEL.md` names explicitly (a forged cross-origin POST against an admin action, under the actual reverse-proxy header shape, not just same-origin `localhost`). Two more stale "Phase 8 not started" entrypoints outside this file (`change.md`'s own reconciliation note, root `CLAUDE.md`'s "Hosted edition (planned)" section) were still telling a fresh reader to expect nothing — both fixed. Phase 8A's own two remaining exit-gate checkboxes were left unchecked while this file already described 8A as concluded — checked off with the narrower evidence each one actually has (the 300+-label item via its own "state plainly why not" branch; the review item via the same standing-instruction waiver already recorded in 8A's build record).

## Earlier phase: Phase 8A — private matching feasibility

**Phase 7A and its one follow-up (newly-crawled-listing classification) are merged** (PR #16, PR #18) — **Phase 7B itself remains open and deferred**, not closed, evidence-gated on the 7A schedules actually running (concept §25); they had in fact been silently dead for a week, fixed the same day this phase started (see the incident above), so there is no multi-day evidence to build 7B on yet. Phase 4 is also merged (below, now "Earlier phase"). Phase 8A was chosen next (2026-09-23) by explicit instruction, ahead of Phase 7B, since Phase 8A's own precondition (Phase 6 merged before any Phase 8 branch) was already satisfied while 7B's was not.

**Scope, per `change.md` §13/§18 (the accepted implementation handoff) and concept §30.6:** a feasibility spike only — pin one candidate embedding model and its redistribution license, build a privacy-safe evaluation harness, prove the exact same model/runtime produces matching vectors in Node and in a browser Web Worker (the "model contract" change.md §7 insists on, since vacancy and CV vectors are only comparable when produced identically), and measure size/latency/memory against change.md §14's performance gates. **No public route, no migration, no production dependency, no admin surface** — all deferred to 8B onward.

### Stage plan (written 2026-09-23, before any code)

**Candidate model, checked live before picking anything (change.md §18 step 4):** `intfloat/multilingual-e5-small` — confirmed directly from its Hugging Face model card and `config.json`: **MIT license** (redistributable), **Georgian (`ka`) explicitly listed among trained languages**, `hidden_size: 384`, `max_position_embeddings: 512`. A Transformers.js-compatible ONNX port already exists at `Xenova/multilingual-e5-small` (the standard community-conversion pattern this ecosystem uses, per Transformers.js's own docs). The E5 family's well-documented convention — not verified against this specific card's usage section, which did not load in full, but standard across every `e5`/`multilingual-e5` release — is a `"query: "` / `"passage: "` input prefix, mean pooling over non-padding tokens, and L2 normalization; this stays an assumption to confirm against the model's own tokenizer/config during Stage 1, not asserted as fact yet. Runtime: `@huggingface/transformers` (the maintained successor to `@xenova/transformers`), which supports `env.allowRemoteModels = false` plus a `localModelPath` for fully self-hosted, no-remote-download operation in both Node and the browser — confirmed against its current docs (Context7), not assumed from training data.

**Stages:**
1. **Pin and record.** Add `@huggingface/transformers` as a dependency. Vendor the exact ONNX/tokenizer files (model weights, `tokenizer.json`, `config.json`) into a self-hosted location (not a runtime download from the Hugging Face Hub), record their SHA-256 hashes and the upstream revision/commit pinned, and write the license/attribution down in-repo. Confirm the query:/passage: prefix and pooling convention directly against the vendored tokenizer/config rather than assumed.
2. **Evaluation harness scaffold** (`src/matching/eval/`): a fixed, versioned corpus format (profile text + a real, traceable opportunity id + a human relevance judgment), and scorers for Recall@20 and NDCG@10 per change.md §12.
3. **Synthetic profile set.** 15+ representative Georgian/English/mixed-language CVs, written as clearly synthetic fixtures (no real person's data) — this is the one part of the evaluation corpus this phase can build without outside input, per change.md §12's own minimum.
4. **Node embedding script**: embed the synthetic profiles and a real, current sample of canonical opportunities (traceable ids, live corpus) with the pinned model; store the raw vectors as fixed golden output.
5. **Browser Web Worker parity proof**: the identical model/runtime, run client-side via WASM against the same fixed non-personal text, behind no public route (a local-only test harness page or a Playwright-driven fixture, not a shipped screen). Assert the Node and browser vectors match within a tight numeric tolerance — this is the phase's central technical question.
6. **Isolated worker proof**: PDF/DOCX extraction (reusing the existing Mammoth path for DOCX; a self-hosted pinned PDF.js worker for PDF) plus embedding, entirely inside a Web Worker, with a Playwright network-assertion test proving zero requests beyond allowlisted same-origin static assets — no upload, no third-party call.
7. **Measure**: model/runtime download size, cold/warm load time, embedding latency and peak memory in a real browser at the four required widths, against change.md §14's gates (warm <=5s, cold <=20s on an agreed mid-range device, ranking off the main thread, reassess past 25MB compressed or 250ms p75).

**Explicitly not attempted in this phase, stated rather than silently skipped:** change.md §12's **quantitative acceptance gate** (Recall@20 >= 0.90, meaningful NDCG@10 lift, zero hard-filter violations) needs **300+ human-labelled profile/opportunity judgments from real, traceable listings** — real human relevance judgment, not something to fabricate, and this project's hard rule against invented data applies here exactly as it does to rendered listings. Stages 1-7 above build every piece of infrastructure that gate needs to run the moment a labelled set exists, but the set itself is a separate, human task for the project owner. Until it exists, this phase's exit is change.md §13's own fallback: **"the branch records an honest lexical-first decision"** rather than a claimed quantitative win — Stage 7's measured size/latency/memory and the Stage 5 parity proof are still real, checkable evidence on their own, just not the full quality gate.

**Exit gate (8A):**
- [x] One candidate model/runtime is pinned, self-hosted, and its license recorded.
- [x] Node and browser-Worker embeddings of the same fixed text match within tolerance — the model-contract question this phase exists to answer.
- [x] *(Narrower than worded)* The isolated worker proof shows zero non-allowlisted network requests during **embedding** — real, measured, in a real Chrome instance. PDF/DOCX parsing was not added to the worker: once Stage 7 showed this candidate impractical (below), building the full extraction pipeline for a model already being rejected had no return: that proof belongs against whatever model actually gets chosen next, not this one.
- [x] *(Narrower than worded)* Size/latency are measured against change.md §14's gates, under a real ~10Mbps/5Mbps/40ms network throttle (not the four UI widths — there is no rendered screen in this no-UI spike for that convention to apply to; peak memory was not separately profiled). This is the substantive part of the gate a worker-only phase can actually exercise.
- [x] Either the 300+ label quantitative gate is met, or the branch says plainly that it isn't and why — never a claimed pass without the evidence. *(The second branch: the build record's "Conclusion" states plainly the 300+ label set was never attempted, and why — a human labeling task, not something to fabricate — rather than claiming a quantitative pass.)*
- [x] Per-commit review clean or recorded OWED; whole-branch review run or explicitly waived. *(Neither ran: skipped per this session's standing instruction not to wait on Codex's usage-limit cooldown, recorded in the build record below rather than silently — the same "explicit decision, recorded rather than quietly satisfied" shape as every other waiver in this file.)*

### Build record (2026-09-23, in progress)

**Stage 1 (pin and record) — done, with an important measured finding.** Added `@huggingface/transformers` (dependency, not dev-only — it runs in the shipped Web Worker too). `src/matching/models/multilingual-e5-small.ts` pins `Xenova/multilingual-e5-small` by its exact HF commit `sha` (not `main`), records the upstream MIT license and Georgian-language confirmation, and hashes every vendored file (`config.json`, `tokenizer_config.json`, `tokenizer.json`, the ONNX weights) by SHA-256 — checked live, not copied from memory.

**The size measurement changes this phase's likely direction.** All three `dtype` variants were downloaded and measured on this machine: **fp32 470,268,533 bytes, int8 (`q8`) 118,308,185 bytes, q4 398,649,233 bytes.** q4 is *larger* than int8 for this model — surprising, and recorded as measured rather than explained away; the most likely cause is the ~250k-token multilingual vocabulary's embedding table not compressing the same way under blockwise 4-bit packing as it does under plain int8, but that is an inference, not a confirmed fact. int8 is the best of the three at 118MB — still **~4.7x over** change.md §14's 25MB-compressed reassessment threshold. This is very likely a structural property of multilingual sentence embedding in general (a shared large subword vocabulary is what gives a model broad language coverage, including Georgian, and that vocabulary's embedding table dominates total size regardless of how many transformer layers sit above it) rather than a defect specific to this one candidate — stated as reasoning, not verified against a second candidate yet. A quick sanity check on quality: cosine similarity between an English and a Georgian sentence describing the same accountant profile was **0.907-0.916** across all three dtypes (a real cross-lingual signal, consistent across quantization levels), versus **0.80-0.82** between that English accountant sentence and an unrelated English frontend-developer sentence — a real but modest gap, exactly the kind of thing Stage 2's proper Recall@20/NDCG@10 metrics exist to judge instead of eyeballing raw cosine numbers.

**Stage 2 (evaluation harness scaffold) — metrics done and tested; corpus format still open.** `src/matching/eval/metrics.ts`: pure `recallAtK` and `ndcgAtK`, both taking a ranked id list and a relevance-judgment map, with hand-computed-by-hand test cases (`metrics.test.ts`, 9 tests, including the "unjudged id counts as grade 0, not excluded" rule and both metrics' vacuous-1 case when nothing is relevant). No model or corpus dependency, so these are real `npm test` coverage now, not deferred. Full suite 857/857, typecheck and lint clean.

**Stage 3 (synthetic profiles) — done.** `src/matching/eval/fixtures/synthetic-profiles.ts`: 18 profiles (exceeds the 15 minimum), Georgian/English/mixed, spanning role/seniority/location, deliberately written without a personal name. 4 guardrail tests (unique ids, non-empty text, all three language categories present, every `ka`/`mixed` entry actually contains Georgian script).

**Stage 4 (Node embedding script) — done, run for real against the live corpus.** `src/matching/embed.ts` wraps the pinned model with `env.allowRemoteModels = false` against the `.matching-models/` self-hosted copy — no network at embedding time, verified by it actually working (the vendoring script ran first). `src/matching/opportunity-embedding-input.ts` samples real, traceable canonical opportunities (title + organization + locations + truncated description, per change.md §8's composition, simplified for a spike: any one live member per opportunity rather than the "most authoritative" pick that matters for the Phase 8C production bundle). `npm run matching:embed-eval-corpus` embedded all 18 synthetic profiles (as E5 queries) and a real sample of **40 live canonical opportunities** (as E5 passages) and wrote `src/matching/eval/fixtures/golden-vectors.node.json` (committed, 671KB) — this is the fixed reference Stage 5's browser-Worker parity test compares against. `golden-vectors.test.ts` (4 tests) guards it against change.md §14's own named failure modes: wrong model/revision pin, wrong dimension, non-finite values, and a broken normalization — all real checks against the actually-generated file, not assumed. Full suite 865/865.

**Stages 5, 6 and 7 (partial) — done, in a real Chrome instance, and they settle this phase's central question.** `scripts/matching-parity/` (not shipped code — a throwaway local harness, per the stage plan): a minimal static server (`server.mjs`), a Web Worker (`worker.mjs`, bundled to `worker.bundle.mjs` by `bundle-worker.mjs` since `@huggingface/transformers`'s browser dist has an internal bare-specifier import, `onnxruntime-web/webgpu`, that fails to resolve when served unbundled — found and fixed while building this, not assumed), and an orchestrator (`check.mjs`) driving real Chrome via `@playwright/test`'s bundled `chromium.launch({ channel: 'chrome' })` (this machine has no access to Playwright's own Chromium download, same reason `playwright.config.ts` already uses the system channel).

- **Stage 5 (parity) — real match, precisely explained, not just asserted.** Cosine similarity between the committed Node golden vectors and freshly-computed browser vectors for 6 real texts (3 synthetic profiles, 3 real opportunities): **0.9973-0.9982**. A control experiment isolates why it isn't 1.0: the same two backends (`onnxruntime-node` vs `onnxruntime-web`/WASM) embedding the same text at **fp32** agree to cosine **0.9999999999538** (max abs diff ~6e-8 — floating-point noise). The gap only appears at this pin's actual **q8** dtype, so it is int8 dequantization/kernel differences between the two backends, not a bug in prefix/pooling/normalization (identical code paths in both, and the fp32 control proves they agree when quantization isn't in the picture). The model-contract question this phase exists to answer — does the same model/runtime produce matching vectors in Node and a browser Worker — is answered **yes**, with a quantified, explained margin.
- **Stage 6 (isolation) — clean.** 10 requests observed loading the harness and running two embedding calls; every one was same-origin, within the `/harness/`, `/vendor/ort/` (the ONNX WASM binary + loader) or `/models/` (the self-hosted model files) allowlist. One `blob:` URL (ONNX Runtime's own Worker bootstrapping) is same-origin/in-memory, not network egress, and is excluded from the check rather than miscounted as a violation (an earlier version of this check got that wrong and had to be fixed). PDF/DOCX extraction was not added to the worker for this proof — see the exit-gate note above for why.
- **Stage 7 (partial) — decisive, and not favorable to this candidate.** Cold load (first `pipeline()` call: model download + WASM compile + session init), measured under a real CDP network throttle (~10Mbps down / 5Mbps up / 40ms latency — decent home broadband, not a worst case) rather than localhost speed (which would make the 118MB finding meaningless to check): **126,215ms — 6.3x over change.md §14's 20-second cold-load gate.** Warm reuse (embedder already loaded) is instant, and embed latency for 3 texts is 155-321ms either way — the runtime itself is fast; it is the one-time download that fails the gate, consistent with the 118MB size finding from Stage 1.

**Conclusion — the mechanism works, the candidate does not.** Every technical question this phase exists to answer came back with real, explained evidence: the model contract is sound across Node and browser (Stage 5), the isolation story is clean (Stage 6), and `intfloat/multilingual-e5-small` is measurably too large for practical browser deployment on ordinary broadband (Stage 1 + Stage 7 agree: 118MB best case, 126s cold load). Per change.md §13's own anticipated exit, **this phase closes with an honest lexical-first recommendation for this candidate** — not a claimed quantitative win, since the 300+ human-labelled judgment set was never built (a human task, not attempted here, stated above rather than skipped silently). **One explicit limitation, stated rather than hidden: only one candidate model was empirically tested.** The reasoning that a large shared subword-vocabulary embedding table is structural to essentially any credible multilingual sentence-embedding model (not a defect specific to this one) is inference from how these models are built, not verified against a second, independently-measured candidate — a natural next step before fully closing the door on browser-side semantic matching, left for whoever picks this back up: either test a second, genuinely smaller candidate, accept the slow load behind an explicit lazy-load/consent UI (change.md's own gate list already anticipates a progress-and-cancel affordance), or proceed with the lexical/taxonomy-only path change.md names as an acceptable fallback.

Full suite 865/865, typecheck and lint clean throughout. Per-commit and whole-branch Codex review skipped per this session's standing instruction not to wait on Codex's cooldown.

## Earlier phase: Phase 4 — attachment visibility (narrowed scope)

**Phase 6 (outreach drafts) is merged (PR #17); its full record is below.** Phase 4 was chosen next (2026-09-23) by project-owner decision, and deliberately re-scoped from what the concept originally asked for — see the grounding and decision below before the concept §16/§25 amendment this section also makes.

### Stage plan (written 2026-09-23, before any code)

**Grounding, checked against the live corpus first (concept §25's own instruction for this phase: "analyze observed attachment formats and frequency"):** of 3,524 current hr.ge listings, **27 (0.77%) carry `structuredAttributes.hasAttachment: true`**; jobs.ge carries **zero** across 711 current listings and has never been observed to. Formats, from the 27 real `attachmentUrl` file extensions: mostly `.docx`/`.xlsx`/`.xls`, plus a handful of `.zip`, `.rar`, `.odt`. **Every one of those URLs is a presigned S3 link (`s3.eu-west-1.amazonaws.com`) that expires roughly 40 minutes after hr.ge generates it** (`X-Amz-Expires=2400`) — so storing the URL itself for later use is pointless, and actually fetching content would need to happen within the crawl run that discovers it, not lazily afterward.

**Decision: do not fetch, quarantine, or process attachment content at all — the concept's original Phase 4 (§16, §25) is deferred indefinitely, not built now.** Two reasons, not one: the observed volume is small enough (27 listings total, one source) that the fetch/quarantine/archive-safety machinery §16 specifies would cost far more engineering and security surface than it returns; and the accepted hosted-edition direction (§30) already leans the product toward "the person reviews the real listing and acts on it themselves" rather than Xtelo re-hosting source content. A person who wants the attachment already has, and will keep having, a link straight to the real hr.ge listing — they get it there, at the source, same as anyone visiting hr.ge directly would. This is recorded as a concept amendment in `docs/scraplify-concept.md` (§16/§25), not just a STATUS.md scope note, since it changes what the concept itself asks for.

**What this phase actually delivers: one visibility flag, nothing else.** hr.ge's `structuredAttributes.hasAttachment` is already captured by the existing detail parser (Phase 1) and already reaches `getOpportunity` — it has simply never been rendered. `web/lib/opportunity-detail.ts`'s `EXTRA_TRUE_FLAGS` list (the same "print only when true" pattern already used for `isWorkFromHome`/`isSuitableForStudent`) gets one more entry: "This listing has an attachment on hr.ge — see the original listing." No new table, no migration, no fetch, no jobs.ge change (zero observed attachments there, so nothing to show).

**Stages:**
1. Add `hasAttachment` to `EXTRA_TRUE_FLAGS` and confirm it renders on the detail screen for a listing that actually has one.
2. Unit test coverage for the new flag in `opportunity-detail.test.ts`.
3. Browser QA against the live corpus (read-only `npm run dev:web` is sufficient — nothing here writes) using several of the 27 real listings, at all four gate viewports.

**Exit gate:** the flag renders exactly for a canonical opportunity whose current live hr.ge member's structured attributes say `hasAttachment: true`, and for no other listing; nothing is fetched, stored, or linked to any URL other than the listing's own existing source link.

### Build record (2026-09-23)

One commit: `EXTRA_TRUE_FLAGS` gains an `Attachment` entry, two new tests (one confirming the flag appears only when `hasAttachment` is true, one confirming the presigned `attachmentUrl` — with its `X-Amz-Signature` — never reaches the rendered output even though it sits right next to `hasAttachment` in the same source object). Full suite 846/846, typecheck and lint clean.

**Verified live**, not just from the source: seeded a real hr.ge listing's `structuredAttributes` in the disposable `scraplify_qa` copy (a genuine listing already in the corpus, not a synthetic fixture id) and loaded its detail page. "Attachment — The board marks this listing as having an attachment — open it on the source above" renders under "Also recorded by the board," directly beside the existing link to the real hr.ge listing. No horizontal overflow at 390px, no console errors. Reverted the seed immediately after and removed the throwaway scripts used to make and undo it — `scraplify_qa` is unchanged from before this phase.

**OWED review — DISCHARGED (2026-09-23).** `codex review --commit 24cd8ef` found two real P2s, both fixed here rather than in a follow-up commit: the "Phase 4 is still not started" line and the file's own "Last updated" date, both left over from before this phase started, contradicted the new "Current phase: Phase 4" section a reader would see three lines away — fixed above; and the browser QA claim only covered 390px against `browser-qa.md`'s four-width requirement — the missing 768/1280/1920 checks were run against the same real, seeded hr.ge listing (re-seeded and re-reverted for it, `scraplify_qa` left unchanged again), all clean, no overflow, the flag's value column staying inside its own bounded width at every size.

## Earlier phase: Phase 6 — outreach drafts

**Merged as PR #17 (2026-09-23).** Its full build and review record, including the two post-merge review rounds and the explicit waiver of the whole-branch review, is unchanged below.

**Phase 7A (operations baseline) is merged (PR #16) and running for real; its record and open follow-ups are below.** Phase 6 was started 2026-09-16 while the first scheduled crawls run, by project-owner decision, scoped as **6A — drafts and exact-content approval, with no sending of any kind**.

### Phase 6A — outreach drafts, stage plan (written 2026-09-16, before any code)

**The rule this phase exists to enforce (concept §18, §25 Phase 6 exit gate):** drafting does not imply approval; an approval binds the exact recipient, subject, body, attachments, listing and version; any change after approval invalidates it; every approval and mutation is audited; **no external action is possible without a current explicit approval.** 6A builds the draft and approval half and deliberately **no send path at all** — no SMTP, no HTTP submit, no messaging channel. A later 6B adds one channel, gated on the approvals built here.

**Grounding, checked against the live corpus first:** of 3,803 active listings, 2,485 apply by `email` (with a real address), 1,221 by `url`, 67 by `form`, 30 unspecified. So a draft is an **email** (recipient taken from the listing's own application method, never typed or inferred) when the listing carries an email, and otherwise a **cover letter** with no recipient.

**Decision 1 — drafts are written by Claude, from the corrected profile claims and the listing text only.** Same model and pattern as Phase 5's extraction (`claude-opus-5`, forced tool use, fake client in tests, nothing about the profile or the draft ever logged). The tool schema returns the subject, body, and the ids of the profile claims the text relies on; ids that do not belong to the profile are rejected, and the draft screen lists the claims used, so any statement can be traced to a claim the person reviewed. The prompt forbids stating experience, skills, years or qualifications absent from the claims — the letter-writing equivalent of "never render invented data." Language defaults to the listing's own (Georgian listing → Georgian draft), switchable EN/KA. Generating sends derived CV data to Anthropic, so it carries the same required per-submission acknowledgment Phase 5's upload does (§23.2).

**Decision 2 — approval is a hash over the exact content, and invalidation is explicit AND checked.** New tables (migration reviewed by `migration-safety-reviewer`; additive only):
- `outreach_drafts` — profile id + profile version, opportunity id + opportunity revision id, source listing id, kind (`email` | `cover_letter`), recipient (null for a cover letter), subject, body, language, model/prompt version, timestamps, soft delete.
- `outreach_approvals` — draft id, `content_hash` (sha-256 over kind, recipient, subject, body, attachments — none in 6A — opportunity revision id, profile version), approved_at, invalidated_at + reason.
- `audit_events` — entity type/id, action (`draft_generated`, `draft_edited`, `draft_approved`, `approval_invalidated`, `draft_deleted`), at, and hashes/ids only — **never draft or profile text**.

An approval is **current** only if it is not invalidated, its hash equals the draft's hash recomputed now, and the opportunity revision it bound is still the opportunity's current one. Saving an edit invalidates any current approval in the same transaction; the recompute is the defense in depth if some future write path forgets.

**Stages:**
1. **Store and domain** (`src/outreach/`): schema + migration, `createDraft`/`editDraft`/`approveDraft`/`deleteDraft`/`loadDraft` with hashing, invalidation and audit in one transaction each; tests including "edit after approval invalidates", "listing revision change makes the approval stale", "approving stale content is refused", "audit carries no content".
2. **Generation** (`src/outreach/generate-draft.ts`): the Claude call, claim-id validation, recipient from the listing only; fake-client tests; no content in logs, verified by grepping real log output as in Phase 5.
3. **Web**: a "Draft application" action on `/ranked` rows → `/drafts/[id]` editor (recipient read-only, subject/body editable, claims used, Save, Approve, current-approval state) and a `/drafts` list. The only "ready to use" affordances — copy text, and for an email an "open in your mail app" `mailto:` link, which still leaves pressing send to the person — render **only while the approval is current**. Every mutation behind `assertWritesEnabled()`; browser QA against `dev:web:qa`.
4. **Review gate**: `security-review` plus an approval-workflow pass (concept §24's Phase 6 row: exact-content approval, invalidation after edits, recipient binding, audit events), and a search proving no send path exists.

**Development constraint while the scheduled crawls run:** no `npm run build` (the running wrappers call `dist/` for dedupe and taxonomy when each crawl ends). The migration is additive (new tables only), applied to `scraplify_qa` for browser QA and to the real database for the test suite.

**Exit gate (6A):**
- [x] A draft can be generated from a real profile and a real listing, edited, and approved, end to end in a browser against the disposable database.
- [x] Any edit invalidates a current approval, a changed listing revision makes it stale, and the copy/`mailto:` affordances disappear in both cases — covered by tests and seen in the browser. *(Narrower than worded: the **edit** half was seen in the browser; the **changed listing revision** half is covered by tests only, since making a revision change by hand would mean writing to the corpus purely to watch a screen.)*
- [x] Every generate/edit/approve/invalidate/delete writes an audit event with no draft or profile text in it; no draft or profile text in application logs on a real generation.
- [x] No send, submit or messaging code exists anywhere in the repo (verified by search, recorded here).
- [x] Per-commit review clean or recorded OWED; whole-branch review run or explicitly waived. **Both waived by explicit project-owner decision (2026-09-23) — see the merge record below.**

### Phase 6A build record — Stage 3 (web) and the browser QA gate (2026-09-23)

**Migration 0022 had been generated and committed but never applied to either database.** Found by running the suite, not by reading: 270 of 838 tests failed on `relation "outreach_drafts" does not exist`, since the test-support cleanup helper touches the table for every test that creates a listing. Applied to `scraplify` and to `scraplify_qa` — additive only (3 enum types, 3 tables, FKs, indexes; no drop, no alter of an existing table, no `NOT NULL` added to existing data). Suite then **838/838 green**, typecheck and `biome lint` clean.

**End-to-end browser pass against `scraplify_qa` (`npm run dev:web:qa`, writes enabled, the live corpus untouched):** Ranked row → `/drafts/new` → real `claude-opus-5` generation (20.6s, Georgian, 6 claims cited) → approve → edit → re-approve. Specifically verified rather than assumed:

- **The consent gate refuses before spending anything.** Submitting without the §23.2 acknowledgment redirected back with the refusal and wrote **zero** rows — checked in the database, not inferred from the screen.
- **The approval boundary holds.** Copy and `mailto:` render only while the approval is current; saving an edit withdrew the approval and **both affordances disappeared in the same render**. The `mailto:` encodes every part with CRLF line breaks per RFC 6068.
- **The draft does not invent qualifications.** For an English-teaching vacancy the model wrote, in Georgian, that the candidate has no teaching experience, no pedagogical education and no CELTA/TEFL certification, and offered only what the claims support — the letter-writing form of "never render invented data." Every cited claim traced to a real CV quote on screen.
- **Audit and logs carry no content.** The four audit rows for this run hold only `contentHash`/`previousHash`/`approvalId`/`kind`/`claimCount`. The real generation logged `kind, language, claimCount, durationMs, servedBy` and nothing else: grepping the server log for the draft's Georgian text, the profile's claim text and the recipient address returned **0 matches each**.
- **No send path.** No mail or messaging dependency exists in `package.json` at all; `rg` over `src/`, `web/` and `scripts/` for SMTP/SES/SendGrid/Mailgun/Postmark/Twilio/Slack/webhook returned nothing; `src/outreach/` and `web/app/drafts/` contain no `fetch`/HTTP call of any kind; and the repo's only outbound-capable module, `src/net/http-fetcher.ts`, is imported solely by the two crawl adapters and their CLIs.
- **Viewports and keyboard.** No page-level horizontal overflow at 390/768/1280/1920 on any of the three screens. Tab order sensible with a visible 2px focus ring on every control; the delete confirmation opens, closes on `Escape` and on Cancel, and returns focus to its trigger (an `popover="auto"`, matching the pattern `/profile/[id]` already uses — not focus-trapped, which is that primitive's normal behaviour).
- **Nav breakpoints measured, not estimated.** With "Drafts" as the 9th link the desktop bar appears at exactly 1480px in English (hidden at 1479) and 1720px in Georgian (hidden at 1719), with no header overflow at any width checked.

**Two defects found by the gate and fixed, not deferred:**
1. `/drafts/new`'s error notice rendered without `role="alert"` — it had been copied from `/profile`'s older version, while the two other draft screens already used it. Since these messages arrive *after a redirect*, a refusal was landing silently for a screen reader. Now consistent across all three draft screens.
2. The editor had **no unsaved-changes warning**. The body is text a person has read through and reworded, the textarea is its only copy, and "All drafts" and the nav are plain `<a href>`s — so a stray click was a real document unload and real lost work. Added `web/components/unsaved-changes-guard.tsx`; verified in the browser that a clean form does not prompt, a dirty one cancels the unload, and reverting the edit clears it again. Saving is unaffected, since a server action redirects client-side rather than unloading.

**Noted, deliberately not changed (pre-existing, outside this branch):** `<html lang>` stays `"en"` while the nav renders Georgian labels. That is the documented outcome of a 2026-09-16 Codex review — the root layout cannot know the current route, so reading the locale cookie there would mislabel every still-English screen. The narrow fix would be `lang="ka"` on the nav element itself; recorded here rather than done, since the nav predates this phase.

**Per-commit Codex gate, round 1: one P1 and three P2s, all fixed.**

- **[P1] The exact-content approval invariant had a real hole (client-only fix, later found insufficient — see round 2).** Save and Approve were separate forms; without a check, editing the body and pressing Approve *without saving first* approved the last-saved text while different, unsaved text sat visible above it — and once approved, the same gap let Copy and the mailto link keep serving the old approved text while unsaved edits were on screen. First fix: `web/components/draft-approval-guard.tsx`, a client component watching the edit form's dirty state that disables Approve and replaces Copy/mailto with a plain notice the instant a keystroke makes the visible text diverge from what's saved.
- **[P2] Generation wasn't bound to what the confirmation screen showed.** `/drafts/new` renders a specific profile version and listing revision, but the submit only carried ids — `generateDraft` reloaded whatever was current, so a profile edit or a crawl landing in the gap between rendering and pressing "Write draft" could send different claims or listing text to Claude than the person had just read and agreed to, undermining the §23.2 acknowledgment. Fixed: the confirmation screen now echoes its own profile version, opportunity revision and listing revision through hidden fields; `generateDraft` takes them as `expected` and refuses (`CONFIRMATION_STALE`) before the billed call if any moved. Covered by a new test asserting the model is never reached on any of the three mismatches, and is reached exactly once when they match.
- **[P2] `listDrafts` read every draft's full body and subject just to show a list.** `approvalState` needs the full row to recompute its hash — that's Stage 1's deliberate "never trust a stored flag" design, not something to weaken — but a draft's first line of `approvalState` shows it can only ever be `{status: 'none'}` when there's no live approval row at all, which never needs the hash. Rewritten as two queries: a summary query with a left join for whether a live approval exists, then a second targeted query for full rows only for the drafts that have one — so a draft with no live approval (typically most of them) never has its up-to-20,000-character body read into memory at all. Covered by a new test listing one approved and one unapproved draft together.
- **[P2] The three `/drafts*` routes had no `loading.tsx`/`error.tsx`.** Added one pair at `web/app/drafts/` — the App Router's normal boundary inheritance means a segment's own loading/error state covers every route nested under it that doesn't define its own, so this one pair (matching `/saved`'s existing pattern: a sentence, not a skeleton, since a skeleton resolving to "No drafts yet" asserts a list that was never coming) covers `/drafts`, `/drafts/new` and `/drafts/[id]`.

**Per-commit Codex gate, round 2: one P1 and one P2, both fixed.**

- **[P1] Round 1's fix was client-only, so it failed exactly when JavaScript is off or hasn't hydrated — which is also when the exact-content guarantee matters most, since there is no other line of defense.** Without JS, `dirty` in `draft-approval-guard.tsx` never becomes true, so Approve stays enabled after an unsaved edit; and because Save and Approve were still two separate `<form>`s, the Approve submission physically could not carry the edit form's current textarea value even if it wanted to — a browser only ever posts one form's own fields. **Real fix: Save and Approve now share one `<form id="draft-edit-form">`.** Approve is a `formAction={approveDraftAction}` override on that same form, so pressing it — with or without JavaScript — submits the current subject and body alongside the draft id, exactly as Save does. `approveDraft` now compares that submitted subject/body byte-for-byte against what is actually stored and refuses (`UNSAVED_EDITS`) on any mismatch, *before* even checking the content hash. This is unconditional: it does not depend on the client guard, which remains only as a cosmetic, instant-feedback layer on top. `web/components/submit-button.tsx` gained a `formAction` prop and an action-identity check (`useFormStatus().action === formAction`) so Save and Approve show their own correct pending label rather than both reacting to either button's submission. Verified: a new store-level test submits mismatched visible content directly and asserts `UNSAVED_EDITS`, independent of the browser; the merged form was also re-verified end to end in a real browser (dirty→disabled, save, approve, dirty-while-approved→hidden) plus a four-viewport check that the `display: contents` wrapper introduces no overflow.
- **[P2] Saving an untouched email whose generated subject was blank silently invalidated a current approval.** `writeDraft` stores an empty subject as `null`, but the subject `<input>` renders `''` for that case, and an untouched save posted `subject: ''` back — `'' !== null` in the hash's JSON array, so a no-op save withdrew a live approval. Fixed in `web/lib/draft-input.ts`: a blank submitted subject now normalizes to `null`, matching how a blank generated one is already stored. Covered by two new cases in `draft-input.test.ts`.

**Per-commit Codex gate, round 3: one P1 and two P2s, all fixed.**

- **[P1] The `mailto:` link itself was still unsafe without JavaScript, even after round 2's server-side fix.** `mailto:` is a plain, static anchor `href` computed once at render time from the STORED draft — there is no form submission and no server round trip to check it against anything, unlike Approve. The client guard defaulted to `dirty = false` on first render (matching server-rendered output, since the server cannot know client-side state), so the link sat live in the initial HTML and, with JavaScript off or not yet hydrated, stayed live and unchanging regardless of what was typed into the textarea. **Fixed by flipping the default: `draft-approval-guard.tsx`'s dirty check now starts at a third state, `'unknown'`, treated the same as `'dirty'` by both consumers — Copy and the mailto link render only once JavaScript has actively confirmed `'clean'`, never before.** With no JavaScript, or before hydration completes, they simply don't appear — a real cost to convenience, but the approved text stays fully visible and selectable in the page regardless, so nothing is actually blocked, only the one-click affordance. Verified in the browser both ways: on a hard reload of an approved draft, Copy/mailto are absent in the pre-hydration snapshot and present within seconds afterward; the existing dirty/clean transitions still work exactly as before once hydrated.
- **[P2] A generation could produce a body or subject longer than the editor's own limits, permanently locking the draft — unsavable and unapprovable.** `max_tokens: 16000` can exceed `MAX_BODY_CHARS`/`MAX_SUBJECT_CHARS` (20,000 / 300), which every Save and, since round 2, every Approve now enforces via the shared form. A draft that arrived over that bound could be viewed but never saved (even unchanged) or approved. Fixed at the source of truth: the two limits moved from `web/lib/draft-input.ts` into `draft-store.ts` as `MAX_DRAFT_BODY_CHARS`/`MAX_DRAFT_SUBJECT_CHARS` — one constant each, not two that could drift — and are now checked twice: in `writeDraft`, refusing an oversized model response before it is ever stored (a wasted billed call, but a truncated letter mid-sentence would be worse), and in `createDraft` itself as the real write-boundary backstop, the same "never trust a single check" reasoning this file already applies to the approval hash. Covered by new tests in both `generate-draft.test.ts` (refusal over each bound; a long cover-letter subject is *not* refused, since it's dropped to `null` regardless of length) and exercised transitively through the existing store tests.
- **[P2] The draft screens showed a title that could drift from what the draft was actually written about.** `loadDraftContext` and `listDrafts` both read `opportunities.canonicalTitle` — the LIVE, mutable title that moves every time the opportunity is re-resolved (a later crawl, a merge) — rather than `opportunityRevisions.canonicalTitle` for the specific revision `draft.opportunityRevisionId` pins. A draft could end up displayed as "For: <new title>" while its actual generated text answered the old one, quietly breaking the concept's exact-version requirement. Fixed: both functions now join through `opportunityRevisions` on the draft's own pinned revision id. Covered by a new test that re-resolves the opportunity to a different title (updating both the revision AND the live `opportunities` row, matching what a real re-resolution does) and asserts both read paths still return the original.

**OWED review of the round-3-fixes commit — DISCHARGED (2026-09-23), and a narrower one opened in its place.** `codex review --commit f089150` ran once Codex's cooldown cleared, and found real defects the local gate had not: **[P1]** Copy and the mail-app link checked only local form-dirty state, never whether the approval was still current server-side, so another tab saving, a profile revision, or a crawl advancing the listing could leave both usable against an approval that had already stopped being current — nothing before this reviewed that gap, because nothing before this asked the question. Plus four P2s: `saveDraftAction` had no optimistic-concurrency check, so two tabs open on one draft let a stale save silently overwrite a newer one; the read-only profile passed a form id that never exists there to the dirty-state hook, which stuck on `'unknown'` forever and permanently hid Copy/mailto; the `approved=1` banner was tied to the raw query param rather than computed approval state, so it could go on reading "Approved." after a later edit made that false; and `mailtoHref` had no length bound, so a long or Georgian-heavy body could produce a `mailto:` URI past what browsers, OS handlers and mail clients reliably accept.

All five fixed: a new `checkApprovalCurrentAction` server action plus a `useLiveApprovalState` hook that revalidates on reveal and again immediately before Copy/mailto actually fire; `editDraft` now takes `expectedContentHash` and rejects a stale save with `CONTENT_CHANGED`; `editFormId` is `string | null` so read-only mode reports `'clean'` immediately instead of hanging; the banner now checks `approval.status === 'current'`; `mailtoHref` returns `string | null` past a conservative 1,800-character bound, with the screen falling back to Copy and an explanatory note.

**A second review, `codex review --uncommitted` on that fix itself, found three more real issues — and two unrelated ones, from files another concurrent session had sitting uncommitted in this same shared working tree** (`.claude/skills/refresh-corpus/SKILL.md`, `.claude/agents/adapter-drift-checker.md` — real findings, but not in this diff and not this branch's to fix; `--uncommitted` reviews the whole working tree, not just what is staged, per `AGENTS.md`). The three real ones, all fixed: **[P1]** `navigator.clipboard.writeText` was called *after* awaiting the server recheck, which can outlast the browser's transient-activation window for the Clipboard API on a slow connection, failing every Copy attempt even with a valid approval — fixed by copying first, within the same click, and letting a failed recheck only downgrade the confirmation message afterward, since clipboard content never leaves the person's own machine and so has nothing to fail closed against. **[P2]** the mount-time revalidation effect had no rejection handler, so a transient failure (a network blip, the server briefly unreachable) left Copy/mailto hidden indefinitely with no way back short of a reload — it now has its own `'error'` state, distinct from `'stale'`, with a "Try again" action. **[P2]** `saveDraftAction`'s new stale-content refusal discarded the rejecting tab's just-typed text on redirect — a real regression introduced by the fix above, not present before it — so `DraftConflictRecovery` now stashes the edit form's contents in `sessionStorage` around every submit and restores it into the form specifically on that conflict, verified end to end below.

**Verified in a real browser against `scraplify_qa`, all three fresh fixes together, not assumed from the code:** two tabs open on the same draft — tab B saves an edit, tab A (still showing the original text) edits and saves against its now-stale hash, gets refused with `CONTENT_CHANGED`, and **the exact text tab A had just typed reappears in the form** with a note explaining why, while tab B's save is confirmed untouched in the database. Copy and "open in your mail app" both fire correctly for a genuinely current approval (mailto confirmed via the browser's own "Launched external handler for 'mailto:…'" log line, address/subject/body all correctly encoded). A long Georgian draft (well past the 1,800-character bound) shows the suppression note and no mailto link, with Copy still working. No horizontal overflow at 390px. Typecheck, `biome lint`, and the full suite (845/845) all green.

**OWED review of the fix-of-the-fix commit — WAIVED at merge, not discharged.** The pre-commit hook hit the same usage-limit cooldown a third time this session (recorded until 2026-09-23 15:29), so the commit landed without a per-commit review of its own final form — only of two earlier, less-complete versions of it (see the two reviews above). What was unreviewed was specifically the three-fix response (clipboard-activation ordering, the error/retry state, and `DraftConflictRecovery`), not the whole feature, and every claim in the verification paragraph above was checked directly (database rows, console log, viewport measurement), not inferred from the UI alone.

**Merged as PR #17 (2026-09-23), by explicit project-owner decision to skip the whole-branch adversarial review this file's own merge convention (root `CLAUDE.md`) otherwise requires, and to not wait out Codex's cooldown for the one still-owed per-commit review above either.** Different in kind from every other waiver on this project so far: those were forced by a Codex outage mid-review with the *per-commit* gate having already run cleanly on everything else; this is a deliberate choice to merge with the branch's last commit carrying real, structural changes (the stale-save race and its recovery path) that no whole-branch pass has ever looked at cross-commit, on top of one still-outstanding per-commit debt. Recorded here in full rather than folded into a checked exit-gate box with no comment, because the risk this waives is real: the whole-branch review is exactly the pass that found four P1s invisible to per-commit review and passing tests during Phase 3C (see that section below), and nothing equivalent has run here. Mitigating, not eliminating: every commit up to and including `f089150` (the drafting/approval feature itself) WAS reviewed, twice over in the approval-guard's case; the full suite is 845/845; and the two most recently fixed rounds were both verified live in a real browser against `scraplify_qa`, not self-assessed from reading the diff.

## Earlier phase: Phase 7 — operations

**Phase 5 (CV parsing) and the profile hub are both merged (PR #14, PR #15); their records below are unchanged.** Phase 7 was chosen next (2026-09-16) over Phases 4 and 6 because the 2026-09-15 incident showed the corpus can silently go stale for days, and nothing ran unattended at all. Scoped as **Phase 7A — operations baseline**; see its stage plan below.

### Phase 7A — operations baseline, stage plan (written 2026-09-16, before any code)

**State this was planned against, all checked live rather than assumed:** no scheduled task registered on this machine (`Get-ScheduledTask`); last crawl of any kind was hr.ge's full-coverage run on 2026-09-15 (3,354 discovered, 12 failed); jobs.ge last crawled 2026-09-06 and has never had a full-coverage run; only jobs.ge has a scheduler wrapper, and nothing ever invokes it; `runDedupe` has no concurrency lock of any kind; the corpus has no backup.

**Out of scope, deliberately (concept §25 Phase 7 bullets carried to a later 7B):** `pg-boss` (no heterogeneous durable work exists yet), sandboxed parser-repair proposals and canaries (§22), hosting/scaling reassessment — all three are explicitly "from measured evidence," and that evidence starts accumulating only once 7A's schedules run.
