Registering the scheduled tasks is **also not done by this phase's code** — it starts real, unattended, recurring requests against both live sites, so it stays a deliberate step the project owner runs.

**Stage 7-1 — unattended pipeline.**
- `runDedupe` serialized by a Postgres session advisory lock (acquired on a dedicated pooled connection, blocking wait, not skip — a dedupe after a crawl should run late rather than never). Without it, two scheduled sources finishing close together run two `--auto-link` passes concurrently, which can canonicalize the same listing twice.
- The jobs.ge-only wrapper/registration pair generalized to `scripts/run-crawl.ps1 -Source jobs-ge|hr-ge` and `scripts/register-crawl-schedule.ps1 -Source …`, both chaining `run-dedupe --auto-link` after every crawl attempt with its failure folded into the exit code. The old jobs.ge-specific scripts are removed (verified never registered). README updated.

**Stage 7-2 — silence becomes visible.**
- A pure `assessSourceHealth` producing typed alerts per source: never crawled; last run older than 48h (2× the default 24h cadence); last run not `completed`; no completed full-coverage run in 7 days; unresolved incidents. Plus corpus-level alerts: active listings with no live opportunity membership (the exact 2026-09-15 incident signature) and the review backlog.
- Surfaced on `/health` and in `npm run browse health`; a new `npm run health:check` exits non-zero on any critical alert, so a scheduler or a person gets a real failure signal, not just a page to remember to open. No push channel yet (concept §27 leaves the notification channel open).

**Stage 7-3 — anomalous runs leave a durable record.** Most of §21.3 already exists (floor, relative-baseline, totalCount, VIP/standard partition, quarantine-rate and fetch-failure-rate guards all downgrade a run to `partial`, which already excludes it from closure). The real gaps:
- A guard failure downgrades the run but records **no** `parser_incidents` row — it is visible only in a log file, so `/health`'s incident count stays 0 through a real collapse. Fixed: every failed whole-run guard records a typed incident with its measured evidence.
- No surge detection. Added as a non-blocking `count_surge` incident (a surge cannot drive closure, so it does not downgrade the run).
- No per-run cap on closure beyond the 3-miss streak. Added in `closeMissingListingsInTransaction`: a batch that would close more than max(25, 10%) of a source's open listings closes nothing, keeps streaks advancing, and records a critical `mass_closure_suspected` incident; an explicit `--allow-mass-closure` crawl flag is the reviewed override.

**Stage 7-4 — backup and a practiced restore.** `scripts/backup-db.ps1` (`pg_dump -Fc` via the Compose container into gitignored `backups/`, with retention), and one real restore drill into a throwaway database, verified by row counts against the live corpus — recorded here with its numbers.

**Exit gate (7A)** — as written before any code; results in the build record below, which states where an item was met more narrowly than worded:
- [x] Both sources can be scheduled by one registration script each, and every scheduled run chains a locked dedupe pass whose failure fails the run.
- [x] A stale source, a stale full-coverage run, and unlinked active listings each produce a visible alert on `/health` and a non-zero `health:check`, verified against real data. *(Narrower than worded — see below: stale full coverage is a warning, so it does not by itself fail `health:check`; unlinked listings are verified at DB-test level only, since the live corpus has none.)*
- [x] A failed whole-run guard, a surge, and an oversized closure batch each produce a durable incident, covered by tests.
- [x] A backup has been taken and actually restored, with row counts matched.
- [x] Per-commit review clean or recorded OWED (Codex cooldown until 2026-09-20 10:31); whole-branch review run or explicitly waived. *(Per-commit: OWED. Whole-branch Codex review: **WAIVED** by explicit project-owner decision, 2026-09-16 — see below.)*

### Phase 7A — built (2026-09-16), `phase-7-ops`

Nine code/doc commits plus review fixes, each on a green suite — finally **811/811 tests**, `npm run typecheck` and `npm run lint` clean.

**7-1 — unattended pipeline.**
- `src/dedupe/dedupe-lock.ts`: `withDedupeLock` holds a session advisory lock on one dedicated connection for the whole pass, waits up to 30 min (`lock_timeout`) rather than skipping, and destroys the connection if unlocking fails. `run-dedupe` uses it. Four tests on real Postgres (held/released, released on throw, two concurrent passes serialize, typed timeout); mutation-checked — replacing the lock call with a no-op failed 3 of 4.
- `scripts/run-crawl.ps1 -Source` and `scripts/register-crawl-schedule.ps1 -Source` replace the jobs.ge-only pair (never registered — `Get-ScheduledTask` showed no task). Verified with a stub standing in for `node` (no live crawl): crawl exit 0 + dedupe exit 7 made the wrapper exit 7; an invalid `-Source` is rejected.
- **Found and fixed on the way: the old wrapper's logs were unreadable for Georgian.** `*>>` appended UTF-16 beside `Add-Content`'s ANSI headers, and native output was decoded with the OEM code page. The new wrapper writes UTF-8 throughout; verified with a stub emitting `ბუღალტერი` on stdout and a line on stderr — both logged intact, and the stderr line no longer risks aborting the wrapper under `ErrorActionPreference = 'Stop'`.
- **Not done, deliberately:** neither task is registered. That starts real unattended requests against both sites and stays the project owner's step: `./scripts/register-crawl-schedule.ps1 -Source jobs-ge` and `-Source hr-ge`.

**7-2 — silence becomes visible.**
- `src/browse/source-health.ts` (`assessSourceHealth`, pure, 11 tests): critical for never crawled, no crawl in 48h, failed last run, active listings unlinked for over 12h (the grace is 12h, not the 6h first drafted — a full jobs.ge crawl runs ~8-9h before its dedupe, so 6h would have raised false criticals mid-run), and any unresolved critical incident; warning for a partial/quarantined last run, a run "running" over 24h, no completed full-coverage run in 7 days, and other open incidents.
- `getSourceHealth` gained `unlinkedActiveListings`, `staleUnlinkedActiveListings` and `unresolvedCriticalIncidents`; a DB test covers linked, superseded-only, stale, fresh and non-active listings.
- `npm run health:check` exits 1 on any critical alert; `npm run browse health` prints the alerts. **Live against the real corpus:** `hr-ge: ok`; `jobs-ge` critical `run_overdue` (last crawl 2026-09-06, 10 days) and warning `full_coverage_stale` (never); exit 1. That is the true state, and it stays critical until jobs.ge is actually crawled.
- `/health` states each source's alerts above its figures (level carried by a word and a shape as well as colour), a header summary, and a "not yet browsable" field. Browser-QA'd read-only against `dev:web` at 390/768/1280/1920 — no horizontal overflow at any width, alert label stacks above its message at 390. `web-design-guidelines` pass found three issues in alert copy, all fixed: counts not `Intl`-formatted, straight quotes, a literal-backtick command. **Not browser-verified:** the all-healthy header and a non-zero "not yet browsable" field — neither state exists in the real corpus today; both are covered by unit/DB tests only.

**7-3 — anomalous runs leave a durable record.**
- `src/adapters/run-anomalies.ts`, called from both adapters **only for a finished full walk** (not incremental, not stopped by a block/backoff, and for hr.ge not resumed mid-index — those `partial` runs are routine and already surface as a degraded last run). A failed guard records a critical `count_collapse` (any count guard) or `other` incident with the failed guard names and measured figures; a count above 2x the last completed full-coverage baseline (baseline >= 100) records a non-blocking `count_surge` warning. An unresolved run-level incident of the same kind suppresses repeats until someone resolves it (mutation-checked). The existing jobs.ge relative-collapse test now also asserts the incident.
- `closeMissingListingsInTransaction`: a pass may close at most max(25, 10% of open listings). Over that it closes nothing, advances the streaks (listings stay `missing_suspected`), and opens one critical `mass_closure_suspected` incident. `--allow-mass-closure` on either crawl CLI is the reviewed override. Four DB tests (at cap closes, over cap holds + incident, cap scales with source size, override closes); mutation-checked.

**7-4 — backup and a practiced restore.**
- `scripts/backup-db.ps1`: `pg_dump -Fc` written inside the container (never piped through PowerShell, which would corrupt binary), validated with `pg_restore --list`, copied to gitignored `backups/`, newest 14 kept.
- `scripts/restore-db-drill.ps1`: restores into `<db>_restore_drill`, compares exact per-table row counts with the source, drops the drill database, exits 1 on any mismatch.
- **Drilled for real, 2026-09-16:** 10.1 MB backup of `scraplify`, restored in 2s, **all 23 public tables matched** (e.g. `source_listings` 3,705, `opportunities` 3,671, `source_listing_revisions` 7,076, `fetch_attempts` 8,397). Negative check: the same backup drilled against `scraplify_qa` exited 1 with mismatches reported. `pg_database` afterwards lists no leftover drill database. Not yet scheduled — run it by hand, or register it the same way as the crawls, when wanted.

- **P1 — `withDedupeLock` leaked `lock_timeout` into the pool.** `reset lock_timeout` ran only after the lock was acquired, so a timed-out wait handed its connection back still carrying the setting (the reviewer confirmed it live: `300ms` on the released connection). Harmless in the one-shot CLI, which ends the pool; real in the shared test pool and any longer-lived caller. Now reset on every path, or the session destroyed. Now gated only on "full, not stopped, (hr.ge) not resumed", with `discoveryComplete` as a guard. New hr.ge tests: a redesigned page 1 records a critical `count_collapse` naming `discoveryComplete`; a 429-stopped run records none; the existing totalCount-shortfall test now asserts its incident. Mutation-checked (restoring the `complete` gate fails the redesign test). Consequence stated plainly: a transient non-retry discovery failure (not a block/backoff) now also opens an incident, once, until resolved — accepted as the cost of not missing a real one.
- **P2 — the mass-closure cap decided on a count, then updated by predicate.** Unreachable while the crawl exclusivity lock holds, but the cap should not depend on it: candidates are now locked `FOR UPDATE` and updated by id. Not reproduced as a race test; covered only by the existing cap tests still passing.

**Merged as PR #16 (2026-09-16), CI green. Schedules registered the same day, by explicit project-owner decision.** The first real run of `register-crawl-schedule.ps1` exposed two bugs it had carried over, unrun, from the old jobs.ge script: `-RepetitionDuration ([TimeSpan]::MaxValue)` serializes to a duration Windows 11 Task Scheduler rejects, and `Register-ScheduledTask` reported that as a non-terminating error, so the script printed "registered" for **both** tasks while `Get-ScheduledTask` showed none. Fixed on `main`: the duration is omitted (which repeats indefinitely), the call uses `-ErrorAction Stop`, and the script now reads the task back and verifies its repetition interval before claiming success. Re-registered and confirmed from Task Scheduler itself: both tasks repeat every day (`P1D`) with no end date, run `scripts/run-crawl.ps1` with `C:Program Files
odejs
ode.exe` (the machine-wide install, not an fnm per-shell path), and both first runs started at 20:10 local. `crawl_runs` shows a `running` full-coverage row for each, with fetches accumulating. Their results (and whether jobs.ge's `run_overdue` critical clears) should be checked with `npm run health:check` once they finish, roughly 4h for hr.ge and 8-9h for jobs.ge.

### Phase 7A follow-ups (2026-09-16, while the first scheduled crawls run)

- **Daily backup scheduled.** `scripts/register-backup-schedule.ps1` registers `Scraplify - database backup` daily at 13:00 (`-StartWhenAvailable` catches up after a missed time), reading the task back before reporting success. `backup-db.ps1 -LogToFile` now keeps a transcript in `logs/backup-db-<date>.log`; verified with a real run (10.2 MB, taken while both crawls were running — `pg_dump` reads one consistent snapshot, so that is safe).
- **Log retention.** `run-crawl.ps1` deletes `logs/*.log` older than 30 days, best effort, never failing the crawl. Verified on the snippet itself with a 40-day-old and a 10-day-old test file: only the old one was removed, real logs untouched. (Not verified through the wrapper, deliberately: running it with the stub would have written fake lines into the live crawls' own logs.)
- **Found: nothing classifies newly crawled listings.** `classifyListings` runs only from `npm run taxonomy:backfill`, so every hr.ge listing the new schedules add would stay uncategorized — the same shape as the 2026-09-15 dedupe incident. Fixed on `phase-7-taxonomy-automation`: `run-crawl.ps1` now runs `backfill-taxonomy` after dedupe on every crawl, its failure folded into the exit code (verified in a throwaway worktree with a stub `node`: crawl 0, dedupe 0, backfill 5 made the wrapper exit 5). The backfill holds its own advisory lock: the dedupe lock became a shared `withAdvisoryLock` (`src/db/advisory-lock.ts`) with every key in one registry, and a test proves the two locks do not wait on each other.
- **Found: the real-data guard cannot pass while a real crawl runs.** It fingerprints jobs.ge/hr.ge rows before and after the suite, and a scheduled crawl changes them — so from now on any test run overlapping a crawl (up to ~9h a day for jobs.ge) reports "TEST SUITE MODIFIED REAL SOURCE DATA". Not loosened, since it cannot tell a crawl's writes from a test's; instead it now checks for unsettled real crawl runs and adds a **LIKELY CAUSE** line naming them. Seen for real: the full suite (813/813) run during the first scheduled crawls tripped it, with the new line naming `hr-ge, jobs-ge`. **OWED: a full `npm test` after both crawls settle**, for a conclusive guard result on this branch. **After merging, the operational steps this phase deliberately leaves to the project owner:** register both schedules (`./scripts/register-crawl-schedule.ps1 -Source jobs-ge` / `-Source hr-ge`), after which `npm run health:check` should clear jobs.ge's `run_overdue` critical within a day; and decide whether to schedule `scripts/backup-db.ps1`.

**This heading previously read "Phase 3C — duplicate review and taxonomy" well after 3C (and 3D, and 3E) had actually merged** — the sections below for all three are real and stayed accurate, but the heading itself had drifted, and `.githooks/pre-commit` derives its suggested `git checkout -b` name from this exact line, so a stale heading was actively misleading that mechanism rather than just cosmetic. Corrected here, when starting Phase 5 gave a concrete reason to touch it. (The heading is kept short on purpose, same reason as before: trailing status in parentheses would end up in the branch name.)

**Phase 3C: both sub-phases delivered and merged into `main` (2026-09-15): `phase-3c1-duplicate-review` (PR #9) and `phase-3c2-taxonomy` (PR #10).**

**Stage 1's commit gate found five P1s and a P2, every one of them real, none of them theoretical** — the pattern this whole branch keeps producing held on the very first commit of a fresh sub-phase. All fixed, all tested, mutation-checked where the fix was a guard.

- **P1 — the fan-out guard only protected the MOVING side of a merge, not the survivor.** Written for the case a listing sits in two pending pairs and gets accepted twice (real, live: one jobs.ge listing scores against three hr.ge announcements posted four seconds apart). The first version checked whether the listing's OWN membership row said `decidedBy: 'human'` — but only the moving side of an accept gets a freshly written row; the SURVIVING side's row is untouched and can still read `ruleset` even after anchoring a human decision. So a listing that survived one merge, then became the moving side of a second, unrelated pair, read as unprotected. Rebuilt on cluster SIZE instead of who decided: a listing may not leave an opportunity that currently has more than one live member, full stop, regardless of `decidedBy`. Proven both directions — the original fan-out scenario, and the new survivor-becomes-mover one, each with its own mutation-checked test.
- **P1 — rejecting a stale-link pair recorded a verdict the corpus went on contradicting.** `run-dedupe.ts` re-queues a candidate as `needs_review` when an EXISTING automatic merge's evidence evaporates — both sides already share a live opportunity in that case. Rejecting through the plain `resolveDuplicateCandidate` only recorded "distinct" and touched no membership, so the screen would say the listings are different while the opportunity page kept showing them merged. `rejectDuplicateCandidate` is new: it detects the stale-link shape and splits the listing out atomically with the verdict, in the same transaction, reusing `splitListingIntoNewOpportunity`'s body (extracted the same way `reassignListingWithin` already was, for the same reason — a crash between the two halves must not happen).
- **P1 — the same race `acceptDuplicateCandidate` was already guarded against was open on reject.** Fixed by giving `rejectDuplicateCandidate` its own lock-and-check on `needs_review`, mirroring accept's. **Not** fixed by adding the same check to the shared `resolveDuplicateCandidate` itself — that was the first attempt, and it broke a real, legitimate caller: `run-dedupe.test.ts` records `distinct` on a candidate that already reads `confirmed_same`, a human correcting an auto-link's verdict after a manual detach, which starts from `confirmed_same` on purpose. `resolveDuplicateCandidate` stays general-purpose; the race guard belongs at the two verbs that actually need it.
- **A second self-correction the same round: `status` is not the field that means "awaiting a human."** The first fix checked `status !== 'pending'`, on the strength of a test fixture that sets `status: 'pending'`. Checked against `run-dedupe.ts`'s real write path before trusting it: it stamps **every** scored candidate `status: 'evaluated'`, including a fresh `needs_review` proposal — confirmed against the live table, which has zero rows reading `pending`. `resultingDecision === 'needs_review'` is the actual signal, and the one `acceptDuplicateCandidate` already used.
- **P1 — a duplicate suggestion with no evidence still let a reviewer decide.** `AGENTS.md` classes this P1 outright ("a duplicate suggestion shown without its evidence"). The evidence section already hid itself for an empty `reasons` array; the accept/reject buttons did not. Now gated together: no evidence, no decision — a message naming the two ways to settle the pair with direct access instead (`npm run dedupe`, `npm run browse`).
- **P1 — an expected conflict surfaced as "Postgres may be down."** The new fan-out guard's refusal (and a stale-decision race) threw, which Next routed to the nearest `error.tsx` — a boundary whose message is honest for a real infrastructure failure and actively misleading for a business-rule refusal. Fixed with the documented pattern for a Server Action to hand back an expected outcome without client JavaScript: caught, then `redirect()`'d to `/review?conflict=<message>` — confirmed against the Next docs first (a redirect inside a Server Action throws `NEXT_REDIRECT`, which Next itself intercepts, not a normal error) rather than assumed. `error.tsx` still covers everything actually unexpected.
- **P2 — a merge made on the review screen had no way back except direct database access**, which is exactly what Phase 3's exit gate exists to rule out. `detachListing` already existed and was already tested (Phase 2C); what was missing was a web route to reach it. Added to the opportunity detail screen, next to the grouping decision it undoes — the one screen with enough context to judge whether a merge was wrong.

**One thing the review could not check and this environment could not supply: the write path's actual click-through.** `.env.qa` does not exist in this environment, and creating it — a file this project has been careful about precisely because it gates writes against the live corpus — is outside what should happen without being asked. Every mutation is tested at the database level (`acceptDuplicateCandidate`, `rejectDuplicateCandidate`, the guards, the split) and the screen is verified in the browser against live data at all four required widths, but accept/reject has never been clicked in a running instance. Recorded as open rather than assumed clean.

**A second commit gate round found five more P1s and three P2s — all in the fixes from the first round.** The pattern this whole branch keeps producing held on its own review pass: fixing a real defect can introduce another one, and the second pass is what catches it.

- **P1 — the fan-out guard protected the moving listing but not a survivor that later became a mover itself.** After one accept, the SURVIVING side's own membership row is untouched and can still read `decidedBy: 'ruleset'` even though it now anchors a human-confirmed cluster. A second, unrelated accept that later tries to move THAT listing read as unprotected, because the guard only ever checked the moving side's own row. No further guard change was needed — the existing cluster-size check already covers it once the survivor's actual membership state is read correctly; what was missing was realizing the check needs no `decidedBy` branch at all, since cluster size already implies it.
- **P1 — a test leaked seven orphan opportunities into the live corpus.** The new stale-link-split test never registered `result.splitOpportunityId` with the suite's own cleanup tracking, so `afterEach` deleted the membership but never the opportunity row itself — confirmed for real: seven sourceless `Split out on rejection` opportunities were already sitting in the live database from earlier runs of this exact test, exactly the class of fabricated-data risk the test-debris sweep spent five rounds closing three days ago. Fixed in the test; the seven leaked rows were deleted after confirming zero references from anything (memberships, revisions, rankings, decisions).
- **P1 — undoing a merge left the candidate claiming a decision the corpus no longer supported.** `detachFromOpportunity`'s call to the bare `detachListing` removed the membership but left `duplicate_candidates` reading `confirmed_same, decidedBy: human` — and `run-dedupe.ts`'s upsert *refuses* to overwrite a human verdict, so the stale claim would not merely risk a silent re-link on a later pass, it could sit there **permanently**, hiding a genuinely open question from every future reviewer. New `undoAcceptedMerge` detaches and reopens the candidate to `needs_review` in one transaction.
- **P1 — rejecting a stale-link pair inside a 3-or-more-member cluster could sever a relationship nobody judged.** §14.1 builds clusters from independent pairwise merges, so a shared opportunity can legitimately hold A~B~C. The first version of the split logic unconditionally split the rejected side into a fresh singleton regardless of cluster size — safe for A~B, silently wrong for A~B~C, where it would also tear B away from C. No 3-member cluster exists in the live corpus today (checked directly), but the schema and the dedupe logic both allow one. Fixed by refusing rather than guessing: `rejectDuplicateCandidate` now throws if the shared cluster has more than two live members, naming `npm run browse` as the way to resolve it directly.
- **P1 — `pickSurvivor`'s first-seen tie-breaker could pick the direction the new cluster-size guard then permanently refuses.** Once one fan-out sibling is accepted, that side's opportunity has more than one member — and a first-seen-only rule can still select the ALREADY-clustered side as the one to move, which the guard then blocks every time, making an otherwise-safe accept of the next sibling impossible through this screen. `listReviewQueue` now reports each side's live cluster size (`aClusterSize`/`bClusterSize`, one extra query joined once per page render, not once per pair); `pickSurvivor` checks it first and only falls back to first-seen date when both sides are equally (un)clustered.
- **P2 — two fan-out accepts on the same shared listing, submitted concurrently rather than in sequence, could both pass the cluster-size guard** by each locking a different candidate row and reading the listing's membership before either committed — the second then failed on the database's own partial unique index instead of this file's clear refusal. Fixed with a row-level lock (`for update`) on the moving listing's own membership read, so the second transaction waits and re-reads the true post-accept state.
- **P2 — the review screen's own copy said rejecting "touches nothing else," which the stale-link split (the P1 two above) made false for exactly the pairs that trigger it.** Reworded to describe both outcomes.
- **P2 — a conflict's raw internal message was rendered verbatim, UUIDs and function-name jargon included** — exactly what `anti-patterns.md`'s Vocabulary section forbids ("raw enums or IDs", "untranslated jargon from the codebase"). A `userFacingConflictMessage` mapping now translates each known refusal into a plain sentence naming the situation and what to do about it; the internal message is kept for whatever reaches the generic error boundary instead. The same round also fixed a stale conflict banner persisting after a later, unrelated success — both actions now redirect to a clean `/review` URL on success rather than only revalidating.
- **Reconciling shortlist decisions across a merge was the sixth finding, and the deepest.** If the listing losing its opportunity to a merge had been saved or dismissed, the decision stayed keyed to the now-empty opportunity — invisible to the shortlist screen, which surfaces nothing for a memberless opportunity — while the surviving opportunity showed no decision at all. For a dismissal specifically, that is the exact failure the shortlist's own design note warns against: a vacancy dismissed once would read as never decided. `reassignListingWithin` — the ONE shared primitive both `reassignListing` and `acceptDuplicateCandidate` funnel through — now reconciles automatically whenever a move leaves the losing opportunity fully empty: the losing decision moves onto the survivor if it has none, or is discarded if the survivor already has its own (which wins, since a reviewer explicitly directed the merge there). Left alone when the losing opportunity still has other live members, since its decision still describes them.

Every fix above is mutation-checked (a targeted revert flips exactly the test written for it, and nothing else) and grounded in the live corpus where the scenario allowed it — real fan-out, a real leaked-row count, a real query against `duplicate_generation_method`'s and `duplicate_candidate_status`'s actual values rather than a test fixture's. 677 of 678 tests pass; the one failure remains `queries.test.ts`'s pre-existing, already-flagged corpus-growth fragility, unrelated to this branch.

**A third commit gate round found four more P1s and two more P2s — again inside the round-2 fixes.** Five rounds now, on one screen, and every round has found something real in the round before it.

- **P1 — the new cluster-size fallback claimed a singleton opportunity a detached listing does not have.** `clusterSizesForListings` reported `1` (not `0`) for a listing absent from the map — exactly what `undoAcceptedMerge` leaves behind. `pickSurvivor` could then read that fabricated `1` as tied against a genuine singleton and pick the UNCLUSTERED listing as survivor, at which point `acceptReviewPair`'s own "no live membership to merge into" check would fire — so undo, then immediately try to re-accept the same pair, and it could fail until an unrelated dedupe pass gave the listing a fresh opportunity. **The bug had already shipped past its own new test**, because that test's "singleton" fixture never got a real membership either — `addListing` alone creates none — so it was silently asserting `1` for a listing that, by the code's own logic, should have reported `0`. Fixed by changing the fallback to `0`; both affected tests were corrected to use a REAL singleton opportunity (matching the production invariant every listing actually has one), and a new, separate test proves the true zero-membership case directly.
- **P1 — the survivor's membership was read unlocked, before the transaction that moves anything.** The round-2 concurrency fix locked the MOVING listing's row; the SURVIVING side's row — read to decide the merge target — was not. With fan-out pairs A-B and B-C accepted concurrently, one transaction could cache B's opportunity as a merge target, have B moved elsewhere by the OTHER transaction in the meantime, and still proceed — recording A-B as `confirmed_same` while A and B sat in different clusters. Fixed by locking both rows in ONE query, ordered by listing id so two transactions contending for the same pair always request their locks in the same order rather than risking a deadlock.
- **P1 — the undo button detached whatever the listing currently happened to be in, not what the page showed.** A stale detail page — left open while another review decision, a fresh dedupe pass, or a direct correction moved the listing — could have its "Undo this merge" button silently detach the listing from a cluster the reviewer never looked at. `undoAcceptedMerge` now takes the opportunity id the page is displaying, locks the listing's current membership, and refuses if it no longer matches — check and act as one atomic step, not read-then-hope.
- **P1 — a listing with no readable title could get a fabricated one, permanently.** `sourceListings.currentRevisionId` is nullable, so the title lookup for a stale-link split could come back empty; the fallback (`'Split from a rejected duplicate pair'`) would then be stored as a REAL canonical title if a split actually happened — the fabricated-data failure this project treats as P1 everywhere else, reached through the one screen that had so far avoided it. Now refused outright, before `rejectDuplicateCandidate` is even called, with the same reasoning applied to `type`.
- **P2 — the evidence shown to a reviewer was the scorer's own internal sentences, spec citations included** ("§14.2 forbids auto-linking...", "vacancy-level application value") — exactly the jargon `anti-patterns.md`'s Vocabulary section names as forbidden, missed because round 1's evidence-gating fix checked only WHETHER reasons existed, never what they said. A new `reviewerReasons` translates each of the handful of shapes a `needs_review` reason can actually take into plain wording, checked against the real live queue's own reason set rather than invented examples; a reason it does not recognise becomes a stated placeholder, never the raw sentence and never silently dropped.
- **P2 — two listings' fields were rendered as independent columns, so a missing field on one side shifted every field below it out of alignment with the other's** — on the one screen whose entire job is making differences between two listings visible. Rebuilt as a real `<table>`, mirroring the opportunity detail screen's own comparison table: one row per field, both sides in fixed columns, a missing value rendered "not stated" in its own cell rather than closing the gap.

Every fix in this round is mutation-checked the same way as the two before it. 682 of 683 tests pass; the one failure is still `queries.test.ts`'s pre-existing, unrelated corpus-growth fragility.

**A fourth commit gate round found four more P1s and three more P2s.** Four rounds now, every one finding something real in the round before it.

- **P1 — undo left the reopened candidate vulnerable to a silent re-merge.** `undoAcceptedMerge` set `decidedBy: null` on the candidate it reopened. `run-dedupe.ts`'s own upsert only skips overwriting a row when `decidedBy` reads exactly `'human'`; left `null`, the very next `npm run dedupe --auto-link` could silently re-score the pair `confirmed_same` and re-merge it before the reviewer who clicked undo ever saw the reopened candidate — reversing the undo with nobody touching anything. Fixed by setting `decidedBy: 'human'`: a person did just act on this pair, and that is exactly what the field is for.
- **P1 — the survivor's membership was still unlocked in reject's stale-link path**, the same concurrency class round 3 already fixed for accept but had not yet reached reject: `isStaleLink` was computed from an unlocked read, so a concurrent transaction could move either listing between that check and the actual split. Fixed with the same combined, id-ordered lock.
- **P1 — the undo button detached whatever the listing currently happened to be in, not what the page showed.** A stale detail page could silently detach a cluster the reviewer never looked at. `undoAcceptedMerge` now takes the opportunity id the page displayed and refuses under lock if it no longer matches.
- **P1 — a listing with no readable title could get a fabricated one, permanently, if a split happened to be needed.** `sourceListings.currentRevisionId` is nullable. Refused outright now, before `rejectDuplicateCandidate` is even called.
- **P1 — the shortlist reconciliation round 2 added had no reverse.** Undoing a merge whose losing side once had a decision that got reconciled onto the survivor leaves that decision looking like it is about the wrong vacancy, with no way back. **Investigated rather than patched over**: a full reversal turns out to need either changing `detachListing`'s contract for every other caller (it deliberately leaves a detached listing with no opportunity at all, so there is nowhere valid to move a decision back to) or building the audit trail `opportunity_decisions` deliberately does not have, by Stage 9's own explicit design choice. Implemented instead: a loud warning, the same pattern `cleanupTestSource`'s entangled-cluster case already uses, since the corpus holds zero real shortlist decisions today and this is about correctness once it does not — not a fabricated fix for a problem this schema was never built to solve silently.
- **P2 — the review screen never caught its own new fan-out and stale-detach conflicts**, so they fell through to the generic "Postgres may be down" error boundary on the opportunity detail page specifically (the review screen's OWN actions already caught theirs). Now caught and redirected to an inline, refreshable message on the same page.
- **P2 — a stale-link pair looked identical to an ordinary one**, so a reviewer had no way to know that "Different vacancies" on THIS pair actually splits an existing merge apart rather than merely recording a verdict. `run-dedupe.ts`'s own marker string (`'existing link no longer supported by the current ruleset'`) is now detected, surfaced as a prominent notice ahead of the comparison table, translated properly in the evidence list, and reflected in both button labels ("keep merged" / "split apart").
- **P2 — the pending count and the rendered list could disagree**, since `listReviewQueue` silently drops a candidate whose listing has no readable current revision while `countReviewQueue` counts it anyway — so the screen could show a positive count above an empty-state message insisting nothing was pending. Fixed on both ends: the count line now says how many aren't shown rather than only sometimes admitting it, and the empty state itself branches on `total`, so "nothing is pending" is never shown when something demonstrably is.

Every fix is mutation-checked. 684 of 685 tests pass; the one failure is still `queries.test.ts`'s pre-existing, unrelated corpus-growth fragility.

**OWED: a Codex review of the round-4 fix commit.** The pre-commit hook did not report a finding — it hit the same usage-limit error recorded repeatedly during Phase 3B (`ERROR: You've hit your usage limit … try again at Sep 15th, 2026 2:03 AM`), twice in a row inside the same review attempt, then failed the hook outright rather than completing. Committed with `--no-verify` by the same standing decision this project has applied every prior time credits ran out: the code was typechecked, linted, the full suite run, every non-trivial fix mutation-checked, and the screen re-verified in a real browser (console clean, zero horizontal overflow at 390/768/1280/1920px) before this commit — matching the evidentiary bar the three prior owed-review episodes in this file used, not a lower one. **This branch must not merge until that review has run**, per the same rule stated for every earlier owed review here.

**A supplementary review found and fixed one more real defect while Codex's own review stayed owed.** With Codex still down, a general-purpose subagent ran the same checklist the newly-added `dedupe-correctness-reviewer` agent definition specifies (that custom agent type itself wasn't loadable mid-session; the checklist was carried over by hand) against the full branch diff. Everything from all four prior rounds held up under a fresh, independent pass — the fan-out guard, the lock ordering, the stale-link 3-member refusal, `decidedBy: 'human'` on reopened candidates. One real gap survived: **`rejectReviewPair` (`web/app/review/actions.ts`) read the moving listing's opportunity `type` unlocked, outside any transaction, before calling `rejectDuplicateCandidate`** — but whether a split actually happens (`isStaleLink`) is decided inside that function's own transaction, from a fresh LOCKED read. A reassignment landing between the two (e.g. a direct `npm run browse` correction, or an unrelated accept) could move the listing into a differently-typed opportunity before the split ran, storing the stale pre-fetched type as the new opportunity's real, permanent type. Fixed by removing `type` from `rejectDuplicateCandidate`'s public input entirely and resolving it inside the same locked transaction that decides `isStaleLink`, from the shared opportunity's actual live row — the caller no longer supplies a value that can go stale. New test: `makeOpportunity` widened to take a type, and a dedicated case splits a `scholarship`-typed cluster and asserts the split inherits `scholarship`, not `job` (the fixture default) — mutation-checked by reverting to a hardcoded `'job'` and confirming exactly that one test fails. 685 of 686 tests pass; the one failure is still the same pre-existing `queries.test.ts` fragility. Re-verified in the browser: `/review` renders with zero console errors after the action file's import changes.

**OWED (sixth): a Codex review of this fix commit.** Same usage-limit error as round 4's, still unresolved at the time of this commit. Committed `--no-verify` by explicit user decision, on the same evidentiary bar as round 4's owed review — this is real application code (`src/dedupe/`, `web/app/review/actions.ts`), not tooling, so it is carried as debt rather than waived. **This branch must not merge until both owed reviews (this one and round 4's) have run.**

**These two reviews are now discharged (2026-09-15), run individually against their commits (`codex review --commit <sha>`) since credits had returned and both commits already had later history on top.** The round-4 review (`codex review --commit f93f60a`) reported exactly the TOCTOU P1 described above — confirming the supplementary subagent review's finding was real, not a false positive, and that round 6's fix was the correct response, not busywork. The round-6 review (`codex review --commit 864f3b9`, the fix itself) reported no findings: "removes the stale caller-supplied type and derives it from the shared opportunity after locking the relevant memberships within the transaction... no introduced correctness issue identified." **Two more reviews are still owed elsewhere in this file (the stage-plan commit and its follow-up, below) — see the discharge note near them; this branch is not clear to merge until both are resolved too. A first version of this note claimed all review debt was clear and the commit gate correctly rejected it as a P1 (2026-09-15): this file is read as the source of truth for outstanding work, so a locally-true claim that ignores a debt recorded elsewhere in the same document is exactly the kind of error that gate exists to catch.**

**Also completed during this stage, running in the background across all four review rounds: the project's first full-coverage crawl of either source.** `npm run crawl:hr-ge`, started before Stage 1 began and finished partway through round 3 — `status: completed, fullCoverage: true, discoveredCount: 3373, newCount: 3290, failedCount: 5, quarantinedCount: 0`. hr.ge's real corpus is roughly 34× the 100-listing bounded sample every prior figure in this file was built from. This is genuinely useful for the taxonomy work 3C-2 still owes (more real `structuredAttributes.specialty`/`.industry` values to normalize from), but it is also the direct cause of the `queries.test.ts` fragility recorded throughout this stage: several fixture-based tests assumed a small, static corpus and a handful now assert against real, growing row counts. Fixing that fragility was explicitly deferred to a separate, small commit on `main` by the project owner's decision — not part of this branch.

**Phase 3B was re-scoped on 2026-09-14 rather than waived a third time, and is complete with every exit-gate item met.** The last item to close was the stranded `test-source-<uuid>` rows in the board filter; it was briefly carried as an open item, then closed properly by clearing the debris from the live corpus. The two remaining deliverables — the duplicate-review screen and taxonomy review — were split out into a new **Phase 3C**, which has not started. This is a scope decision, not a completion claim, and it is written down here because the alternative was the pattern this file already records twice: declare a phase done with its gate unmet and carry the debt forward under a heading that says "complete".

**What re-scoping does and does not buy.** It buys one thing: Phase 3B's own exit gate below is now checked off because every item in it is actually true, rather than waived. It does **not** close Phase 3. The concept's "Phase 3 — browse and shortlist" explicitly requires duplicate review and taxonomy review to be exposed, so **Phase 3 as a whole stays open until 3C lands** — moving work between sub-phases changes when it is done, never whether it is owed. Anyone reading this heading as "the UI phase is finished" is reading it wrong: six screens are finished, and two are not built.

### Phase 3C stage plan (written 2026-09-14, before any code — the previous phase's plan "only existed in a session" until its fourth stage, and that omission is directly on the list of things a whole-branch review found)

Two independent pieces of work, on two branches, not one — lumping them is what made 3B need a re-scope. Both are written here against numbers read from the live corpus today, not against the eight-day-old note that used to sit in this section.

#### 3C-1 — the duplicate-review screen

**Fully unblocked.** Stage 10 (merged in PR #6) already built everything the screen needs to consume: `listReviewQueue`/`countReviewQueue` in `src/browse/queries.ts` return each pending pair with both listings' full `ListingView` and the scorer's own `evidence.reasons`; `acceptDuplicateCandidate` and `resolveDuplicateCandidate` in `src/dedupe/membership-review.ts` are the two actions a reviewer takes. The shortlist screen (Stage 9) is the template to follow, not a fresh design: server actions gated by `assertWritesEnabled()` before any read, `<form action={...}>` with no client JavaScript, `revalidatePath` after every write. `web/lib/writes.ts`'s own comment already names this screen and the pending-pair count, so the write-gate story does not need re-deciding.

- **The queue today: 11 pairs, read directly rather than assumed.** 8 score `1.00`, 2 score `0.86` (the senior/junior analyst hard-negative pair recorded in the Phase 2B section, still correctly unmerged), 1 more at `1.00` with a distinguishing employer-level-application-value reason. Every pair carries real Georgian titles and organizations — `დიჯითალ ოპერატორი` at `თიბისი`, `დისტრიბუტორი` at `იფქლი`, and so on — so the screen can be built and judged against real content from the first commit, not lorem ipsum.
- **A real design question the queue itself exposes, found by reading the actual rows rather than the function signatures.** Four listings each appear on the SAME side of **two or three** separate pending pairs — one jobs.ge listing (`დიჯითალ ოპერატორი` at თიბისი) is in three, each paired with a different hr.ge listing. `acceptDuplicateCandidate` has no guard against this: accepting one pair moves that listing into a cluster; accepting a *second* pending pair for the same listing does not fail — it silently retires the first, correct membership and moves the listing into a different cluster instead. Nothing in the codebase exercises this today (no test, no guard), because no UI has ever called this function against a queue with real fan-out.

**Decided (2026-09-14), then corrected the same day against real data before writing any code.** The first version of this decision said siblings would auto-resolve as `confirmed_same` — wrong, checked against `DedupeDecision`'s actual four values (`confirmed_same | probable_same | needs_review | distinct`), which have no "moot" state. Marking a sibling `confirmed_same` without merging it fabricates a merge that never happened, the exact failure class this branch keeps finding; marking it `distinct` fabricates a same/different judgment nobody made. Checking the live fan-out settled which risk is real here: the three hr.ge sides are consecutive announcement ids (492305/6/7) posted four seconds apart — genuinely ambiguous, not obviously one vacancy or three.

**So the fix is a guard, not a resolution.** `acceptDuplicateCandidate` gains one more check alongside its existing "the target must be where the other side lives" rule: refuse if the listing being moved already has a live membership with `decidedBy: 'human'` pointing at a DIFFERENT opportunity than the one requested. A stale sibling accept then fails loudly with a clear reason instead of silently un-merging the first, correct decision — failing closed rather than open, which is the pattern this project has praised every other time it appeared. The candidate itself is left in the queue rather than auto-settled; the screen shows the error and a reviewer decides by hand, which is honest given the vocabulary has no better answer. Needs one guard clause in `acceptDuplicateCandidate`, and a test planting exactly today's three-way fan-out, mutation-checked.

*(`main`'s own copy of this stage plan, written before any code, still described the original — wrong — "auto-resolve as `confirmed_same`" decision; superseded by the corrected version above, which is what was actually built.)*
- **A second, smaller question: which side moves.** Every listing lives in its own singleton opportunity until merged (verified: 410 listings, 410 live memberships today) — so accepting a pair genuinely can move either side into the other's cluster, and `acceptDuplicateCandidate`'s signature takes both `sourceListingId` and `toOpportunityId` rather than picking one. The two outcomes are equivalent in content but not in which opportunity id survives. A single deterministic rule (e.g. the earlier-`firstSeenAt` listing's opportunity always survives) removes this as a per-click decision; the alternative is exposing "which one wins" as a UI choice, which is more flexible and more to build and to explain.
- **Stages:**
  1. `web/app/review/` — the queue as a page (mirrors `/opportunities`'s list shape) with each pair rendered like the opportunity detail's board comparison (Stage 6), since that comparison view is exactly what a reviewer needs and already exists once for reference.
  2. `web/app/review/actions.ts` — `acceptDuplicateCandidatePair` and `rejectDuplicateCandidatePair` server actions, following the Stage 9 template exactly.
  3. Resolve the fan-out question (below) and implement whichever guard or UI treatment it settles on, with a test that plants exactly today's live scenario — two pending candidates sharing a listing — and proves the second `accept` either refuses or is unreachable, not merely that it "usually" behaves.
  4. Browser QA at all four required widths (§ the lesson from 3B), against the real queue.
- **Background, not blocking:** the hr.ge full-coverage crawl (~2.75h) runs while this stage is built, per the decision below. It changes the corpus during the stage (new listings, possibly new candidate pairs) rather than before it — the screen must not assume a static queue size, and re-checking the queue count after the crawl finishes is part of finishing this stage, not a separate step.

#### 3C-2 — taxonomy

**The stage plan below (written 2026-09-14) assumed hr.ge's `specialty`/`industry` were flat breadcrumb paths. That assumption was checked against the live corpus on 2026-09-15 and falsified before any schema was written**, on a branch built specifically to verify against real data rather than an 8-day-old 100-listing sample:

- **The same term appears at different array positions across listings — impossible for a single fixed parent→child path.** Querying the full 3390-listing hr.ge corpus (not the old 100-listing sample): `გაყიდვები` ("Sales") occurs 664 times at 7 distinct array positions, from 0 to 11. Depth also runs far deeper than the old note's "8 levels" — up to **16**, with one real example being 16 sibling HR sub-topics (training, labor relations, HR tech, HR policy, …) in one array, which reads as a multi-select tag list, not a narrowing path.
- **Traced to source: the adapter already knew this and discarded it anyway.** `src/adapters/hr-ge/detail.ts`'s `flattenNamedTree` (as of the last commit) had its own comment stating each raw node carries `name` plus an optional `children[]` — a real tree — and flattens parent and child into one undifferentiated name list "because... preserving the parent/child tree is not needed until something actually consumes it." `RECON_NOTES.md:202-203` confirms each node ALSO carries a stable id (`specializationId` for specialty, `advancedIndustryId` for industry, both mongo-style hex strings) plus a numeric `code` (specialty only) — both discarded by the flattening, both required by §15.2 step 1's "preserve source category IDs and labels exactly," which the previously-stored flat name strings genuinely cannot satisfy.
- **Fixed rather than built around.** `flattenNamedTree` replaced with `extractTaxonomyTree`, which preserves `{ sourceTermId, code, name, children }` as real nested nodes instead of a flat string array — verified against real fixture JSON (`detail-492368-email-application.html`'s specialty node `674ef639d86ecbd541ca78f2` / code `739` / "გაყიდვები", with its real child `674ef639d86ecbd541ca7db8` / "გაყიდვების კონსულტაცია და რჩევა" preserved as nesting, not flattened alongside it), not invented. `HR_GE_DETAIL_PARSER_VERSION` bumped `'v2'` → `'v3'` so a re-crawl actually persists the new shape (confirmed via `crawl.ts`: every listing is genuinely re-fetched and re-parsed on a full-coverage run regardless of prior content hash — only the new/changed/unchanged *write outcome* depends on the resulting hash, so nothing skips re-extraction). Mutation-checked: reverting the extractor to return `[]` unconditionally left the OLD `Array.isArray(...)` assertion passing while the new id/nesting assertion correctly failed — proof the old test would never have caught this class of regression.
- **jobs.ge still has nothing: reconfirmed 2026-09-15, 0 of 310 listings carry any `sourceCategories` value.** Unchanged from the 2026-09-14 decision: **hr.ge only, for now**, jobs.ge listings render as uncategorized rather than a fabricated or inferred category. See the fuller reasoning already recorded below, still current.
- **A second correction, found by the commit gate rather than by planning: `src/domain/taxonomy.ts` already exists.** Phase 0 already defined `TaxonomyTermSchema`/`SourceTaxonomyMappingSchema`/`ListingClassificationSchema` with real column shapes — a source-independent canonical term (`axis` from the full 12-value §15.1 enum, a stable `code` slug, a `label`, `parentId`), a per-source mapping (`sourceCategoryRaw` as ONE string, `method` from a fixed `ClassificationMethod` enum, nullable `confidence`), and a classification keyed to a **revision**, not a listing (`sourceListingRevisionId`) — and this file never checked it before drafting the migration plan below. The first draft would have keyed `taxonomy_terms` directly off hr.ge's own raw ids, conflating raw-source identity with canonical taxonomy identity — defensible only as long as hr.ge stays the only source, and exactly the kind of shortcut that breaks the moment a second source needs the same term. Caught before a migration was ever generated, not after. `taxonomy_terms` mirrors the existing domain contract now: `code` is a stable slug **derived from** hr.ge's raw id (honest about being source-derived while hr.ge is the only contributor, not presented as source-independent when it factually is not yet), `label` is hr.ge's own text, `axis` maps hr.ge's `specialty` → `profession` and `industry` → `industry` (the two real §15.1 axes hr.ge's own two structured fields actually distinguish — not a forced split into `profession` vs. `functional_area`, which hr.ge's data gives no way to tell apart).
- **A third finding, also from the commit gate: the opportunity detail screen would have gone blank.** `web/lib/opportunity-detail.ts` displays `specialty`/`industry` via a plain string-array reader (`asStrings`), which silently returns `[]` for the new tree-node shape — every hr.ge listing's "Filed by the board under"/"Board's industry" fields would have disappeared the moment the re-crawl below actually ran, with no error anywhere. Fixed with `asTaxonomyNames`, which reads either shape (a v2 flat string array, still held by every hr.ge revision until the re-crawl below runs, or a v3 tree-node array) and flattens node names the same way the old storage-level flattening used to display them — a display-only compatibility shim, not a change to what `structuredAttributes` stores or what the taxonomy tables are built from. Mutation-checked.
- **Revised stages, migration 0020 onward:**
  1. ~~Schema~~ **Fix the source first — done.** The adapter now preserves real hr.ge ids and tree structure; the detail screen tolerates both shapes.
  2. **Re-crawl hr.ge full-coverage — done, with a real, recorded gap.** `npm run crawl:hr-ge`, started 04:25:02 UTC and finished 08:05:37 UTC 2026-09-15 (12:05 local) per the crawl run's own persisted timestamps — 3h 40m, longer than the 2.75h estimate — this run also absorbed the mid-run death-and-restart recorded above. (A first version of this note said 13:38 local, which was when this session happened to notice the completion, not the crawl's own finish time — the commit gate correctly caught the inconsistency.) `status: completed, fullCoverage: true, discoveredCount: 3354, changedCount: 2201, unchangedCount: 1137, failedCount: 12, quarantinedCount: 0`. 3343 of 3395 hr.ge listings now carry a `v3` current revision; the remaining **52 stayed on the old `v2` shape, and — checked directly, not assumed — every one of them has real, non-empty `specialty`/`industry` data**. A first version of this note claimed that data was empty; the commit gate correctly rejected that as fabricated, since it hadn't actually been checked. The true breakdown: **41 are `expired`/`closed`** — no longer discoverable, so a full-coverage crawl has nothing to re-fetch them from, and their taxonomy data is frozen at whatever the pre-fix crawl last wrote. **11 are still marked `active` in this database, and this run genuinely attempted to re-fetch every one of them** (`fetch_attempts`, `crawlRunId: 86f2e790…`) — 9 came back `HTTP 410 Gone` (hr.ge itself reporting the listing no longer exists, which means our stored `active` status for those 9 is now stale and should itself be reconciled — a separate gap from this one), 2 came back `HTTP 504` (a transient gateway timeout, plausibly resolved by a retry). None of the 52 wrote a new revision, so none contributed to the backfill below — **the backfill covers 3343 listings' current content, not the nominal 3395.** Carried as a known gap rather than fixed here, and precisely bounded rather than hand-waved: retrying the 2 timeouts could recover those 2 listings' real `v3` data. Reconciling the 9 `410`s only corrects their stale `active` lifecycle status to `closed`/`expired` — it does **not** give them `v3` data, since a `410` means there is nothing left to fetch. The 41 `expired`/`closed` listings are permanently on the old shape; they are no longer discoverable, so nothing re-fetches them at all. **At most 2 of the 52 are actually recoverable by any near-term fix; the other 50 need either a legacy `v2`-string-array reader added to the taxonomy functions, or stay unclassified.** Both are real, separate scope from this stage's docs-plus-backfill commit. No cheaper reprocess-from-cache path existed for any of this, since `resources` stores no raw response body (`src/db/schema/resources.ts` has no content column).
  3. **Schema — done (migration 0020).** `src/db/schema/taxonomy.ts`: a faithful Drizzle mirror of the EXISTING `src/domain/taxonomy.ts` contract — `taxonomyTerms` (self-referencing `parentId`, `axis`, `code`, `label`, `taxonomyVersion`), `sourceTaxonomyMappings` (`sourceId`, `sourceCategoryRaw`, `taxonomyTermId`, `method`, nullable `confidence`, `taxonomyVersion`), `listingClassifications` (`sourceListingRevisionId`, `taxonomyTermId`, `axis`, `method`, `confidence`, `evidence`, `taxonomyVersion`, `createdAt`) — not the raw-id-centric shape the first draft above described. Reviewed by the new `migration-safety-reviewer` subagent before applying: pure `CREATE TABLE`/`CREATE TYPE` against three brand-new tables, no existing table touched, every column/constraint cross-checked against both the Drizzle definitions and the Zod domain contract and found to match exactly. One non-blocking note from that review: the domain contract's `confidence ∈ [0,1]` isn't enforced by a DB `CHECK` constraint, only `doublePrecision` — consistent with how confidence is already handled everywhere else in this schema (app-layer only), so not a new gap. Applied to the live database; all three tables confirmed present and empty.
  4. **Seed/backfill scripts — done, and run against the real corpus (2026-09-15).** `src/taxonomy/seed-terms.ts` (`seedTaxonomyTerms`) walks every current hr.ge revision's specialty/industry tree and seeds `taxonomyTerms` + `sourceTaxonomyMappings` for every node not already mapped, keyed by hr.ge's own `sourceTermId` — idempotent by construction, no fuzzy name-based normalization pass needed (a stable source id makes "is this the same node" exact rather than a judgment call, which is what the falsified flattened-string design would have needed one for). `src/taxonomy/classify-listings.ts` (`classifyListings`) then backfills `listingClassifications`, one row per selected node **parent and child both** (matching how the old flattening already treated a listing's selection), `method: 'deterministic_rule'`, `confidence: 1` throughout. Wired as `npm run taxonomy:backfill` (`src/cli/backfill-taxonomy.ts`), seed then classify in one run.
     - **First real run**: `{"seed":{"listingsScanned":3395,"termsCreated":765,"mappingsCreated":765,"termsUpdated":0},"classify":{"listingsScanned":3395,"classificationsCreated":16868,"unmappedNodes":0,"classificationsUpdated":0},"durationMs":22453}`. `listingsScanned: 3395` counts every listing iterated over, not every listing that contributed a term or classification — **the 52 still on the old `v2` shape (Stage 2, above) were scanned and produced nothing**, since `seedTaxonomyTerms`/`classifyListings` only walk the new tree-node shape; their old flat-string data was silently skipped, not counted as `unmappedNodes` (that counter only fires for a genuine tree node with no seeded mapping, which never happens for a listing that has no tree nodes at all). **765 real canonical terms** (617 `profession`, 148 `industry`), real nested Georgian labels with genuine parent/child structure (spot-checked: `კულინარია` nested under `სასტუმრო / რესტორანი / კვება`) — not invented, and correctly covering the **3343** listings that actually had `v3` data to walk. All 16868 classifications land at `confidence: 1`, `method: 'deterministic_rule'` — matching the anticipated shape exactly, so `/taxonomy-review`'s queue legitimately stays empty even with real data present, and its empty-state copy (fixed in Stage 6, below) stays true rather than becoming a false negative now that classifications actually exist. **The 52 un-reprocessed listings remain genuinely unclassified.** Re-running this backfill is idempotent and safe at any time, but it only actually picks up new listings — of the 52, that means **at most the 2 that failed on a transient `504`**, if a retry succeeds. The other 50 (41 `expired`/`closed`, 9 `410 Gone`) have no path to `v3` data at all without a legacy `v2`-string-array reader, which does not exist; nothing about today's run needs to be redone, but this isn't a "run it again later and it's solved" gap.
     - **Re-verified in the browser against the real backfilled data**: `/taxonomy-review` still renders "Nothing needs review" — correctly, since the copy only claims what's true about confidence, not that nothing was classified. The opportunity detail screen's `asTaxonomyNames` compatibility fix (Stage 1) rendered its first real `v3` tree data here too: "Filed by the board under: გაყიდვები · გაყიდვების წარმომადგენლობა და მოლაპარაკება · …" and "Board's industry: …" — real nested paths, correctly flattened for display, footer confirming `parser v3`. Console clean on both pages.
     - **The first commit attempt was blocked by the real commit gate — Codex was back from its usage limit — with one P1 and two P2s, all real.** *(P1)* Both inserts (term, then mapping) were two separate statements; a crash between them left a `taxonomyTerms` row with no mapping, and since `code` is deterministic from the raw id, a retry would hit `taxonomy_terms_code_unique` trying to recreate the term — permanently blocking that node without manual repair. Fixed by wrapping each node's term+mapping write in one transaction. *(P2)* Neither function actually reprocessed a node/pair already mapped under an OLDER `TAXONOMY_VERSION` — a version bump would silently leave every existing row stale despite this file's own "bumped whenever the seeding logic changes what it writes" claim, contradicting §15.2's "versioned deterministic mappings". Fixed: both functions now compare the stored version and re-derive (UPDATE, not INSERT) when it differs, returning new `termsUpdated`/`classificationsUpdated` counts. *(P2)* `cleanupTestSource` deleted a test source's `sourceTaxonomyMappings` row but deliberately left the `taxonomyTerms` row it pointed at (by design, since terms carry no FK to sources) — but the mapping was the ONLY thing that identified the term as belonging to this test source, so a test interrupted before its own cleanup would leave the term as **permanent, unattributable debris** no later orphan sweep could ever identify. Fixed: `cleanupTestSource` now captures the mapped term ids before deleting the mapping, then deletes exactly the ones left with zero remaining references (from any mapping or classification) — safe even if a term were ever genuinely shared across sources.
     - **A fourth bug, self-caught while fixing the first three**: the transaction fix's "already exists" branch stored the WRONG id in the in-memory map (`mappingId: termId` instead of the mapping's own freshly-generated id) — would have silently broken every future version-bump UPDATE for that node (it would match zero rows rather than the mapping). Caught by re-reading the diff before testing, not by a failing test.
     - **A fifth, unrelated `cleanupTestSource` gap found by mutation-testing rather than the commit gate**: `listing_classifications` and `source_taxonomy_mappings` both FK into tables this helper already unwinds, and neither was covered before this stage. Confirmed load-bearing by deliberately removing the `source_taxonomy_mappings` delete — the mutated run left real leaked rows (2 test sources, 4 orphaned terms) that tripped `real-data-guard.ts`'s fingerprint check, not just a test assertion. Leaked rows cleaned by hand (verified zero references before deleting).
     - **A sixth, unrelated fixture bug found by running the full suite, not the single test file**: both new test files hardcoded the exact same real hr.ge fixture id as their `sourceTermId`, and `taxonomy_terms.code` is globally unique (not scoped per source) — vitest runs test files concurrently, so the two files collided on insert the moment both ran at once. Fixed by generating a fresh, realistic-shaped id per test call instead (the id is an internal database key, not user-facing content, so synthesizing it doesn't conflict with this project's real-data-in-fixtures convention — only the Georgian label text needs to be real, and it still is).
     - **The second commit attempt was blocked again — one more P1, two more P2s, all real.** *(P1)* Round one's "delete an orphaned term" fix INFERRED ownership from "nothing currently references it," which is not the same claim as "this test created it" — a disposable source's mapping happening to be the last reference to some other, unrelated term would delete it too, and `taxonomy_terms` carries no FK to `sources` for the fingerprint guard to even notice. Fixed by making ownership EXPLICIT: `cleanupTestSource` now takes an optional `taxonomyTermIds` list, and only ever deletes a NAMED id — never one it inferred. *(P2)* The children-reference check from round one's own P2 fix only looked ONE level: a parent with zero direct mappings but a live grandchild-of-a-child chain could still be wrongly judged safe. *(P2, the same root cause read two ways)* — the real, load-bearing form of this turned out to be simpler and more common than "grandchildren": naming an ENTIRE tree's ids in ONE call (exactly what `seedTaxonomyTerms` produces, and exactly how `seed-terms.test.ts` was already calling this function) checked a child's presence against the SAME candidate batch the child was also being deleted from in that same pass — so the child's row was still there at query time, and the parent read as blocked, every single time. **Confirmed on real test runs, not hypothetically**: after fixing round one's gaps, a full suite run left **seven real leaked top-level terms** in the live database, each missing exactly the child that should have unblocked it. Fixed by making the deletion iterative: delete whatever is safe this pass, then re-check what remains now that the deleted rows are actually gone, looping until no further progress is possible. A dedicated test (`deletes an entire parent+child tree named together in one call, from one source`) locks this in, mutation-checked by bounding the loop to one pass and watching the exact same leak reproduce.
     - **The third commit attempt passed (no P0/P1), surfacing three more real P2s worth fixing rather than skipping — this project's own standing norm.** *(P2)* Round one's version-bump re-derivation compared `TAXONOMY_VERSION` alone: hr.ge renaming a category or moving it under a different parent, keeping the same node id, would never be picked up on an ordinary same-version rerun. Fixed: `seedTaxonomyTerms` now also compares the stored label and `parentId` against the fresh values, re-deriving on either signal. *(P2)* `classifyListings`'s version-bump re-derivation overwrote `axis`/`confidence`/`evidence`/`taxonomyVersion` unconditionally but left `method` untouched — a pair previously classified by `human_review` or `llm_classification` (neither ever produced by this function today, but the enum permits them) could end up claiming its original method while actually carrying this function's own mechanical values. Fixed by refusing to touch a pair whose existing `method` isn't already `'deterministic_rule'` — the same "never overwrite a human verdict" principle `run-dedupe.ts` already applies elsewhere. *(P2, attempted, then reverted as unsound)* the startup orphan sweep's `cleanupTestSource(row.id)` call passes no `taxonomyTermIds`, so round two's "explicit ownership only" fix stops it from ever cleaning up a crashed taxonomy test's debris. The first fix had the sweep derive ownership from "every term this already-confirmed-disposable source currently maps to" — **the fourth commit gate round correctly rejected this as P1**: a confirmed-disposable SOURCE does not prove the TERM it happens to map to was created by that source: a stale mapping pointing at a legitimate, source-independent term would pass it along as sweep-owned and delete it the moment nothing else referenced it. Reverted; the sweep now calls `cleanupTestSource(row.id)` plain, same as every other table it cleans. `taxonomy_terms` carries no durable creator column to name real ownership safely, so a crashed taxonomy test's term surviving as harmless, unmapped debris is an accepted, narrow gap — the same kind of tradeoff already made for stale-lock auto-recovery, documented rather than closed with unsound inference.
     - **The fourth round's other finding was real and fixed properly**: the round-3 relabel-detection fix processed each listing's tree inline as it was scanned, so if hr.ge renamed a category and some cached revisions still carried the pre-rename observation while others already had the post-rename one, the term's final label would toggle between the two depending on the database's unordered scan order — nondeterministic, and `termsUpdated` could over-count. Restructured `seedTaxonomyTerms` into two phases: **collect** every raw node observed across the whole corpus into one map first, keeping only the observation with the latest `provenanceFetchedAt` per node; **apply** each unique node exactly once, parents before children (an iterative pass, same technique as `cleanupTestSource`'s child-before-parent deletion). Two new tests lock this in — one proving the newer observation always wins regardless of scan order, mutation-checked by flipping the comparison and watching the wrong label win.
     - **Fifth round: one more real P2, the mirror image of `classifyListings`'s own method-preservation fix.** `seedTaxonomyTerms`'s re-derivation compared version/label/`parentId` alone, so a `taxonomyTerms` row curated via a `human_review`-method mapping (not produced by this function today, but the schema permits it) could be silently overwritten the moment a fresh hr.ge observation disagreed — destroying a human correction to the source-independent canonical term while the mapping kept claiming its original method. Fixed with the same guard already applied on the classification side: a mapping whose `method` isn't `'deterministic_rule'` is left untouched, version mismatch or not.
     - 19 tests total: `src/taxonomy/seed-terms.test.ts` (8), `src/taxonomy/classify-listings.test.ts` (6), and `src/db/test-support.test.ts` (4). Every fix mutation-checked — each one confirmed to leave real, verifiable debris or a real DB-level rejection when reverted, not just a failing assertion. Every leaked row surfaced across every round's mutation runs, and the one real bug caught mid-development, was individually inspected for zero references and cleaned by hand before moving on — never assumed safe.
     - **Five review rounds on one stage is the last time this file lets that run unbounded.** Every round found something real, which is why each was worth fixing — but the project owner's own explicit instruction going forward: a P2-only finding gets fixed once and committed, not chased through another round. This file will keep recording what was found and fixed; it will stop recording an escalating round count for non-blocking findings.
     - **Two accepted limitations, recorded rather than fixed, per that instruction.** The sixth round's own review named both, and both are genuinely narrow given hr.ge is still the only source contributing any taxonomy data — real if a second source or an actual human-review workflow ever exists, neither of which does today. (1) The `human_review`-method guard only checks the ONE mapping being seeded; a term shared by a deterministic mapping and a separately human-reviewed one (schema permits it, nothing produces it) could still have its shared canonical row rewritten via the deterministic side. (2) `classifyListings` reads a mapping's `taxonomyTermId` without checking the mapping's own `taxonomyVersion`, so a mapping intentionally held back at an old version (e.g. because it's `human_review` and round 5's fix now leaves it there) could still be classified against as if current. Both would need either term-level provenance tracking or a version check threaded through classification — real design work for whenever multi-source sharing or human review actually exist, not invented now against a hypothetical.
     - **A second instance of the already-documented corpus-growth fragility surfaced while re-running the full suite** (unrelated to this stage's own code — neither `src/taxonomy/` nor this fix touches `src/browse/queries.ts`): the background hr.ge re-crawl (Stage 2) has kept growing the live corpus throughout this stage (3706 listings as of this commit, up from 3395), and a second `queries.test.ts` case (`finds only listings whose content has been revised`) has now also crossed `searchListings`'s 500-row cap, alongside the one already recorded. Same deferred fix on `main`, not part of this branch.
     - **The Stage 2 re-crawl itself died mid-run when the previous session's process ended** (confirmed: `crawl_runs` stuck at `status: running` with no `finished_at`, and no hr.ge listing activity for ~4.7 hours despite the run supposedly still "in progress") — real progress survived it (1150 of ~3391 hr.ge listings already carry the corrected `v3` shape), but the stale, unreleased exclusivity lock would have silently blocked any restart. Recovered using this project's own documented procedure for exactly this failure class (`docs/STATUS.md`'s Phase 1A section): `update crawl_runs set status = 'failed', reconciled_at = now() where source_id = '<hr-ge id>' and reconciled_at is null`, confirmed by the single row it updated. Re-launched immediately after (`npm run crawl:hr-ge`); confirmed a fresh run row actually started rather than being skipped by the exclusivity check.
  5. **The jobs.ge decision — confirmed, no code.** Reconfirmed 2026-09-15: 0 of 310 jobs.ge listings carry any `sourceCategories` value. Unchanged from the 2026-09-14 decision — hr.ge only, for now; jobs.ge listings render as uncategorized rather than a fabricated or inferred category.
  6. **The taxonomy review screen — built and browser-verified.** `src/taxonomy/queries.ts` (`listAmbiguousClassifications`/`countAmbiguousClassifications`, threshold `0.8`) plus `web/app/taxonomy-review/` (page/loading/error, mirroring `/saved`'s structure) and nav entries in `web/lib/labels.ts` (`taxonomyAxisLabels`/`classificationMethodLabels`, following the typechecked-enum-map convention every other screen already uses) and `web/components/site-nav.tsx`. 3 new tests (below threshold is returned with real context; at-threshold and confidence-1 rows are excluded — the real, expected shape of this queue today). Count/list use the identical `WHERE` clause, avoiding 3C-1's own "count and list silently disagree" defect from the start; the page still defends against it (a `total > rows.length` note) in case a future join ever drops a row the count doesn't.
     - **Real interactive browser QA could not be completed for most of this stage** — the shared Playwright Chrome instance (profile `mcp-chrome-f292240`) was locked by an orphaned MCP server process (`node.exe`, PID 18908, and its Chrome tree under it) left over from an earlier turn that never released it; `browser_navigate` and `browser_close` both failed identically from this session, and killing the stuck process was blocked by this environment's own auto-mode classifier regardless of prior authorization. Verified in the meantime via an isolated second dev server (`node scripts/next.mjs dev web -p 3002`, same live `.env`, no writes) and its raw server-rendered HTML — real but not sufficient.
     - **Completed for real (2026-09-15)**, once the project owner killed PID 18908 by hand from outside the tool sandbox (`taskkill /PID 18908 /T /F`) and the browser released. Console clean throughout (0 errors, 0 warnings across the whole session). Zero horizontal overflow at all four required widths — measured two ways: same-origin `<iframe>`s at exact CSS pixel widths (390/768/1280/1920, `scrollWidth - clientWidth === 0` at every one), and a real resized viewport confirmed via `window.innerWidth` (this machine's `devicePixelRatio` is 1.25, so the requested and actual CSS width differ unless compensated — confirmed 390 exactly before screenshotting). **The specific risk this stage carried — a 6th nav link causing horizontal overflow at 390px, the exact defect class Stage 7/3B already found twice** — did not reproduce: the nav's `flex-wrap` wraps cleanly to two lines with no clipping. Empty state renders the corrected, non-fabricated copy; no raw enum or UUID visible anywhere on the page.
     - **OWED: a Codex review of this browser-QA commit.** Same usage-limit failure this file has recorded repeatedly (`ERROR: You've hit your usage limit … try again at 1:26 PM`). Committed `--no-verify` by explicit user decision — docs-only (`git diff --stat` shows one file), the same grounds as this branch's other waivers of this kind.
     - **The first commit attempt was blocked by the real commit gate, two P1s and one P2, all real.** *(P1)* Every review row linked to `/listings/<sourceListingId>`, a route this app does not have (only `/opportunities/[id]` exists) — every click would 404. Fixed by joining `listAmbiguousClassifications` out to `opportunitySourceMemberships` (live membership only, `supersededAt is null`) and linking to `/opportunities/[id]` when one exists, plain text otherwise. *(P1)* The empty state asserted "every taxonomy assignment today comes from hr.ge's own structured field, at full confidence" — a claim the query never actually proves: it only shows nothing scores below the threshold, which reads identically whether every classification is high-confidence or **none exist at all** (the real state, since the backfill hasn't run yet) — exactly the fabricated-pipeline-status `AGENTS.md` forbids. Fixed by rewriting the copy to assert only what the query proved. *(P2)* `evidence` was read from the table but never selected or rendered, leaving a reviewer unable to see WHY a low-confidence assignment was made (§15.2 step 7). Fixed with an `evidenceReasons()` reader and a rendered list.
