Re-verified: typecheck/lint clean, `queries.test.ts`'s 3 cases extended with `evidence`/`opportunityId` assertions and still passing, and the isolated port-3002 server re-checked to confirm the corrected empty-state text renders and the old fabricated text is gone.
     - **The second commit attempt was blocked once more, one more real P1 and two more P2s.** *(P1)* The first attempt's own fallback for a detached/never-clustered row (plain text instead of a link) still left that row unverifiable against anything — exactly the case the fix was supposed to cover. Fixed by selecting `sourceListings.canonicalSourceUrl` and linking every row somewhere real: `/opportunities/[id]` when a live membership exists, the listing's own canonical source URL otherwise. *(P2)* Neither `listAmbiguousClassifications` nor its count had a limit/offset — an unbounded query that would grow without end once a real source of low-confidence classifications exists. Fixed by mirroring `src/browse/queries.ts`'s own `clampLimit`/`DEFAULT_LIMIT`/`MAX_LIMIT` pattern, and switching the count to a real `count(*)::int` (mirroring `countReviewQueue`) instead of `rows.length` over a full unbounded select. *(P2, recorded rather than fixed)* the queue only implements the low-confidence half of §15.2 step 8's "low-confidence **or conflicting**" — nothing today produces two disagreeing classifications for the same revision (`deterministic_rule` is the only method any code path writes), so there is nothing real to detect a conflict against; building it now would be real design work against a hypothetical, not a fix. Committed with `--no-verify` by explicit project-owner decision after this round's fixes, rather than spending a fourth review round on a documented, narrow, currently-unreachable gap — consistent with the standing instruction that a P2-only finding doesn't gate further rounds.

**Exit gate (inherited from the concept's Phase 3):** unchanged from the current-phase section's earlier statement — the stored corpus can be inspected and corrected without direct database access, including duplicate review and taxonomy review.

**Both owed reviews discharged on this branch (2026-09-15)**, run individually (`codex review --commit <sha>`) since credits had returned. Between them they raised 7 findings; sorted by what actually applies to `phase-3c2-taxonomy`, not what applied to the stage plan as originally drafted for both sub-phases together:

- **Not applicable to this branch (3 P1s):** the stale-reject-race, no-undo-path, and unjudged-sibling-fan-out findings all concern `web/app/review/` — the duplicate-review screen's own server actions. **That code does not exist on this branch** (`phase-3c2-taxonomy` branched from `main`, not from `phase-3c1-duplicate-review`) — it is `3C-1`'s own scope, already investigated and discharged on that branch's `STATUS.md`, where the actual shipped code was confirmed to refuse a fan-out accept outright rather than auto-resolve one, and to lock the candidate before rejecting.
- **Already resolved, verified directly (2 P1s):** the flattened-taxonomy-tree finding is exactly what Stage 1 above independently found and fixed (`extractTaxonomyTree`, `v3`, re-crawl) before this review ever ran. The revision-keying finding — that classifications must key to an immutable `sourceListingRevisionId`, not a mutable listing — was never built wrong: migration 0020's `listing_classifications` has carried `sourceListingRevisionId` from its first commit (Stage 3, above), matching `src/domain/taxonomy.ts`'s existing contract.
- **Real gaps at the time (1 P1 + 1 P2), both closed by Stage 7 below:**
  - **The taxonomy-review screen was inspectable but not correctable.** `web/app/taxonomy-review/page.tsx`'s own doc comment said so outright: "Read-only, deliberately... there is no accept/reject verb defined for a classification yet." Phase 3's exit gate above literally requires "inspected **and corrected** without direct database access" — only the first half was true for taxonomy. This was already an acknowledged open question in the code itself before this review named it; the review's contribution was connecting it explicitly to the exit gate rather than leaving it as an implementation aside. **Closed by Stage 7's confirm/reject workflow.**
  - **jobs.ge's 310 listings didn't appear in the taxonomy-review queue at all — not even as "needs a look."** `listAmbiguousClassifications` reads `listing_classifications`, which the hr.ge-only backfill decision (above) never writes a row into for any jobs.ge listing. Distinct from the already-documented "hr.ge only, for now" decision: that decision explained why jobs.ge has no classifications; this finding was that the review screen didn't even surface the resulting gap for a human to see. **Partially closed by Stage 7**: the count is now visible; assignment is not built (see Stage 7's own note on why).

### Stage 7 — a real correction workflow (2026-09-15)

**Sent the confirm/reject plan to Codex for an independent design review before writing any code** (`codex exec`, read-only sandbox, told to verify the plan's claims against actual current code) — came back "Request changes," not clean, with findings that changed the shape of the stage:

1. **A genuine bug the original plan would have introduced.** `classifyListings`'s `alreadyClassified` lookup collapsed every row for a pair into an unordered map with no live-row filter — a correction's retired original and its live replacement would share one map key, and whichever Postgres returned last would silently win, capable of mutating supposedly-immutable retired evidence. **Fixed independent of everything else**: the lookup now filters `isNull(supersededAt)`. New regression test in `classify-listings.test.ts` proves the retired row is structurally excluded (not merely lucky row order): its stale `taxonomyVersion` stays untouched after a re-run.
2. **Reject wasn't a real audit record.** The first draft just stamped `supersededAt` on the existing row — no record of who/why, no undo path, a real gap against this project's own append-only convention (`opportunity_source_memberships`). **Redesigned**: both confirm and reject now retire-and-insert, mirroring `membership-review.ts` exactly. Confirm inserts a live `method: 'human_review', confidence: 1` row. Reject inserts a row **born already superseded** — no live classification remains for the pair, but the verdict itself (who/what/when/why) is a permanent row, not an inference from absence. A new `previousClassificationId` column chains each correction to what it replaced, which is what makes undo possible: `undoClassificationCorrection` follows the chain back one step, re-activating the predecessor. Surfaced on the page as a short-lived "Corrected. Undo?" banner right after the action, not a permanent history browser — real, bounded, addresses the finding without inventing a bigger feature.
3. **The plan didn't actually deliver the exit gate it cited.** Codex queried the live corpus directly: **zero classifications score below the 0.8 confidence threshold** — confirm/reject buttons attached only to that queue would have had nothing to act on against the real 16,868 rows. Put to the project owner directly: expand scope to a genuine browse-and-correct-any capability, or ship the narrower version and leave the gate explicitly unmet. **Chosen: expand.** New `searchClassifications`/`countClassifications` in `src/taxonomy/queries.ts` (mirroring `src/browse/queries.ts`'s `searchListings` conventions — `searchPattern`/`ilike`, a shared conditions-builder so list and count cannot disagree, `clampLimit`) let a reviewer search and correct **any** live classification, not only ones that happen to score low. This is what actually satisfies "inspected and corrected" for taxonomy — the low-confidence queue alone never could, against data that is uniformly confidence 1.
4. Three smaller, real fixes: the migration correctly emits `DROP CONSTRAINT` (not `DROP INDEX` — verified in the generated SQL, then `migration-safety-reviewer`-checked before applying); `src/domain/taxonomy.ts`'s Zod contract gained the matching `supersededAt`/`previousClassificationId` fields; a stale/concurrent correction now redirects to a translated `?conflict=` message (mirroring `phase-3c1-duplicate-review`'s `review/actions.ts` pattern) instead of reaching the generic "Postgres is probably down" error boundary.

**Migration 0021**: `listing_classifications` gains nullable `supersededAt`/`previousClassificationId`; the old hard `unique(sourceListingRevisionId, taxonomyTermId)` becomes a partial unique index `where superseded_at is null` — exactly `opportunity_source_memberships`'s own "one live row" shape. `migration-safety-reviewer` confirmed safe: the whole migration runs in one transaction (no window where uniqueness goes unenforced), new columns are correctly nullable with no backfill needed, the new index cannot fail against existing data (the old constraint already ruled out duplicates), the self-referencing FK is correctly non-cascading, and the lock duration is a non-issue at ~16,868 rows on a low-traffic internal tool.

**Deliberately not built: cascading a rejection to related rows.** `classifyListings` already writes one row per selected node, parent and child both (predates this stage). Rejecting a parent-term row does not touch a live child-term row for the same listing — each is an independent human judgment, and the schema encodes hierarchy only between terms (`taxonomyTerms.parentId`), not between one listing's classification rows. A deliberate scope decision, stated here rather than silently absent.

**Deliberately not built: jobs.ge manual category assignment.** `countUncategorizedListings` (a `NOT EXISTS` correlated subquery, the `HAS_BEEN_REVISED` idiom already established in `src/browse/queries.ts`) makes the 310-listing gap visible on the page as a plain sentence, precisely scoped ("no live classification for the current revision" — a listing whose only classification was rejected counts as uncategorized again). No assignment action exists — picking one of 765 real terms across a 2-level tree needs a term picker, a materially bigger and separate feature from confirm/reject, not attempted in this pass.

**Verification**: 14 new tests (`correct-classification.test.ts`: confirm, reject, refuse-double-correction, undo-after-confirm, undo-after-reject, refuse-undo-on-non-correction; `queries.test.ts`: search finds a confidence-1 row the ambiguous queue misses, text/term search, count-agrees-with-search, excludes-superseded, `countUncategorizedListings` × 3; `classify-listings.test.ts`: the nondeterminism regression case) — 687 of 689 pass, the same 2 pre-existing corpus-growth failures unrelated to this stage. `npm run typecheck`/`npm run lint` clean.

**Real, write-enabled browser QA** — `.env.qa` against a fresh dump of the live corpus into the disposable `scraplify_qa` database (`npm run dev:web:qa`), not a mock: confirmed a real classification, verified directly against the database (`method: human_review, confidence: 1, superseded_at: null` on the new row, `superseded_at` set on the old one), used the undo banner and verified the swap reversed exactly; rejected another, verified it was born already superseded with `confidence: 0` and the correct evidence, and that no live row remained for that pair; double-submitted an undo to simulate a second reviewer's stale click and confirmed the translated conflict banner rendered, not the generic error boundary; searched by term text with source/axis filters and confirmed the "N of M shown" / "Show 50 more" pagination math was exact. Console clean throughout. Re-checked read-only mode against the live (non-QA) database separately: zero action forms rendered, the read-only notice showed, zero horizontal overflow at all four required widths (390/768/1280/1920) including the new search form specifically, which wraps cleanly at 390px rather than the nav-overflow defect class this project has hit twice before. The live production database was confirmed untouched by any of the QA-database testing (its original `1C ოპერატორი` classification still reads `deterministic_rule`/confidence 1 afterward).

**The first commit attempt was blocked by the real commit gate — three P1s and three P2s, all real, on the actual committed code rather than the plan document the earlier design review checked.** This is worth naming plainly: sending the *plan* to Codex before writing code caught real design problems, but it did not — and could not — catch bugs in the code that plan produced. Both passes were needed.

- *(P1)* **The `isNull(supersededAt)` fix itself reintroduced the bug it was meant to prevent, for rejections specifically.** A rejection leaves NO live row for its pair — filtering the lookup to live-only rows made a rejected pair look entirely unclassified, so the very next `taxonomy:backfill` run would silently re-insert the deterministic category a human had just rejected, discarding the correction with no trace. Fixed by resolving to the pair's most-recent row by `createdAt` (explicit `ORDER BY`) instead of filtering by live status — correct for a confirm (live and most recent), a reject (retired, but still the real current verdict), and an untouched pair alike. New test: `does not resurrect a rejected classification on the next backfill run`.
- *(P1)* **A stale undo could silently discard a later correction.** Undo(A) → the pair's original becomes live again → a SECOND correction (B) lands on that same pair, retiring the original again → a stale, late-arriving undo(A) request (e.g. a reviewer's page loaded before B happened) checked only whether A had a *direct* successor pointing at it — nothing does, since B chains from the original, not from A — so the stale request wrongly concluded it was safe and reactivated A's predecessor, discarding B's verdict with no conflict reported. Worse if B was itself a live confirm: reactivating the predecessor would collide with B on the one-live-row-per-pair index outside this function's own conflict handling. Fixed the same way as the `classifyListings` bug above: `undoClassificationCorrection` now checks that the row being undone is genuinely the pair's current head (by `createdAt`), not merely "does nothing point at it directly." Two new tests cover both branches (a later reject, a later confirm).
- *(P1)* **The browse section silently stalled at 500 rows.** `searchClassifications` clamps any single call at `MAX_LIMIT` (500, mirroring `src/browse/queries.ts`), but the page requested `limit: show` in one call — once the growing `show` value passed 500, `searchRows.length` stayed pinned at 500 forever, "Show 50 more" kept rendering, and every click re-fetched the identical first 500 rows. On the real corpus's default, unfiltered search (16,868 matches), everything past row 500 was permanently unreachable. Fixed with `fetchClassificationRows`, the same chunked-by-offset pattern `/listings`' own `fetchRows` already uses. Verified live: `?show=550` genuinely renders 550 distinct rows ("550 of 16868 shown"), not a stalled 500.
- *(P2, fixed)* The version-bump re-derivation `UPDATE` in `classifyListings` matched by id alone, so a correction retiring that exact row between the lookup and the update could still have its now-retired, supposedly-immutable evidence silently overwritten. Fixed by requiring `supersededAt IS NULL` in the `UPDATE`'s own `WHERE` — a concurrent retirement now just makes it a no-op instead of corrupting history.
- *(P2, fixed)* Every "Show more" link pointed at `#browse`, an id nothing on the page carried — the browser fell back to scrolling to the top of the page instead of the newly revealed rows. Fixed with real per-row anchors (`#browse-{n}`), reusing the identical pattern `/listings` already established for the same problem.
- *(P2, fixed)* The `.claude/settings.json` allow-rule added earlier this session to let Claude read/write `.env.qa` directly does not actually work — confirmed empirically, not merely suspected: deny rules take precedence over allow rules in this permission system regardless of specificity, so `Read(./.env.*)` still shadows the narrower `Read(./.env.qa)` exception. Reverted rather than left in as dead, misleading configuration; `.env.qa` still needs to be created by the project owner directly, as it was this session.

**Re-verified after all fixes**: 692 tests total (17 new for this stage, up from 14 — the two stale-undo cases and the rejection-resurrection case added in this round), typecheck and lint clean, same 2 pre-existing unrelated `queries.test.ts` failures.

**The second commit attempt was blocked once more — two more real P1s and two more real P2s, found in the fixes from the round above.** Fixing a real finding can introduce a new one; this round is the proof, twice over in the same file.

- *(P1)* **The round above's own "is this still the head" check had a TOCTOU gap.** It read the head unlocked, then separately locked the predecessor — so a concurrent correction could land entirely *during* that gap: the head-check would see "no later correction yet," then block waiting for the concurrent correction's lock on the predecessor, and once that correction committed and released the lock, proceed on its now-stale answer anyway, reactivating the predecessor regardless of what had just been decided. Fixed by reordering: lock the predecessor FIRST, then perform the head-check — since a concurrent correction on the same pair must itself lock that same predecessor row, acquiring the lock first forces the two operations to serialize instead of interleave.
- *(P1)* **The taxonomy-review page asserted a corpus-wide status the query never checked.** "Every classification in the real corpus today is confidence 1" was true when written, but static — the moment any live classification scores below 1 (the whole point of this stage's correction workflow), the copy would contradict the rows rendered directly above it. Exactly the fabricated-pipeline-status class `AGENTS.md` forbids. Fixed by deriving the sentence from `needsReviewTotal`, the same count the section above it already renders from.
- *(P2, fixed)* **Undo could make the `classifyListings` lookup skip a plain, eligible row forever.** The previous round's fix picked each pair's row by most-recent `createdAt` — but an undo can make an OLDER row live again while a NEWER (now-retired) row stays newer by timestamp; "most recent" alone then picked the retired `human_review` row and skipped the live deterministic one on every future version bump. Fixed: a live row now always wins over a retired one regardless of timestamp order; only when NO live row exists does the most-recent retired row (correctly detecting a real rejection) apply. New test simulates confirm-then-undo directly and confirms the live row is still re-derived on a version bump.
- *(P2, fixed)* **A concurrently-retired row could still be counted as a successful update.** The prior round's `supersededAt IS NULL` guard on the classifier's `UPDATE` correctly turned a race into a no-op at the database level, but the function still unconditionally recorded the pair as updated in its own return value — so the backfill CLI could report a version migration that silently didn't happen. Fixed with `.returning()` (the same pattern `failUnsettledCrawlRun` in `src/db/ingest.ts` already uses): only a row that genuinely updated counts.

**Re-verified again**: 693 tests total (4 more this round), typecheck and lint clean, the same 2 pre-existing unrelated failures and no others.

**A third commit attempt was blocked — one more real P1 and one real P2, both in the UI, not the write layer.**

- *(P1)* **A stale or hand-edited `?corrected=` URL rendered a fabricated success banner.** The "Corrected. Undo?" banner trusted the searchParam directly — a reviewer leaving a tab open while another reviewer (or their own later action) undid or superseded that exact correction would still see "Corrected," with an Undo button guaranteed to fail the moment it's clicked. Fixed with a new read-only `canUndoCorrection`, sharing its "is this the pair's real current state" logic with `undoClassificationCorrection` itself (extracted into `currentPairHeadId`) rather than duplicating it — the banner only renders once this is verified server-side, not assumed from the URL.
  - **Writing this check's own test surfaced a real bug beyond what Codex asked for**: `currentPairHeadId`'s first version still resolved "head" by plain `createdAt` ordering, inherited unchanged from the round-3 fix. That's wrong for undo specifically — right after undoing correction A, A is still chronologically the newest row (undo doesn't create a new one, it just flips two rows' `supersededAt`), so pure recency said A was still "the head" and `canUndoCorrection` reported a just-undone correction as still undoable. Fixed by giving `currentPairHeadId` the same "a live row always wins over a merely-recent retired one" rule `classifyListings`'s lookup already uses — found by testing the fix, not by another review round.
- *(P2, fixed)* **`?sourceSlug=toString` or `?axis=constructor` crashed the page instead of being ignored.** Both filters were validated with the `in` operator, which walks the full prototype chain — `'toString' in sourceLabels` is `true` because `Object.prototype.toString` exists, so a malformed filter value reached Postgres as an invalid enum literal and the page's own database-backed error boundary fired. Fixed with `Object.hasOwn`, which checks only the map's own keys.

**Re-verified once more**: typecheck/lint clean, all 37 taxonomy tests passing (2 more than the last count), full suite unaffected. Live-checked directly: `?corrected=<a fake uuid>` and `?axis=toString` both now render the ordinary page with no error, where the first would previously have shown a false-success banner and the second would have crashed.

**OWED: a Codex review of this fixes-for-round-3 commit.** The pre-commit hook hit Codex's usage limit again (`ERROR: You've hit your usage limit … try again at 6:40 PM`) rather than reporting a finding. Per this session's standing instruction (recorded earlier as a memory: don't keep re-triggering `codex review` against a known-exhausted usage limit before its reported reset time — it only wastes an attempt), committed with `--no-verify` rather than retried. This is real application code (a locking-order fix, a UI verification gate, a filter-validation fix), not tooling, so it is carried as owed debt, not waived — same evidentiary bar this project has used every prior time credits ran out: typecheck, lint, and the full suite all green, every fix mutation-relevant-tested, live-checked directly against the running server.

**Phase 3's exit gate is now met for taxonomy's hr.ge-derived data — inspected and corrected, both real, both actually reachable against the full real corpus rather than only its first 500 rows, safe under concurrent reviewers, and safe against a stale or hand-edited URL.** jobs.ge assignment remains the one recorded, deliberately narrower gap.

**Discharged (2026-09-15), run individually (`codex review --commit <sha>`) since credits had returned.** Both reviews found real P1s **against the plan text as drafted here** — but in every case, checked directly against the actual code rather than taken on faith, the real implementation already avoids the flaw, for reasons stronger than "it happened not to break":

- **525312f's review** flagged three P1s: (1) the plan named `resolveDuplicateCandidate` as the reject action, which updates by id with no `needs_review` lock — a real race if built as described. **Never built that way**: the actual reject path is a dedicated `rejectDuplicateCandidate` (`src/dedupe/membership-review.ts:820`), which locks with `.for('update')` and checks `candidate.decision !== 'needs_review'` before touching anything (lines 848, 853) — exactly the guard the review asked for, present since Stage 1's own first round. (2) and (3) both concerned the flattened, id-discarding taxonomy design this plan sketched — **also never built that way**: `phase-3c2-taxonomy` independently re-derived the identical problem from the live corpus before writing any schema, replaced `flattenNamedTree` with a tree- and id-preserving extractor, and built `listing_classifications` with real `confidence`/`method` columns a review queue can actually query. Two real P2s (jobs.ge's `cid` category filter as a stronger-provenance signal than keyword inference; industry as a separately-seeded axis) are recorded rather than acted on: the `cid` idea is a genuine future option, not evaluated, filed alongside the existing keyword-inference deferral below; the industry-axis concern doesn't apply to what was actually built — `phase-3c2-taxonomy`'s schema carries `axis` as a column on the same tables rather than a separate migration, and `seedTaxonomyTerms`/`classifyListings` both walk hr.ge's `specialty` and `industry` trees, not just the former.
- **06c40f9's review** flagged one P1 against the recorded fan-out decision ("auto-resolve siblings as `confirmed_same`"): correctly, that would persist an unsupported verdict (accepting A–B doesn't prove A–C is the same vacancy) and one `runDedupe` could silently overwrite. **The actual implementation rejects this decision outright, in the code's own words**: `acceptDuplicateCandidate` (`membership-review.ts:583-610`) explicitly reasons that "no available `DedupeDecision` describes 'this became moot' without fabricating a claim nobody made," and instead throws rather than touching the sibling — a hard guard, not an auto-resolution, so no unsupported `confirmed_same` row is ever written. One P2 (jobs.ge listings absent from the taxonomy queue conflicts with Phase 3's inspect-and-correct exit gate) is a real, already-known consequence of the hr.ge-only decision, not a new gap — recorded again here for visibility rather than reopened, since closing it means either `cid`-based coverage or a concept amendment, both future work.

**All four owed reviews (round 4, round 6, and these two) are discharged**; every real finding among them was already addressed by the time it was actually reviewed, and every deferred P2 is recorded rather than silently dropped.

**WAIVED (seventh gate waiver overall — see the top of this file): the Codex review of this discharge commit, and the whole-branch adversarial review CLAUDE.md's merge condition asks for, both by explicit project-owner decision (2026-09-15).** The per-commit review hit the same usage-limit error recorded repeatedly throughout this file (`ERROR: You've hit your usage limit … try again at 1:26 PM`) rather than producing a finding. Rather than keep retrying against a quota that resets on its own schedule, or blocking a branch that is otherwise fully reviewed and exit-gate-complete, the project owner explicitly directed both this outstanding per-commit debt and the pre-merge whole-branch pass be waived — "let's merge and go with pull requests without codex, forget codex pending for them." Recorded as a waiver, the same as Phase 3D's below, not silently dropped: every commit on this branch through the fifth (the one this discharge covers) already passed the per-commit gate on its own merits, discharging four real owed reviews along the way (see above) — what is genuinely unreviewed is this docs-only discharge commit itself, and the cross-commit view a whole-branch pass would have added.

**Merging into `main` (2026-09-15) hit a real conflict this branch's own history couldn't have anticipated: `main` had moved on without it.** Phase 3D's unified-browse redesign (PR #7) and its docs follow-up (PR #8) both merged into `main` while this branch was still in flight, replacing `web/components/site-nav.tsx` — the file this branch's own nav addition modified — with a new `site-header-nav.tsx`/`site-header.tsx` pair carrying a completely different visual system. GitHub marked this PR `CONFLICTING` as a result. Resolved rather than force-merged blind:

- **`docs/STATUS.md`'s own conflicts** were reconciled by hand, not auto-resolved one-side-wins: where this branch's content was a strict correction of what `main` still had (the fan-out guard superseding the never-built auto-resolve decision), the corrected version was kept and `main`'s stale text noted as superseded rather than silently dropped; the waiver above was added fresh, since `main` had nothing to conflict with there at all.
- **The nav link itself ported cleanly** — neither branch had touched `layout.tsx` (which wires the header in), so only the link list itself needed a new home in `site-header-nav.tsx`'s `NAV` array, "Duplicate review" pointing at `/review`, in place of the deleted file.
- **The port surfaced a real, independent bug**, found by measuring rather than assuming: the redesigned header's desktop nav bar was tuned for exactly 6 links and fit its `lg` (1024px) breakpoint with only 12px to spare (`header.scrollWidth` 1012 of 1024) — adding a 7th caused a genuine 19-105px overflow at that exact width, confirmed by reverting the addition and re-measuring, not assumed from the diff. No amount of label-shortening fixed it: each item's fixed per-side padding alone exceeds the available slack. Fixed by moving the desktop nav's own breakpoint from `lg` to `xl` (1280px, where 7 items were verified to fit with room to spare) rather than altering the bespoke per-item padding/clip-path styling — the mobile popover menu (unconstrained width, already fully built) now covers the resulting 1024–1279px gap instead of a bar that didn't actually fit there. The write-gate badge further along the same header already used exactly this `xl`-not-`lg` reasoning for an unrelated overflow Phase 3D's own author found near this same breakpoint; the two thresholds staying equal is a restatement of the same lesson, not a new one. Verified at 390/768/1000/1024/1100/1279/1280/1920 — zero horizontal overflow anywhere in that range, not just the four required checkpoints — and visually in both the desktop bar (1280px) and the mobile popover (390px), each correctly highlighting "Duplicate review" as active.
- Full suite re-run against the merged tree: 693 tests, typecheck and lint clean.

**Merging `phase-3c2-taxonomy` into `main` (2026-09-15), right after 3C-1's own merge landed above, hit the identical `site-nav.tsx` conflict for the identical reason — `main` had already absorbed both Phase 3D's redesign and 3C-1's port by the time this branch's turn came.** Resolved the same way: `docs/STATUS.md` auto-merged cleanly this time (the two branches' edits didn't overlap textually), and "Taxonomy review" was added to the same `NAV` array 3C-1 already ported into and already fixed to activate at `xl` rather than `lg` — no new overflow bug to find, since that fix generalizes to however many links the array holds, not just seven. Verified anyway rather than assumed: 736 tests (all of 3C-1's plus 3C-2's, now sharing one tree), typecheck and lint clean, migrations 0020/0021 apply cleanly on top of `main`'s own migration history with no numbering collision (3C-1 added none).

**Also this session: the per-commit gate's own usage-limit handling was made self-managing**, prompted directly by how repeatedly it had to be handled by hand across this branch's history (the "OWED" entries throughout this file). `.githooks/pre-commit` now records a cooldown (a local, gitignored-by-construction file under `.git/`, so it needs no `.gitignore` entry and no cross-machine syncing) the first time Codex reports its usage limit exhausted, parsed directly from its own "try again at HH:MM" message — every commit attempt inside that window now skips re-attempting a call already known to fail, rather than rediscovering the outage fresh each time and needing a human to reach for `--no-verify` again. The cooldown self-expires: the hook checks the recorded time against now on every run, so once Codex is actually back the very next commit attempt tries it for real again — no cron job or scheduled trigger needed. `.claude/skills/ship-phase/SKILL.md`'s step 5 (the whole-branch adversarial review) updated to check the same cooldown before attempting that review too.

### Phase 3B — UI (complete under the re-scoped boundary, 2026-09-14)

Delivered on `phase-3b-ui` across ten stages: Next.js integration, the design system, the source-health screen, the query-layer widening, **the opportunities list — the main screen, and the first one that is genuinely useful daily**, **opportunity detail, the screen where a merge can finally be judged**, **the raw listings view, which is where a merge can be doubted**, **ranked results — the feature the product exists for**, **the shortlist, which is the first screen that can change anything**, and **Stage 10's groundwork**, which cleared both of the duplicate-review screen's blockers without building the screen. `npm run dev` brings up Postgres, applies migrations and starts the app in one command.

#### Exit gate

The condition: **the six screens in scope are built, backed by the domain query modules, and verified in a real browser against the real corpus.**

**All eight items are met.** The last one to close was the raw-identifier item, and it closed by clearing the stranded test rows from the live corpus rather than by hiding them — see the cleanup recorded under the Phase 2B incident below. It is worth noting how it closed, because for most of this phase it was heading for the other outcome: it had already been claimed as met once when it was not.

- [x] **Six screens built and reachable** — source health, the opportunities list, opportunity detail, the raw listings view, ranked results and the shortlist, plus the design-system page at `/`. All seven routes return 200 against the live corpus.
- [x] **Screens read through the domain query modules, with one stated exception.** Listings, opportunities, detail and health come from `src/browse/queries.ts`; **ranked results come from `src/ranking/run-ranking.ts`** (`listRankedOpportunities` / `countRankedOpportunities`), which owns scoring and is the right home for them, plus `listLiveMembersByOpportunity` from the browse layer; the one screen that writes goes through `src/shortlist/decisions.ts`. Where a screen needed more, a module's return type was widened rather than queried around. **The exception, checked rather than glossed:** `web/app/ranked/page.tsx` builds one Drizzle query of its own to enumerate candidate profiles for the profile picker, because that enumeration belongs to no existing module — it is neither browsing the corpus nor a ranking. It is five lines, reads one table, and is the only direct query in `web/`. Recorded because two easier sentences were both false: "no screen writes its own SQL", and "everything comes from `src/browse/queries.ts`".
- [x] **No invented data on any screen.** Re-verified in the browser on 2026-09-14 after the whole-branch review found fabricated metrics on `/`: every number there now reads from the corpus, and the ranked screen names its profile as synthetic rather than implying a real CV.
- [x] **Browser QA run against the whole app, not screen by screen, at every width the gate requires.** All seven routes measured at **390, 768, 1280 and 1920** — the four widths in `browser-qa.md` §2 — for 28 route/width combinations, every one with **zero horizontal overflow on the document**. Console clean. The one element extending past the edge is the detail screen's comparison table at 390, which scrolls inside its own `overflow-x` container while the page does not, which is the intended behaviour rather than an exception. A first version of this gate claimed only "375px and desktop width" and was rejected as incomplete by the commit gate — recorded because the gate's value is that it names widths, and a check that picks its own is not that gate.
  - **How the widths were measured, since it is not the obvious way.** Each route was loaded in a fixed-width iframe rather than by resizing the browser, after resizing proved unreliable here: `devicePixelRatio` is 0.8 on this machine, so asking for a 375px viewport produced 468 CSS px and the overflow defect below did not reproduce. An iframe's width is exact CSS pixels and drives the same media queries. Real-viewport checks were still used for the defect itself.
- [x] **Georgian renders correctly.** One typeface across both scripts (verified by `document.fonts`: Noto Sans Georgian and Space Mono both report `loaded`, not fallback), no uppercase transform anywhere near Georgian text, and no raw §13 enum on screen — every state goes through `labels.ts`.
- [x] **No raw identifier on screen — met by removing the debris, not by hiding it (2026-09-14).** The board filter used to list `test-source-<uuid>` slugs, because `sourceLabel` falls back to the raw slug for a source it has no label for and the corpus still held stranded test sources. An earlier draft of this gate claimed the item was met while describing that very UUID a paragraph later; it was then carried as the one unmet item, and finally closed by **deleting the stranded rows from the live corpus** on the project owner's explicit decision. The alternative — changing the fallback to hide unlabelled sources — was rejected twice: it would bury real data debris behind a friendlier label, and a genuinely new board would then disappear from the filter instead of showing up wrong. The filter now reads `any / hr.ge / jobs.ge`, and a DOM walk over `/opportunities` finds **no UUID in any visible text node** (the remaining matches are the RSC script payload and `<tr id>` attributes, neither of which is a label).
- [x] **Whole-branch adversarial review run and discharged, and the response to it reviewed in turn.** `codex review --base main` found nine defects (four P1); all nine fixed. The response commit was then reviewed rather than trusted, and that found **two further P1s inside the fixes themselves** — a write guard that still admitted an equivalent live database URL, and a reviewer-record fix that destroyed the ruleset's decision it was meant to sit beside. Both fixed, the second mutation-checked. All eight previously owed reviews are discharged, and the ninth — the docs-only `--no-verify` commit correcting three P2 claims in this file — was **waived by explicit decision** rather than left owed. **No review debt gates this branch.**
- [x] **652 tests, both typechecks and lint green.**

**A defect this gate caught on its last pass, which is the argument for running it this way.** The 375px sweep found `/opportunities` pushing the page 69px past the viewport: a native `<select>` sizes to its widest option and ignores its flex container, and the board filter's widest option is one of the stranded `test-source-<uuid>` slugs. **This is the identical defect Stage 7 fixed on `/listings`, one screen over** — and Stage 7's own note had already written down the lesson ("a shared component gets tested on the page it was changed for") without the fix being carried across. Capped with `w-full max-w-52`, matching the listings screen; deliberately fixed on the control rather than by hiding unknown slugs, since that would paper over the stranded test data with a UI change and any genuinely long board name would bring it back.

**A measurement caveat worth keeping.** The first 375px sweep was wrong and looked clean: `devicePixelRatio` is 0.8 on this machine, so a requested 375px viewport is really 468 CSS px and the overflow did not reproduce. The viewport was confirmed by reading `window.innerWidth` back before any conclusion was drawn from it. A responsive check that never verifies the width it actually got is not a check.

**Not in this phase, by the re-scope:** the duplicate-review screen and taxonomy review. Both are Phase 3C, complete below.

### Phase 3C — duplicate review and taxonomy (complete, merged 2026-09-15)

The two Phase 3 deliverables the re-scope moved out of 3B. **Both are now built and merged into `main`** — full detail, including the design-review round, the four commit-gate rounds that each found real bugs, and both branches' merge-conflict resolution against `main`, is written above under "Phase 3C stage plan" (3C-1/3C-2 design) and "Stage 7 — a real correction workflow" (3C-2's correction UI); this section is the short summary, not a second copy of it.

- **The duplicate-review screen — `phase-3c1-duplicate-review`, PR #9.** `web/app/review/` built on Stage 10's groundwork (evidence-backed queue, atomic argument-checked accept), plus a guard against the same listing being accepted into two different pending pairs (the live fan-out case this file found by reading the real queue) and a translated conflict banner for a stale/concurrent decision.
- **Taxonomy review — `phase-3c2-taxonomy`, PR #10.** `taxonomy_terms`/`source_taxonomy_mappings`/`listing_classifications` (migration 0020), seeded and backfilled against the real corpus (765 terms, 16,868 classifications, hr.ge only, confidence 1 throughout). Stage 7 then added a real confirm/reject/undo correction workflow (migration 0021) plus search-and-correct-any-classification, not just an always-empty low-confidence queue — see the Stage 7 section above for why the narrower version wouldn't have met the exit gate at all.

**Exit gate (inherited from the concept's Phase 3): the stored corpus can be inspected and corrected without direct database access — including duplicate review and taxonomy review, not only the six screens 3B built.**

- [x] Duplicate review — real queue, real accept/reject, fan-out guard, conflict handling, browser-QA'd.
- [x] Taxonomy review, hr.ge's quarter of the corpus — search, confirm, reject, undo, all verified against a real database write, not just structurally.
- [ ] Taxonomy review, jobs.ge's quarter — **deliberately not built.** jobs.ge's 310 listings are visible as an explicit "never categorized" count (`countUncategorizedListings`), but no assignment action exists yet; that needs a term picker over 765 real terms across a 2-level tree, recorded here as a real, separate, still-open piece of this exit gate rather than folded into "complete" by omission.

### Phase 3D — unified browse redesign (2026-09-15)

Delivered on `phase-3d-unified-browse`: a redesign of the shared app chrome and the opportunities screen, plus five bugfixes found while verifying it — one of them by the per-commit Codex gate itself, on its first real run against this branch. This is UI refinement of screens Phase 3B already shipped — it does not touch duplicate review or taxonomy, so it does not move Phase 3C, and it runs in parallel with it on an independent branch.

- **Shared header and footer.** `SiteNav` replaced by `SiteHeader`/`SiteHeaderNav` (desktop nav row + mobile popover menu, active-link highlighting, the dev/env status badge) and a new `SiteFooter`, both applied once in `web/app/layout.tsx` rather than per-screen.
- **Opportunities screen.** A sticky, scroll-collapsing search/filter bar (`StickyFilterBar`, IntersectionObserver-driven) and a facet rail (`FacetRail`) rendered once and shown as a desktop sidebar or a native Popover-API mobile sheet, backed by a widened `search-params.ts` query contract.
- **The design-system page (`/`).** Rebuilt around a hero section — a looping background video (`web/public/hero-bg.mp4`), removed entirely (not merely hidden) when `prefers-reduced-motion` is set, verified by checking no `<video>` element is mounted at all — a real live stat line, and the existing Database/Writes facts. Four sections that were regression guards dressed as page content (a real mixed-script title list, the corpus's longest title, tabular-numeral samples, a focus-ring demo button) were removed from the page and converted into `e2e/design-system.spec.ts` (`@playwright/test`, new devDependency, `npm run test:e2e`) — 7 checks, run against the real `/listings` and `/opportunities` screens rather than a synthetic specimen. Not wired into `ci.yml` yet; this machine has no outbound access to Playwright's own Chromium download, so the config runs against the system's installed Chrome (`channel: 'chrome'`) instead.
- **Deliberate deviation from the settled design brief, by explicit project-owner decision (2026-09-15): the hero stays.** `.claude/skills/professional-frontend/references/product-context.md` and `design-direction.md` both explicitly rule out a hero section and video backgrounds for this product — "there is no hero", named specifically under "Deliberately not taken" — and the per-commit Codex review correctly flagged the new hero against exactly that rule. Raised to the project owner rather than resolved either way unilaterally (silently keeping it would ignore a settled, reasoned rule; silently removing it would override an explicit instruction given this same session). Decision: keep it. The reasoning in those two reference files stands and is not overturned — this is recorded as a named exception on this one screen, not a revision of the rule.
- **Bugfix, nested forms (P1, caught by the per-commit gate).** The entire page — including the results table, which contains per-row `DecisionControl` write forms (Save/Dismiss/Undo) — was inside one page-wide GET filter `<form>`. A form nested inside a form is invalid HTML; a browser can reparent or discard the inner one, which would make Save/Dismiss submit the filter form instead of running their server action. Fixed by closing the filter form right after the sticky search bar and associating the facet rail's inputs (which now sit outside it, alongside the results table) via the HTML `form="opportunities-filters"` attribute instead of DOM nesting. Verified two ways: structurally (0 forms nested inside the filter form; a facet checkbox outside the form still reaches the URL on submit) and behaviorally — a real click on a real "Save" button against the disposable `scraplify_qa` database actually persisted the decision and it appeared on `/saved`, with the page URL never touching the filter form's GET action.
- **Bugfix, misleading sync claim (P1, caught by the per-commit gate).** The "synced X ago" kicker used the newest *start* time of any crawl run across every source, regardless of whether that run succeeded, is still running, or failed — so it could (and, checked against the live corpus, did) claim the whole corpus was fresh as of a time when only one source had even attempted a run. Fixed to use each source's last *confirmed-complete* full-coverage run, take the oldest across sources (the weakest link, not the newest), and show nothing at all when any source lacks one — which is honestly the current state, since jobs.ge has never completed a full-coverage run.
- **Bugfix, dead "Show more" anchor (P2, caught by the per-commit gate).** The redesign stopped giving each row a positional id, so `ShowMore`'s `#row-N` fragment matched nothing and every "show more" click landed back at the top instead of the newly revealed rows. Fixed by adding a `row-${position}` id to each row's first cell, alongside (not replacing) its existing `opp-<id>` id, which the detail screen's own back-link depends on.
- **Bugfix, focus hidden by opacity (P2, caught by the per-commit gate).** A row's Save/Dismiss controls were only revealed via `:hover`, so tabbing to them with a keyboard left the controls — and the focus ring itself — at 35% opacity. Fixed by adding a matching `focus-within` variant.
- **Bugfix, header overflow (found in this branch's own browser QA, not the gate).** The dev/env status badge showed from `sm` (640px) while the full desktop nav only appears from `lg` (1024px); in the ~1024–1279px band the two together overflowed the viewport. Fixed by deferring the badge to `xl` (1280px) — verified with zero overflow swept across 1000–1300px, and the exact breakpoint edge (hidden at 1279px, shown at 1280px) confirmed directly rather than assumed.
- **Bugfix, test fragility (found in this branch's own test run, not the gate).** Two `src/browse/queries.test.ts` cases (`firstSeenFrom`, `changedOnly`) queried the entire live corpus unscoped, capped at 500 rows; as the live hr.ge crawl keeps growing the corpus, enough real rows now match to push the test's own disposable row out of the cap — the same corpus-growth fragility this file already recorded once, in a second place. Fixed by scoping both queries (and a third, adjacent `deadlineTo` case with the identical latent flaw) to the test's own throwaway source via `sourceSlug`, matching this file's own established pattern elsewhere. 659/659 tests pass.

**Both remaining review gates waived by explicit project-owner decision (2026-09-15), rather than left owed.** The commit carrying the fixes above hit the same Codex usage-limit error this file has already recorded repeatedly during Phase 3B/3C (`ERROR: You've hit your usage limit … try again at 1:26 PM`) rather than a finding, and was committed with `--no-verify`. The whole-branch adversarial review CLAUDE.md's merge condition asks for was never run at all — the assisting session had no access to that review mechanism. Rather than wait on Codex's quota or find another way to run a whole-branch pass, the project owner chose to skip both and merge on the strength of what **did** run: the per-commit gate's first two real passes against this branch, which found and got fixed five genuine defects (two P1, two P2) plus surfaced the hero decision above; 659 tests; a real database write verified end to end, not just structurally; and full manual browser QA at all four required widths. Narrower than it looks — every substantive finding this branch produced was still fixed and verified, not waved through; what's missing is a second, independent pass that might have found more.

The condition, same shape as Phase 3B's: **the changed/added screens are verified in a real browser against the real corpus, at every required width, with no regressions.**

- [x] Header, footer, and the opportunities screen verified in a real browser at 390/768/1280/1920 — zero horizontal overflow, console clean, including the reduced-motion hero path.
- [x] No invented data on the redesigned homepage — the hero's stat line and the Database/Writes facts are read from the corpus/environment, not authored.
- [x] Georgian renders correctly on the redesigned screens (unchanged typeface; re-verified, not assumed, after the header/footer rewrite).
- [x] Every per-commit Codex finding resolved or explicitly decided — two P1s and two P2s fixed, one P2 (the hero) raised to and settled by the project owner rather than silently overridden either direction.
- [x] The one write path this redesign touches (Save/Dismiss) verified against a real database, not just structurally — see the nested-forms bugfix above.

- [x] **Whole-branch adversarial review — waived by explicit project-owner decision (2026-09-15), not run.** CLAUDE.md's merge condition asks for `codex review --base main` against the whole branch; the assisting session had no access to that mechanism, and rather than block on Codex's quota or find another path to run one, the project owner chose to merge on the strength of the per-commit gate's two real passes (five defects found and fixed) plus the manual verification recorded above. No review debt is silently carried — this is a decision, recorded as one, not a gap left unmarked.

### Phase 3E — landing hero (built and verified 2026-09-15)

UI-only redesign of the root landing page (`/`), independent of Phase 3D and of Phase 5 (CV upload/parsing, scoped separately the same day) — own branch, `phase-3e-landing-hero`, following the Phase 3D precedent of an out-of-sequence UI branch that does not move the numbered phase count. Replaces the page's current "design system" content (type-scale specimen, color swatches — already fully superseded by `e2e/design-system.spec.ts`, nothing orphaned by removing it) with a real, fully-data-wired motion hero, keeping the existing `<HeroVideo />` background by explicit project-owner instruction.

**Sourced from an external design handoff** (a prototype HTML file plus a written spec, outside this repo), explicitly high-fidelity on type/color/spacing/motion but not on data — the prototype's numbers and Georgian sample content are mock, and this project's own "never render invented data" rule applies in full: every number, title and employer in the built version must come from `src/browse/queries.ts` against the live corpus.

**Planned via this project's established plan-before-code sequence**, same as Phase 3C-2's Stage 7: a Plan-mode pass (Opus) produced a detailed implementation plan against the actual current code (not the handoff's own claims), then `codex exec --sandbox read-only` reviewed that plan independently before any code was written. That review returned **Request changes** — and correctly: its central finding was that the plan's "live proof" numbers were already stale against a corpus that had grown without dedupe running, which is the incident recorded above, not a defect in the design itself. Four further real findings, to be carried into implementation:

- **Ticker needs an interruption and provenance contract.** An auto-scrolling 38s-loop strip of real listing titles with no pause control and no route back to the source listing violates both WCAG's auto-updating-content expectation and this project's own "no data without a route back to its source" principle. Each ticker item and each "Newest" panel row must link to its opportunity; the ticker needs a pause/stop affordance, and the duplicated second copy of its item list needs `aria-hidden="true"`.
- **`toRow`'s `employers[0]` was the wrong read of an intentional convention.** `toRow` deliberately returns every distinct employer and every source a cluster carries; picking only the first discards real information other screens preserve. The panel/ticker rows must keep all employers (or state an explicit, narrower display rule) rather than silently narrowing an established shared helper.
- **Source and listing ordering needs to be made deterministic**, not assumed. Neither `getSourceHealth`'s row order nor `OpportunityView.members`' order is guaranteed by any query contract — the kicker's "jobs.ge + hr.ge" phrasing and any single-row title/employer/board pick both need an explicit, stated ordering rule rather than relying on today's incidental result order.
- **The reduced-motion CSS diagnosis (an unlayered `letter-spacing: normal` rule beating Tailwind's layered `tracking-*` utilities, confirmed real and already silently breaking `/opportunities`'s own kicker today) needs an automated e2e check**, not only a one-time manual QA pass — the risk of a future edit silently reopening it is exactly what this file's own Tailwind-cascade lesson already warns about elsewhere.

**Design-reference docs corrected as a precondition, not left as a STATUS.md-only footnote:** `product-context.md`, `design-direction.md` and `anti-patterns.md` (all under `.claude/skills/professional-frontend/references/`) previously stated flatly that this product has no hero and no landing page — literally true when written, false since Phase 3D's hero exception and now Phase 3E. All three now name `/` as the one explicit, deliberate exception, reasoned rather than silently overridden, with the rule otherwise left standing for every working screen. A first version of this section cited "Phase 3E" from those reference docs before this section existed at all — caught by the same Codex review that found the corpus-staleness issue, fixed by writing this section rather than by weakening the citation.

**All four review findings addressed in the build, not narrowed or deferred:**

- Ticker items and every "Newest listings" panel row are real `<a>` elements — the panel's title links to the opportunity's own `/opportunities/[id]` page, and a separate board pill per source (same convention `/opportunities`'s own `SourceLinks` already uses) links out to the real hr.ge/jobs.ge listing, `target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" translate="no"` with a proper `aria-label`. The ticker pauses on real pointer hover or keyboard focus (`group-hover`/`group-focus-within` → `animation-play-state: paused`, CSS only, no JS driving it) — verified directly, not just styled: dispatching a real hover on the ticker's stationary wrapper flips its track's computed `animationPlayState` from `running` to `paused`. The duplicated second copy of the item list (needed for the seamless -50% loop) renders as plain non-interactive markup under one `aria-hidden="true"` wrapper, not a second set of real, redundant Tab stops.
- Panel/ticker rows render `row.employers.join(' · ')` — every distinct employer `toRow` returns, matching `/opportunities`'s own established rendering, not a narrowed `employers[0]`.
- `getSourceHealth`'s result is sorted by `sourceSlug` before deriving the kicker's board list ("hr.ge + jobs.ge", alphabetical and now deterministic), since the underlying query itself carries no `ORDER BY` and this project's own convention is to fix what a screen actually depends on rather than assume incidental result order will hold.
- Two new automated e2e checks (`e2e/design-system.spec.ts`, now 10 tests total) directly exercise the cascade bug: one asserts a real Georgian panel title's computed `text-transform` is `none` and its font is Noto Sans Georgian; one asserts the kicker's tracked inner `<span>` has a non-`normal` computed `letter-spacing`, proving the "tracking goes on an inner span, not the `<p>`" pattern actually works rather than merely looking right. A third drives `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` end to end: zero `<video>` elements, the headline's masked inner spans at `opacity: 1` (not stuck invisible mid-delay), and the ticker's own computed `transform` unchanged across a 200ms wait (not silently vibrating at `0.01ms` × `infinite`).

**A fifth, unplanned finding — a real, pre-existing bug in the shared site header, unrelated to this screen's own content.** Browser-QA sweeping the required widths (via the iframe technique this project uses to get exact CSS pixel widths on a machine whose `devicePixelRatio` has caused false-clean readings before) found genuine horizontal overflow at exactly 1280px — one of the four required checkpoints — on **every** screen in the app, not just this one: `site-header-nav.tsx`'s desktop nav (8 links, since `phase-3c1-duplicate-review` and `phase-3c2-taxonomy` each added one) and its write-gate/env badge share one `xl` (1280px) breakpoint, on the claim both had independently been "verified to fit" there. Measured directly rather than trusted: the nav alone overflows by 37px at exactly 1280px, and together with the badge by 155px — the claim was never actually checked with both elements present at once. Fixed by moving the shared threshold to a verified-clear `min-[1500px]` (checked directly at 1280, 1499, 1500 and 1920 with zero overflow; the 1320-1440px range measured noisy/inconsistent under repeated concurrent test load rather than settling on one stable edge, so a round value with real margin was chosen over chasing an exact pixel). Fixed here, on this branch, because it blocked verifying this screen's own required-width gate honestly — recorded as its own, separate finding rather than folded silently into the hero's own changes.

**Corrected live-data expectations, post-dedupe-fix, and live-checked again after the build (see the incident above):** `/` reports 3,627 open vacancies / 2 boards merged / 3,705 listings tracked; `/opportunities?status=active` independently reports **3,627 matches** — the landing page's own stat and its destination's real count agree, verified by actually navigating both CTAs rather than assumed from the shared filter constant. The "Closing soon" CTA (`/opportunities?status=active&closing=7`) resolves to a real, different filtered result set (894 matches at the time of writing), not a dead or disabled button standing in for the design reference's undesigned "Get daily alerts" flow.

**Verified:** 736 unit/integration tests, 10/10 e2e tests (`npm run test:e2e` — `@playwright/test` itself was declared in `package.json` but missing from `node_modules`, a pre-existing environment gap fixed with a plain `npm install`, not a new dependency). Zero horizontal overflow, confirmed by direct `scrollWidth`/`clientWidth` measurement rather than eyeballed, at 390/768/1059/1060/1280/1360/1920 on both `/` and `/opportunities`. Zero console errors on a real, cache-busted navigation (a batch of errors seen mid-testing traced to this session's own iframe-teardown racing a live Turbopack hot-reload, confirmed as a test-harness artifact rather than a page defect by reproducing a clean run immediately after). No orphaned functionality — the type-scale/swatch content it replaces was already fully superseded by `e2e/design-system.spec.ts` before this phase began.

**A per-commit Codex review round found one real P1 and three real P2s in the build above — all fixed, not narrowed:**

- **[P1] The body copy made an unsupported continuous-monitoring claim.** "…checked continuously. When a listing changes or disappears, you know the same day" was carried over from the design reference's own copy without checking whether it's actually true for this deployment — it isn't: jobs.ge has no registered scheduled task (`Get-ScheduledTask` confirmed empty on this machine) and its own full walk takes ~7.9h, and hr.ge has no scheduler either, per the dedupe-incident section above. Rewritten to the claim this build actually makes good on: "Every vacancy from jobs.ge and hr.ge, deduplicated into one record — browse, filter and shortlist without checking two sites separately." Real, not invented, per this project's own correctness rule.
- **[P2] The ticker's pause had no touch-operable form.** `group-hover`/`group-focus-within` alone (the first build's implementation) give a mouse user a way to pause but nothing persistent for a touch-only visitor — tapping an item just navigates away, leaving the WCAG auto-updating-content requirement genuinely unmet on exactly the 390px checkpoint this gate requires testing. Fixed by extracting the ticker into its own client component, `web/components/hero-ticker.tsx` — the one deliberate client boundary on an otherwise fully server-rendered page — with a real `<button>` toggling React state, not just a CSS pseudo-class.
- **[P2] A keyboard user tabbing into the ticker could land focus on a link already scrolled outside the visible, clipped area**, since DOM tab order stays fixed while the strip visually translates and `group-focus-within`'s pause arrives one step after the link already received (invisible) focus. Fixed by the same state-driven pause: every real ticker link starts `tabIndex={-1}` and only becomes reachable once the strip is actually paused (via the button, or a mouse hover) and therefore actually stationary — verified directly, not assumed: a real click on the button flips the button to "Play"/`aria-pressed="true"`, the links' `tabIndex` to `0`, and the track's computed `animationPlayState` to `paused`, together.
- **[P2] The mobile popover menu didn't account for the header's own responsive height.** `site-header-nav.tsx`'s header is `lg:h-[78px]` (1024px+) but the popover was unconditionally `mt-[58px]`/`h-[calc(100vh-58px)]` — at 1024px and up (now 1024–1359px, widened by this same phase's nav-overflow fix below) its top sat 20px inside the header instead of below it. Fixed with matching `lg:mt-[78px] lg:h-[calc(100vh-78px)]` — verified directly at 1100px: popover top (78px) now equals the header's own bottom edge (78px) exactly.

**Two further, unplanned fixes, from direct user testing rather than Codex — real correctness issues, addressed the same way:**

- **Stale/outdated listings could appear in the panel and ticker.** `canonicalStatus` is a stored column, refreshed only when a dedupe pass revisits an opportunity — it can and does lag the real per-member state: a live check found 23 of the top 40 "newest active" candidates already had a stated deadline in the past despite `canonicalStatus` still reading `active`, exactly the class of listing a visitor clicking through would find the source site itself already reports as gone.
- **Nav simplified, hero clip re-timed, by explicit request.** `/` dropped from the shared `NAV` list (it's the landing page now, already reachable via the wordmark link, not a page in its own right) — down to 7 links, which let the header-overflow fix below move from a very conservative `min-[1500px]` down to a measured `min-[1360px]` (natural unclipped width ~1270px). `HeroVideo`'s `<video>` now sets `playbackRate = 0.7` (a 30% slowdown) via `onLoadedMetadata`, the only way to control video speed — no HTML attribute or CSS property does it.

**A third per-commit Codex round, against the fix for the stale-listing finding above, found one more real P1 and four more real P2s — the fix itself had two genuine bugs, and the earlier ticker-pause fix had two more:**

- **[P1] Still "Every vacancy"** — the rewritten body copy from the previous round dropped the false freshness claim but kept an equally unsupported coverage one: jobs.ge has never had a full-coverage run and tracks only 310 of its real ~5,647 listings. Rewritten again, this time without any completeness word at all: "Job vacancies from jobs.ge and hr.ge, deduplicated into one record — browse, filter and shortlist without checking two sites separately."
- **[P2] The stale-listing fix's own `limit: 40` was applied before its filter, not guaranteeing enough eligible rows.** A fixed-size fetch filtered afterward only guarantees the eligible rows *within that window* — with roughly half of any given window failing the filter on the live corpus, this could under-fill the panel/ticker or skip a genuinely eligible older opportunity even when one exists just past the cutoff. Fixed with `fetchEligibleOpportunities` (`web/app/page.tsx`): re-queries with an escalating `limit` (40 → 120 → 500, `searchOpportunities`'s own ceiling) until enough eligible candidates are found, rather than one fixed-size guess.
- **[P2] Status and deadline were checked as independent aggregates across all of an opportunity's members, not together on the same one.** `row.sources.some(active)` and `row.deadline` (the LATEST deadline across every member) could each be satisfied by a *different* board — an active member with its own past deadline plus an unrelated closed member with a future one would have wrongly passed; an active member with no stated deadline plus a closed member with a past one would have wrongly failed. Fixed with `hasGenuinelyOpenMember`, checking `status === 'active'` and that same member's own deadline together, per member, directly against `OpportunityView.members` rather than through `toRow`'s aggregated fields.
- **[P2] A cross-posted opportunity with one board inactive rendered every source pill identically and still linked to the dead one.** The filter above only requires *one* member to be genuinely open — a real, correct requirement, but the panel's board pills gave no indication when a *different* member than the reader is about to click was the open one. Fixed by mirroring `/opportunities`'s own `SourceLinks` convention exactly: a pill whose own `status !== 'active'` appends the short status label and carries the full explanation as a tooltip, rather than looking identical to a genuinely open one.
- **[P2, in the earlier ticker-pause fix] Clicking "Play" while the pointer or keyboard focus was still on the button didn't actually resume anything.** The first pause fix kept `group-hover`/`group-focus-within` CSS pausing alongside the new button-driven state — clicking Play set the state to "not paused," but the mouse or focus, still on the button inside the same `.group`, kept the CSS rule forcing `animation-play-state: paused` regardless. Fixed by dropping the CSS pause entirely; the button's own state is now the one source of truth. Verified directly: a real click on Play, with the pointer left resting on the button exactly as Codex's scenario describes, now reads `animationPlayState: "running"`.
- **[P2, in the earlier ticker-pause fix] Pausing made every real link tabbable even though most were still off-screen inside the clip.** At 390px, several real titles are themselves wider than the whole ticker strip, so a strict "fully unclipped" visibility rule (the first attempt) could leave zero links tabbable depending on where the animation happened to stop — found by testing the fix, not assumed correct. Replaced with an intersection-ratio measurement (`web/components/hero-ticker.tsx`): a link counts as visible once at least 90% of whichever is smaller — its own width or the wrapper's — is actually within the clipped strip, measured via `requestAnimationFrame` after the pause state change (so it reads the settled, post-paint position, not a stale pre-pause transform). Verified live at 390px: exactly the links genuinely ~99% within the visible strip become tabbable; the rest, including one measured at literally 2px past the edge under the old strict rule, correctly stay out of the tab order.

**A fourth per-commit Codex round, against the fixes for the third round above, found one more real P1 and four more real P2s — the "genuinely open" logic itself had real gaps, both in what it computed and in where it computed it:**

- **[P1] The stat and the panel/ticker used two different definitions of "open."** The stat (and its CTA's destination, `/opportunities?status=active`) still counted by `canonicalStatus`, while the panel/ticker had moved to the real per-member check — against the live corpus, 3,627 vs the true 3,498, a real ~3.5% overstatement of the headline number. Rather than patch around this a third time, the per-member check was moved into the query layer itself: `src/browse/queries.ts` gained a `genuinelyOpenAsOf` filter on `SearchOpportunitiesFilters` (and its `countOpportunities` sibling, since both share `opportunityConditions`) — one `liveMemberExists` clause checking `status = 'active'` and that same member's own deadline together, mirroring the exact pattern the file's own `deadlineFrom`/`deadlineTo` handling already uses (and the exact comment there warning against two independent `EXISTS` clauses is what this bug in the earlier client-side version had walked straight into). The stat and the panel/ticker now both call this one filter — the same predicate, not two call sites that happen to agree. Six new tests in `src/browse/queries-filters.test.ts`, including a direct regression case for the different-member bug and a count/search parity check.
- **[P2] The escalating-fetch loop's final iteration could discard eligible rows it had already found.** Fixed alongside the P1 above by removing the loop entirely — with `genuinelyOpenAsOf` filtering in SQL before `LIMIT`, one `searchOpportunities` call returns exactly the true eligible rows up to the limit, with no risk of an under-filled window or a silently-dropped fallback.
- **[P2] A cross-posted pair with one member `active`-but-past-its-own-deadline still showed that pill as open.** The panel's inactive-pill check (added for the previous round's finding) compared `source.status !== 'active'` — true for a `closed`/`expired` member, but blind to an `active` member whose own deadline has simply passed without reconciliation catching up yet, which the real corpus contains genuine examples of. Fixed with a proper per-source `openSourceSlugs` set, computed in `web/app/page.tsx` from the same live status-and-deadline check the query filter uses, passed alongside each panel row; a pill now shows "deadline passed" specifically for that case rather than either silently passing it as open or mislabeling it with its own (accurate but confusing) `active` status enum.
- **[P2] Reduced-motion visitors could never reach a real ticker link by keyboard.** `globals.css`'s own reduced-motion rule already freezes the ticker without this component's `paused` state ever becoming `true`, so every real link stayed `tabIndex={-1}` permanently and the button sat there still reading "Pause" over already-stationary content. Fixed in `web/components/hero-ticker.tsx` by tracking `prefers-reduced-motion` directly (mirroring `hero-video.tsx`'s own `matchMedia` pattern) and folding it into one `effectivePaused` flag; the button itself is hidden under reduced motion, since a toggle with no effect is its own kind of misleading. The e2e reduced-motion test was strengthened to assert this directly (no Pause button rendered, at least one real link genuinely `tabIndex="0"`) rather than only checking the strip visually stops moving, which the bug would have passed regardless.
- **[P2] The paused-link visibility measurement never reran on resize.** A viewport resize or device rotation while paused changes which links actually intersect the clip, but the effect computing `visibleIds` depended only on `paused` — fixed with a `ResizeObserver` on the wrapper, re-running the same measurement on any size change while paused, not just once when pausing begins. Verified live: 4 tabbable links at 1280px dropped to exactly 1 after resizing to 500px, matching a fresh direct measurement at that width.

**Re-verified after all four rounds:** 742 unit tests (six new, for the query-layer filter), 10/10 e2e tests, zero console errors on fresh navigation, zero horizontal overflow at every required width and boundary, the corrected stat independently re-checked directly against the database (3,498, exact match), and the corrected panel rows re-checked against the live database — zero of the 14 currently-shown opportunities fail the per-member genuinely-open check.

**Exit gate (same shape as Phase 3D's), met:**

- [x] The redesigned hero verified in a real browser against the real, post-dedupe corpus, at 390/768/1280/1920 plus the 1059/1060 column-boundary this design introduces and the 1359/1360 nav boundary — zero horizontal overflow, console clean.
- [x] All four original Codex design-review findings resolved in the build, plus four from a first per-commit round, five from a second, and five from a third (unsupported copy corrected twice, the "open" definition unified into one query-layer filter shared by the stat and the panel/ticker, per-member status/deadline matching moved into SQL, inactive-source pill disclosure corrected to cover a deadline-only staleness case, Play-doesn't-resume, paused-but-invisible tab stops, and reduced-motion visitors permanently locked out of the ticker's real links) — see all four lists above.
- [x] Zero invented data — every number, title and employer comes from `src/browse/queries.ts` against the live corpus; the stat, the panel/ticker, and the query layer's own filter all now share one definition of "open," independently re-verified against the database; no listing shown that the source site itself already reports as gone, checked per-member.
- [x] Correct Georgian handling — no uppercase or letter-spacing near real titles/employers, single typeface (Noto Sans Georgian), verified by two automated e2e checks, not only manual inspection.
- [x] Full reduced-motion coverage, automated: no video, headline fully visible (not stuck mid-delay), ticker and pulse genuinely stationary, ticker links genuinely keyboard-reachable (not a `0.01ms` strobe, and not permanently unreachable either).
- [x] No orphaned functionality from the page it replaces.
- [x] A real, pre-existing header-overflow bug found during this gate's own required-width sweep, fixed rather than worked around or left for later — and re-verified after the nav simplification that followed it.
- [x] The ticker's pause control is genuinely keyboard- and touch-operable, resumes when activated regardless of pointer/focus position, correctly reflects reduced motion, and re-measures on resize — verified by real clicks, real geometry measurement, and a real resize, not assumed from the code.

**OWED: a Codex review of the commit landing all four rounds above.** The fifth attempt to commit this work hit a genuine Codex usage-limit error (`try again at Sep 16th, 2026 12:20 AM`) — real application code (`src/browse/queries.ts`, `web/app/page.tsx`, `web/components/hero-ticker.tsx` and the rest of this phase's changes), so per this project's own convention it is carried as owed debt, not waived. Committed `--no-verify` by explicit project-owner decision, on the same evidentiary bar every prior owed-review commit in this file has used: typecheck, lint, 742 unit tests and 10/10 e2e tests all green, every fix in all four rounds verified live against the running app and the real database (not just structurally), not merely claimed.

**Also found and fixed while this commit was blocked: the self-managing cooldown mechanism itself (`.githooks/pre-commit`, built earlier this session) had a real gap.** Its regex only matched Codex's bare-time reset message ("try again at 1:26 PM"); the message this outage actually produced includes a full date when the reset crosses midnight ("try again at Sep 16th, 2026 12:20 AM"), which silently failed to match and left the cooldown unrecorded — precisely the "out of the blue" rediscovery this mechanism exists to prevent. Fixed to try the date-qualified form first (GNU `date` rejects the ordinal day suffix "16th" outright, confirmed by testing both directly, so it is stripped before parsing), falling back to the original bare-time handling. Verified against both real and synthetic messages of each shape before retrying the blocked commit, which then correctly recorded a cooldown until 00:20 the same run.

Rather than wait, the project owner directed both this and the per-commit debt above be skipped and the branch merged now — "skip it merge," the same decision already made for PR #9 and PR #10 earlier this same day. Narrower than it looks: every per-commit gate pass before the final commit ran cleanly, and all four rounds of real findings this phase produced (design review plus three per-commit rounds) were fixed and verified live, not waved through — what's missing is the cross-commit view a whole-branch pass would add, and the one commit's own review.

Both commits (`aa3836d`, `677a68d`) landed while Codex's usage-limit cooldown (recorded above, until 00:20) was still active, so the per-commit hook's own self-managing cooldown check skipped both reviews automatically rather than attempting a call already known to fail — **OWED**, same convention as above, since both touch real application code (`web/app/page.tsx`, `web/components/hero-video.tsx`).

### Phase 5 — CV parsing, stage plan (written 2026-09-15, before any code)

Phase 5A (`src/ranking/`, below) built the scoring/ranking engine but deliberately stopped at "profiles are reviewed JSON, entered through the CLI" — this stage closes that gap: a real PDF/DOCX CV, uploaded in the browser, becomes a draft candidate profile a person corrects before ranking, per §17.1. Planned the same way Phase 3C-2's Stage 7 and Phase 3E were: a Plan-mode pass against the actual current code (not assumptions), two explicit decisions confirmed with the project owner before writing this plan, and a `codex exec --sandbox read-only` review of this section intended before any code lands (Codex's usage-limit cooldown, recorded above, gates when that review can actually run).

**Decision 1 — extraction calls Claude (Anthropic API), not a heuristic/dictionary extractor.** Chosen for accuracy against messy real CVs (mixed Georgian/English, inconsistent formats) over a no-external-call heuristic approach.
