    - **`searchListings` gained a tie-breaker before anything paged it.** It ordered by `firstSeenAt` alone, which is the defect Stage 5 fixed for opportunities. Not live here — 411 of 412 first-seen values are distinct, because listings are inserted one at a time — but it stops being latent the first time a crawl stamps one run timestamp across a batch, and this screen pages the query.
    - **The state select is disabled inside a view that already fixes the state**, and the URL drops the status when a view overrides it. Otherwise someone could pick "held back" while the "may be gone" view was active and one of the two would silently win, or share a link describing a filter that is not applied.
    - **Browser QA found one defect the source could not show:** at 375px the page scrolled sideways, 41px past the viewport, because a `<select>` sizes to its widest option and ignores its flex container. Every control is capped and every label given `min-w-0`. Also verified: no raw enum on screen, no uppercase transform anywhere near Georgian, external links carry `rel="noreferrer"` and an "opens in a new tab" name, the active view carries `aria-current`, and 25 rows fit a 900px screen.
    - **The state options are derived from `labels.ts`, not written out.** A second hand-maintained copy of the §13 vocabulary is how a state ends up spelled two ways; the map is typed against the Drizzle enum, so a new state is a typecheck failure rather than a silently missing option.
    - **The stranded test sources are visible here too**, as UUID-bearing slugs in the board select — `sourceLabel` falls back to the raw slug for a source it has no label for, which is the right behaviour for a genuinely new board and the wrong-looking result for test debris. Left alone deliberately: changing the fallback to hide unknown sources would paper over stranded data with a UI change.
    - **21 new tests** — 15 over the URL state, including every rejected input and the both-bounds window, and 6 over the query layer, one of which pages a deliberately tied result set to prove the ordering cannot duplicate a row.
  - **Stage 8 done (2026-09-08): ranked results.** `web/app/ranked/`, `web/lib/ranked-row.ts`, plus a widened `listRankedOpportunities` and a new `countRankedOpportunities`. The feature the product exists for, and the screen whose job is the **why** rather than the number: §17.2 chose a deterministic scorer precisely so a rank could be explained, and a list of scores with no visible reasoning throws that away.
    - **Every row shows what the scorer recorded, ordered by contribution.** Four weighted factors — skills 0.45, role 0.35, preferred field 0.15, languages 0.05 — each with the terms that matched and the ones the listing asked for that the profile does not claim, verbatim and in Georgian. Ordered by `score × weight` rather than raw score, because that is what actually moved the rank: a factor scoring 1.00 at weight 0.05 is nearly irrelevant, and leading with the raw number invites exactly that misreading.
    - **The screen was empty when it was first built, and the cause was not a bug.** All 502 stored rankings were pinned to canonical revisions a later dedupe pass had superseded, and `listRankedOpportunities` refuses those by design — a row pinned to a superseded revision described different content. The fix was operational: re-running `npm run rank` took the current-revision match from **0 to 407** (399 eligible, 8 hard-filtered) and left the 502 historical rows intact, as §17.2 requires. The empty state now names this cause specifically, because it will recur every time dedupe runs after a ranking.
    - **Hard-filtered results were ranked 1-8, above every real match.** A filtered opportunity has a null score — §17.2 treats filtering as a stage before scoring, not a score of zero — and **Postgres orders `DESC` with NULLS FIRST by default**, so asking to include them put all eight at the top of a numbered list. The screen was presenting "your best result" as something the scorer had rejected outright. Found in browser QA; fixed with `nulls last`; **mutation-checked** — reverting it flips the new regression test.
    - **`listRankedOpportunities` had no tests at all**, nor did `runRanking`; only the pure scorer was covered. It now has nine, including the superseded-revision guard whose silent failure is what made the screen blank, the profile-version and evaluation-version guards, and the count agreeing with the list. The two are built from one shared condition list so they cannot describe different sets.
    - **The 200-row ceiling is gone.** `Math.min(limit ?? 25, 200)` would have hidden roughly 60% of 399 eligible results with no way past it — the defect class rejected twice during Stage 5. The limit now clamps to the query layer's own maximum, and the screen stops at it deliberately with a sentence saying so, because a ranking is a shortlist and page 40 of one is not a question anyone asks.
    - **Component ties are broken by weight, not by array order.** Skills at 0.33 × 0.45 and preferred field at 1.00 × 0.15 contribute **exactly** 0.15 — verified as IEEE-754 equal, not approximately — and that pair occurs throughout the corpus. Left to the sort alone the display order fell back to whatever order the scorer wrote its array in, an implementation detail presented as a ranking of importance.
    - **A list rather than a table**, deliberately departing from the two browse screens. Those are scanned thirty rows at a time; this is read a few rows at a time to decide whether a match is real, and the reasoning does not fit a table cell without being reduced to the number the screen exists to explain.
    - **Adding two nav links broke every screen on a phone.** Five links measured 401px against a 375px viewport, so the whole app scrolled sideways — not just the screen being worked on. The nav wraps now. Recorded because the lesson generalises: a shared component gets tested on the page it was changed for.
    - **20 new tests**, and one existing test corrected. `web/lib/ranked-row.test.ts` runs against payloads copied from the live `rankings` table rather than composed ones, including malformed-jsonb cases, since one bad row must not take out the list.
  - **Two tie-breaker tests were found not to discriminate** (2026-09-08). Mutation-checking the ones added in Stages 7 and 8 showed both stay green with the tie-breaker removed: Postgres returns a small heap scan in a stable order, so the nondeterminism cannot be provoked at test size — it depends on plan choice and physical row order, neither of which a test controls. Both comments now say so outright rather than letting a green test imply a guarantee it never established. The tie-breakers stay, because the guarantee is structural: an ORDER BY that is not total permits any order among ties, and LIMIT/OFFSET over that duplicates and drops rows. Recorded because a test that quietly proves less than its name claims is worse than no test.
  - **Stage 9 done (2026-09-08): the shortlist, and the first writes this app performs.** `web/app/saved/`, `web/components/decision-control.tsx`, `web/lib/decision-input.ts`, and `src/shortlist/decisions.ts` on a new `opportunity_decisions` table (migration 0018). Everything before this was a read.
    - **One table with two states, not two tables.** Saved and dismissed are the same statement with opposite signs and are mutually exclusive — saving something already dismissed is changing your mind, not holding both views. Two tables would let that contradiction exist and would need a rule about which wins.
    - **Deliberately mutable, unlike `opportunity_source_memberships`.** §12.5 makes cluster membership append-only because a dedupe decision is evidence later work depends on. A person changing their mind about a job owes nobody an audit trail, and keeping every toggle would turn a shortlist into a diary. The one exception is `first_decided_at`, kept because it is the only history a mutable row cannot reconstruct: "I dismissed this weeks ago and it keeps surfacing" is a complaint about the corpus, and needs the original date to be true.
    - **`on conflict` rather than read-then-write.** A form plus an impatient second click races between a SELECT and an INSERT and fails on the unique index; the upsert makes the second submit an update, which is also what pressing twice meant. Tested with two genuinely concurrent submits.
    - **The write gate was exercised for real, on both instances.** The QA instance against `scraplify_qa` wrote a decision with a Georgian note, changed it, and cleared it — and the live database ended with **zero** rows throughout, checked directly after each step. The read-only instance renders **no submit buttons at all**: `writesEnabled()` is false, so the control shows the current decision as text and says the instance cannot change it. A row of dead buttons invites clicking and explains nothing.
    - **The disposable database exists now**, which it did not before this stage: `scraplify_qa`, seeded by `pg_dump | psql` from the live corpus (408 opportunities, 412 listings, real Georgian content). `.env.qa.example` is committed — `.gitignore` needed a `!` exception, since `.env.*` was swallowing it — and is the only written record of how the copy is made.
    - **Decisions are made from the detail screen**, not the list, because that is the only screen with enough context to make one: the boards compared, both descriptions, and what the grouping rests on. The shortlist screen itself can also change a decision, since an undo nobody can find is not an undo.
    - **Dismissals are listed rather than hidden.** They are reversible, and "why does this keep coming back" is only answerable if the dismissal and its date are visible.
    - **19 new tests.** Ten over the write operations — including the double submit, the concurrent submit, and the difference between "no note supplied" and "clear the note" — and nine over form input, where the note is cut by **grapheme**: `georgian-typography.md` rule 7 forbids index-based truncation of this corpus, and the same defect in the search box was a P1 during Stage 5.
    - **Not done here, deliberately:** the browse screens do not yet show or set a decision per row, and dismissed opportunities are not hidden from them. `decisionsByOpportunity` and `dismissedOpportunityIds` exist for exactly that and are tested; wiring them into a 406-row table is a density question that deserves its own pass rather than being bolted onto the stage that introduced writing.
  - **Stage 10 groundwork done (2026-09-08): both blockers on duplicate review are cleared.** No screen yet — this is the backend work that had to happen before one could exist, and it is committed separately so the screen is not the thing that carries a migration.
    - **`duplicate_candidates.evidence` exists** (migration 0019), nullable rather than defaulted. A default of `{}` would make "written before this column existed" indistinguishable from "the scorer found nothing", and a reviewer has to be able to tell which.
    - **Both insert sites write it, and the backfill reached everything.** Writing only in `values` would have left the entire backlog empty, since every candidate row predates the column. Measured after a re-run: **16 of 16 rows carry evidence, including all 12 `needs_review` pairs** — the ones that had nothing. The stored reasons are genuinely explanatory rather than a score dump: *"same organization and closely matching titles, but no vacancy-level application value"*, *"§14.2 forbids auto-linking on employer and title agreement alone"*.
    - **The two insert sites record different KINDS of reason**, and the distinction matters to a reviewer: one is a pair proposed by fresh evidence, the other is an existing link that no longer scores as one. The second says so in its own words rather than looking identical to the first.
    - **`acceptDuplicateCandidate` makes the composite verb atomic.** Accepting a pair is a membership change *plus* a resolution, and it was two public calls each opening its own transaction — so a crash between them left the merge applied and the pair still pending, and the next dedupe pass re-queued a decision the reviewer had already made, with the cluster changed underneath them. `reassignListing`'s body was extracted so both halves share one transaction.
    - **The atomicity test was wrong first, and mutation-checking caught it.** It used a missing target opportunity, which fails *before* any write — so two separate transactions roll back exactly as one does, and the test passed against the defect. Rewritten to fail *between* the halves (a valid move, a missing candidate id), it now fails when the transaction is removed. **That is the second test this week that proved nothing until it was mutation-checked**, after the two tie-breaker tests in Stages 7 and 8.
    - **`listReviewQueue` returns the evidence**, which is the half that reaches a screen. Still nullable, for the reason the column is.
  - **The whole-branch review ran on 2026-09-08 and found nine real defects — four P1s.** `codex review --base main` over all 19 commits (80 files, 17,138 lines), once credits returned. **This is the pass the eight owed reviews were waiting for**, and it justified the wait: everything below was invisible to the per-commit gate, the test suite and the browser QA that had already passed on each piece.
    - **P1 — the front page was rendering invented metrics, and had been since Stage 2.** `/` is the design specimen, linked in the nav as **"Overview"**, and its "Numerals" section printed hard-coded triples: `2026-09-13  0.8241  310`. A date, a score, a count — shaped exactly like a listing and a ranking, on the product's front door. Fabricating data is the failure `anti-patterns.md` puts first and `AGENTS.md` classes P1, and scaffolding is not an exemption. **Every browser-QA pass this phase missed it because none of them opened `/`** — the gate was always run against the screen being built. The numerals now come from the corpus, and the nav calls the page "Design system", which is what it is.
    - **P1 — the QA profile could point at the live corpus.** `scripts/next.mjs` overlaid `.env.qa` and opened the write gate without checking that the target differed from `.env`. An `.env.qa` that merely omitted `DATABASE_URL` inherited the live database and then enabled writes on it — the exact accident the profile exists to prevent, reached by editing the file that prevents it, while the launcher's own comment claimed it could not happen. It now fails closed, comparing host, port and database name rather than the raw string so that `localhost` and `127.0.0.1` are not mistaken for different databases.
    - **P1 — `acceptDuplicateCandidate` trusted its arguments.** Written hours earlier as the fix for a *different* defect, it took a candidate id, a listing id and a target with nothing tying them together: a stale form could accept candidate X while moving an unrelated listing, leaving the cluster and the candidate row describing different facts. A caller could also pass `decision: 'distinct'` and merge two listings while recording that they are different. It now loads the candidate `for update` first, requires it to be awaiting review, verifies the moved listing is one side of that pair and the target holds the other, and fixes the decision to `confirmed_same` rather than accepting one.
    - **P1 — accepting a stale link recorded no reviewer at all.** When both listings are already in the target, `reassignListingWithin` short-circuits, restores the previous *automatic* membership and returns. So the reviewer's evidence, identity and timestamp were never written, while the candidate said `human` — and the detail screen, which reads membership evidence, showed the ruleset's original reasoning as though nobody had looked. The reviewer's record is now written unconditionally.
    - **P2 — both deadline bounds could be satisfied by different members.** Two independent `EXISTS` clauses, so a cross-posted opportunity with deadlines either side of a window matched a query for that window although neither board says it closes then. One correlated predicate now carries both bounds.
    - **P2 — the ranked "show more" link dropped the selected profile**, so growing the list silently swapped which profile you were reading, under an unchanged heading. **P2 — the list screen still rendered deadlines as relative time**, counting down within the very day jobs.ge named as the deadline; the detail screen had been fixed for this and the list kept the old rendering. **P2 — the shortlist had no scoped loading or error state**, alone among the database-backed screens. **P2 — this file's own headline still said Stage 9 and still described the evidence column as missing**, in the same patch that added it.
    - **What this says about the process.** Five of the nine are cross-cutting or older than the commit that exposed them, which is exactly what a whole-branch pass is for and exactly what the per-commit gate cannot see. Two of the four P1s were in code written the same day, one of them in a function added *as a fix*. The fabricated-metrics one is the most instructive: it survived a whole phase because the browser gate was interpreted as "check the screen you built" rather than "check the app".
  - **The review of the response commit (2026-09-14) found two more P1s, and both were in the fixes themselves.** `codex review --commit 764a98b` over the nine-defect response. This is the third time on this branch that reviewing a fix has been worth as much as reviewing a feature, and the first where the fix was strictly worse than a no-op in one respect.
    - **P1 — the QA write guard still let an equivalent live URL through.** The 2026-09-08 fix compared host, port and database *path*, and treated a URL with no path as its own distinct identity. But an omitted path does not mean "no database": node-postgres falls back to the **user name**, so `postgres://scraplify@host` opens the live `scraplify` database while comparing as `127.0.0.1:5432` against the live `127.0.0.1:5432/scraplify` — different strings, same database, write gate open on the live corpus. Codex verified the defaulting against this machine's Postgres rather than asserting it. The guard now **fails closed on both sides**: a connection string that does not name its database is rejected outright, and if `.env`'s own URL does not name one either, the profile refuses to start rather than treating "cannot prove they differ" as "they differ". Re-checked against four cases — the pathless attack and a bare `/` are both rejected, the original `localhost`-versus-`127.0.0.1` equivalence still catches a same-database copy, and a genuine `scraplify_qa` still starts.
    - **P1 — the reviewer-record fix destroyed the ruleset's decision.** To stop a human reaffirmation going unrecorded, the previous fix UPDATEd the live membership row's decision, evidence, decider and timestamp. `opportunity_source_memberships` is **append-only** (§12.5), and its schema comment says in as many words that an in-place edit would destroy "the previous decision, its evidence, and who made it" — so the fix traded an invisible reviewer for an erased automatic decision, and `getMembershipHistory` lost the row that explained why the cluster existed. Now the stale-link path **retires the automatic membership and appends a human one**, leaving both in the trail; and the write happens only in that path, since a listing that genuinely moved already gets the reviewer's evidence from the insert in `reassignListingWithin`.
    - **The existing test passed against the defect, which is the fourth such case on this branch.** `records the reviewer even when both listings are already clustered` asserted that the live membership was the human one — true before and after — and never that the ruleset's row survived. It now asserts two rows with the retired one still carrying `decidedBy: 'ruleset'` and its original evidence and timestamp. **Mutation-checked:** restoring the in-place update fails exactly this test (`expected [ … ] to have a length of 2 but got 1`) and nothing else.
    - **P2 — this file contradicted itself about merge readiness**, recording the eight owed reviews as satisfied in one place and as blocking in another. Fixed by the banner below.
  - **The commit gate then rejected the first draft of the re-scope itself, with three P2s — all of them false claims in this file, not defects in code (2026-09-14).** Worth recording because the pattern is now consistent: the code passed and the *description of* the code did not.
    - **The browser gate was claimed as met at "375px and desktop width"**, when `browser-qa.md` §2 names four widths — 390, 768, 1280, 1920. Re-run properly across all seven routes at all four: 28 combinations, zero document overflow.
    - **"No raw enum or UUID presented as a label" was checked, and was false** — the board filter shows the stranded `test-source-<uuid>` slugs, which the very next paragraph of the same draft described. Now unchecked, with the cleanup named as the owner's decision.
    - **The shared-query-layer claim was wrong about where ranked results come from** (`src/ranking/run-ranking.ts`, not `src/browse/queries.ts`), and contradicted its own stated exception two paragraphs later.
  - **WAIVED (ninth), by explicit user decision (2026-09-14): the Codex review of the commit that fixed those three P2s.** Committed with `--no-verify` on the same decision ("commit next round without codex gate"), after the gate had already run on this round's substantive changes and cleared them with no P0/P1 — the three P2s above came from that same pass, and this commit is the answer to them. **Waived rather than owed, so it does not gate the merge**, which makes it different in kind from the eight before it: those were carried as debt and eventually paid, this one is a decision recorded as taken. The grounds are that the commit contains **no code** — `docs/STATUS.md` claims plus this entry, verified by `git diff --stat` showing one file. The grounds are not that it is small: the last three rounds each found real defects in something that looked like a tidy-up, and two of the four P1s on this branch were in code written as fixes. Recorded here rather than left implicit so the third waiver on this project is as visible as the first two.
  - **ALL EIGHT OWED REVIEWS ARE DISCHARGED (2026-09-08), and the entries below are history, not an open gate.** They are kept because they record how the debt built up and what was merged unreviewed, but every "**N owed reviews now gate this branch's merge**" sentence below is a statement of what was true *when it was written*. The whole-branch `codex review --base main` pass covered all eight commits at once, and its own response commit was then reviewed as well (see the review of `764a98b` below). **Nothing in the list below still gates this branch.** This banner exists because the eighth review itself flagged the contradiction as a P2: the file recorded the reviews as satisfied in one place and as blocking in another, so its merge-readiness could be read either way depending on where you looked — and a status file that answers "can this merge?" two different ways is worse than one that says no.
  - **OWED (eighth): a Codex review of the Stage 10 groundwork commit.** Same cause; **eight owed reviews now gate this branch's merge.** The second covering a migration, and the first covering a change to the dedupe write path — `run-dedupe.ts` now writes evidence at both candidate-insert sites, and a mistake there is the kind that corrupts a backlog quietly rather than failing. Also unreviewed: the `reassignListing` body extraction, which every existing caller now reaches through a thin wrapper.

  - **OWED (seventh): a Codex review of the Stage 9 commit.** Codex was still out of credits, so the hook failed rather than reporting a finding and the commit landed with `--no-verify` by explicit user decision. **Seven owed reviews now gate this branch's merge.** *(An eighth followed — see above.)*

    **This is the first owed review covering a migration and a write path**, which makes it different in kind from the six before it. Those were read-only screens where the worst outcome was a wrong number on a page; this one adds a table, an upsert, and three server actions that mutate. The write gate and the disposable database were exercised for real — the live database was checked directly after every write and held zero rows throughout — but "I tested it and it behaved" is exactly the evidence a review exists to go behind.

    Specifically unreviewed: migration 0018 and the `opportunity_decisions` schema, the `on conflict` upsert and its `first_decided_at` preservation, the three server actions and the ordering of `assertWritesEnabled()` within them, and the `.gitignore` exception that makes `.env.qa.example` trackable.
  - **OWED (sixth): a Codex review of the Stage 8 commit.** Codex remained out of credits, so the hook failed rather than reporting a finding and the commit landed with `--no-verify` by explicit user decision. **Six owed reviews now gate this branch's merge.** *(A seventh followed — see above.)*

    What is unreviewed here is more than a screen: `listRankedOpportunities` was restructured — its condition list extracted and shared with a new `countRankedOpportunities`, its ordering changed twice, its limit ceiling replaced — and that function had **no test coverage at all** before this commit. The nine tests added with it are the only thing standing behind the refactor, and they were written by the same pass that wrote the code, which is exactly the arrangement a review exists to check.

    Mitigating: two of this stage's defects were caught by its own tests and browser QA rather than by review (the NULLS FIRST ordering, the component tie order), and the NULLS-LAST fix is mutation-checked. That is evidence the tests bite, not evidence the diff is clean.
  - **OWED (fifth): a Codex review of the Stage 7 commit.** Codex was out of credits for the whole working session on 2026-09-08 (limit hit at 18:47, reset 21:58), so the hook failed rather than reporting a finding, and the commit landed with `--no-verify` by explicit user decision — "codex is down, we need to move forward without it". **Five owed reviews now gate this branch's merge.** *(A sixth followed — see above.)*

    **This is the weakest-reviewed commit on the branch, and by a wide margin.** Stage 6 at least had five completed rounds before its last four fixes went unreviewed; this one has had **zero**. It adds a query filter (`changedOnly`), an ordering change to `searchListings` that every caller shares, a new URL-state module, and a new screen — the kind of surface that has repaid review every time it has been looked at on this branch. Local verification was green (608 tests, lint, format, both typechecks) and every view was exercised in a real browser against live data, which is the same combination that was green while twenty-two real findings were outstanding during Stage 6.

    Specifically unreviewed: the `HAS_BEEN_REVISED` subquery, the tie-breaker added to an ordering the opportunities CLI also pages, the view-versus-select precedence in `listing-params.ts`, and the whole of `web/app/listings/`.
  - **OWED (fourth): a Codex review of the Stage 6 commit.** Same cause a fourth time — Codex hit its usage limit partway through the pre-commit review on 2026-09-08 at 18:47 (reset 21:58), so the hook failed rather than reporting a finding, and the commit landed with `--no-verify` by explicit user decision. **Four owed reviews now gate this branch's merge.** *(A fifth was added the same day — see above.)*

    This one is the least like the other three, and in both directions. In its favour: **five gate rounds did complete on earlier versions of this exact diff, finding twenty-two issues, every one of which was real and is fixed here** — so the code has been reviewed harder than anything else on the branch, and what is unreviewed is only the fifth round's four fixes. Against it: those rounds establish that this diff has a poor record of being clean when nobody has looked. Rounds three, four and five each found P1s in code that had already survived a round, and the fifth round's findings were not variations on the fourth's — they were new classes. Diminishing returns were not in evidence when the credits ran out.

    Specifically unreviewed: the fabricated-takedown wording, the audit metadata now carried on retired memberships, the `grouped` predicate replacing `crossPosted` as the gate on grouping evidence, and the corrected 404 copy. Local verification was green (591 tests, lint, format, both typechecks) and every change was checked in a real browser against live data — which is the same combination that was green while five rounds of real findings were still outstanding, so it is weak evidence on its own.
  - **OWED (third): a Codex review of the P2 clean-up commit.** Same cause again — Codex was still out of credits at 19:16 on 2026-09-07 (reset 21:47), so the hook failed rather than reporting a finding, and the commit landed with `--no-verify` by explicit user decision. **Three owed reviews now gate this branch's merge.** *(A fourth was added on 2026-09-08 — see above.)* This one is the smallest of the three: a one-line SQL change with a mutation-checked test, one token swap verified at 6.27:1 in the browser, and two documentation corrections. Every finding it acts on was itself raised by Codex, so the changes are responses to review rather than unreviewed invention — but the responses have not been checked.
  - **OWED (second): a Codex review of the exhaustive-list commit.** Same cause as Stage 1's — Codex ran out of credits partway through the pre-commit review on 2026-09-07 at 19:06, so the hook failed rather than reporting a finding, and the commit landed with `--no-verify` by explicit user decision. **Two owed reviews now gate this branch's merge, not one.** Mitigating, but not a substitute: the same change had already been reviewed twice in earlier forms and rejected both times (a 500-row cap, then a 10,000-row one), and this version is what those findings produced — but this exact version has been reviewed by nobody. The local gate was green before the commit (542 tests, lint, typecheck, format, both builds), and every behaviour was exercised in a real browser against live data. That is exactly the combination that passed while the two rejected versions were still wrong, so it is evidence of nothing much.
  - **OWED: a Codex review of the Stage 1 commit.** It was committed with `--no-verify` by explicit user decision, because Codex ran out of credits partway through the pre-commit review — the hook failed rather than reporting a finding. Bypassing the gate is normally forbidden by `CLAUDE.md`, so this is recorded rather than left implicit. **This branch must not merge until that review has run.** Codex had already found and blocked one P1 before it died (the `instanceof Pool` defect described above), which is a fair argument for not skipping the rest.
  - **Stages 2 and 3 done (2026-09-07).** Stage 2 applied the settled palette and the Georgian typeface split, proven in a real browser: a canvas width probe measures 113.1px for Georgian in our stack against 101.28px for a forced fallback, so Noto Sans Georgian is genuinely loaded rather than silently falling back. Stage 3 is the source-health screen, which needed no backend change — `getSourceHealth` already returned everything. It exists to make one thing visible: both sources read **"Never"** for last full-coverage crawl, styled as a warning with the consequence stated, because that is the state in which absence reconciliation silently stops happening.
  - **Browser QA caught four defects the source could not show.** Tailwind v4 tree-shakes unreferenced `@theme` variables, so half the token contract was never emitted (fixed with `@theme static`); the specimen violated this project's own Georgian tracking rule; `next/font` preloaded three weights nothing used (fixed by taking the variable font); and the facts row had regressed from `dl` to plain divs. Each was invisible in review of the code and obvious in the rendered page.
  - **Stage 5 done (2026-09-07): the opportunities list.** `web/app/opportunities/`, plus `web/lib/search-params.ts` and `web/lib/opportunity-row.ts`. The screen's entire state lives in the URL — no client component, no hydration, so a filtered view is a link and the back button works. Filters are text, board, state, first-seen window and cross-posted-only; sorts are the three `searchOpportunities` supports. Verified against the corpus in a real browser: 406 total, 52 for a Georgian query (`მენეჯერი`), and exactly the 4 known cross-posted clusters, each linking out to both boards.
    - **Two derivations deliberately mirror the SQL.** A row's closing date is the MAXIMUM live-member deadline and its first-seen is the MINIMUM, because those are what `LATEST_OPEN_MEMBER_DEADLINE` and `EARLIEST_MEMBER_FIRST_SEEN` sort by — a summary computed any other way would render the column a user just sorted on in an order contradicting its own header. `opportunity-row.test.ts` is what notices if either drifts.
    - ~~**All four cross-posted clusters disagree about the closing date.**~~ **This was wrong, and stood here for a day.** Only **two** of the four do. The marker compared stored instants, and jobs.ge stores a calendar date as Tbilisi local midnight — 20:00 UTC the previous day — while hr.ge stores an end-of-day minute, so two boards printing the same 20 September looked different to it. The marker is still load-bearing for the two clusters that genuinely differ; on the other two it was asserting a source conflict that does not exist, which is the same fabrication it exists to prevent. Found by the Codex gate during Stage 6 and fixed in both screens there.
    - **URL parameters are validated against the schema's own enums.** An unrecognised status reaches `inArray` on a Postgres enum column, where it is a query *error*, not an empty result — so a hand-edited URL could have 500'd the screen. Search text is capped at 120 characters because it becomes an unindexed `ilike '%…%'`.
    - **Browser QA found four defects the source could not show**, all fixed and re-verified. At 390px the table overflowed — board and state columns off-screen, titles running off the edge instead of ellipsizing, because `truncate` needs a definite width; fixed with `table-fixed` and a declared width per column per breakpoint, which also stopped columns shifting as you page. Tailwind's `text-sm` was overriding the body leading to 1.43, under this project's own Georgian floor. The `Closes` column was narrow enough at tablet width that every date wrapped, adding a second line to every row (rows went 57.8px → 35px once widened). And `?cross=1&page=5` — four matching rows, page five — rendered an empty table, the range "201–200 of 4", and an empty state blaming the filters.
    - **The Codex pre-commit gate blocked the first attempt with three P1s, all real, all fixed.** (1) Below the `md` breakpoint the deadline-conflict marker was not rendered at all, so on a phone every cross-posted row showed one board's closing date as if both had stated it — provenance loss, which `AGENTS.md` classes P1. (2) `searchOpportunities`' `firstSeenFrom` was an ANY-member `EXISTS`, so a months-old vacancy matched "first seen in the last 24 hours" the moment a second board picked it up — while the row displayed, and the `recent` sort ordered by, the EARLIEST member. It now compares against `EARLIEST_MEMBER_FIRST_SEEN`, so filter, sort and displayed value finally mean one thing. This changes the query layer and the CLI's `--first-seen-from` with it. (3) The search cap used `slice`, which counts UTF-16 code units and can cut a character in half — forbidden outright by `georgian-typography.md` rule 7; it now caps by grapheme via `Intl.Segmenter`, and the input's `maxLength` was removed because that attribute has the same defect. Both new tests were mutation-checked: reverting either fix fails them.
    - **A second gate run found two more P1s and one finding worth more than its label.** (4) An opportunity whose last live membership was retired is deliberately kept as a row for audit, and `searchOpportunities` still returned it — so the list could show a shell with no route to any source and count it toward a total claiming to be one row per vacancy. Zero-live-member opportunities are now excluded from browse and count unconditionally; the tombstones keep the audit trail. (5) Per-board lifecycle state existed only in a `title` tooltip, which is unavailable on touch and unreliable for keyboard users — and every cross-posted cluster is currently active on jobs.ge and `missing_suspected` on hr.ge, so a row reading "open" was telling half the truth. Each board now prints its own state as visible text when it differs from the cluster. (6) Flagged only P3, but repo-wide: **`rounded-[--radius]` compiles to `border-radius: --radius`, which is invalid CSS the browser discards**, so the settled 0.75rem radius had never rendered on any screen since Stage 2 — measured at `0px` in the browser, now `12px` after switching all 18 occurrences to `rounded-[var(--radius)]`. Browser QA had not caught it because square corners look deliberate with no reference to compare against.
    - **A third gate run found the pagination bug that mattered most.** `searchOpportunities` ordered by non-unique keys — 174 opportunities share one deadline and titles repeat — with no tie-breaker under `LIMIT`/`OFFSET`. Postgres may return tied rows in a different order per query, so a tie straddling a page boundary silently shows some opportunities twice and hides others. Every ordering now ends in `opportunities.id`. Also fixed from that round: `error.tsx` called `reset()`, which in this Next version only clears the boundary without re-fetching server-component content, so "Try again" could never recover from the database error it exists for — it now uses `retry()`, per the version docs `web/AGENTS.md` points at; and the `lg` column widths totalled 102%, which over-constrains a fixed-layout table.
    - **The list fills the window (user request, 2026-09-07).** It was capped at `max-w-6xl` (~1152px), which left most of a 1920px monitor unused and truncated titles that had room to spare. `main` and the nav are now full width, with an `xl` column tier so the short columns do not each take 200px to hold the word "open". Verified at 390 / 767 / 1280 / 2400 CSS px: the visible columns total exactly the wrapper width at every breakpoint, no page scrolls sideways, and no scrollbar appears inside the results wrapper.
    - **Pagination was removed entirely (user decision, 2026-09-07), and that is the real fix for two of the defects above.** All 406 matching rows render on one page: this is a scanning tool, one scroll and the browser's own find-in-page cover the whole corpus, and `data-density.md` already said 406 rows need no virtualization. It also deletes a bug class rather than patching it — with no page boundary, neither the out-of-range page number nor the tie-straddling duplication can occur, so `clampPage` and the pagination controls are gone. `searchOpportunities` is capped at the query layer's own MAX_LIMIT of 500; if the corpus ever exceeds it the screen says "showing the first N" rather than truncating silently. The `opportunities.id` tie-breaker stays regardless, because the CLI still pages with `--limit`/`--offset`.
    - **The 500-row cap was a real defect, caught by the gate before merge.** Dropping pagination on a 406-row list looked safe; it was not. The database holds 406 opportunities only because **no full-coverage crawl has ever run** — jobs.ge carries ~5,647 listings and hr.ge ~3,265, so a fixed 500-row cap would have hidden roughly 95% of the corpus the day a real crawl finished, unreachable from the UI. The list now starts at one 500-row chunk and a link at the bottom grows it by another, carrying the depth in the URL: still one continuous scroll with no page numbers and no client JavaScript, but exhaustive. **The first attempt at this fix was itself rejected, correctly** — it swapped the 500 ceiling for a 10,000 one, which is the same defect at a different number, and past it `Show more` would have rendered a link that parsed back to the ceiling and did nothing. There is now no terminal depth at all: the page clamps against the real total, so the control disappears exactly when nothing is left. Rows are read in 500-row batches, which lets `MAX_LIMIT` in `src/browse/queries.ts` stay at its original 500 rather than being raised to suit one screen — safe under OFFSET only because every ordering ends in `opportunities.id`, without which adjacent batches would overlap and skip. The link is anchored at the first newly revealed row, so growing the list opens where the reader left off rather than back at the top. Verified against live data in a real browser by temporarily shrinking the chunk to 50: 50 rows → `Show 50 more` → 100 rows landing on row 51, and `?show=400` returning 400 rows with 400 distinct ids across 8 batches.
    - **The severity gate changed on 2026-09-07: P2 and lower are now fixed, not skipped.** Blocking is still P0/P1 only. The old rule ("skip P2/lower 100%") existed to prevent review-loop fatigue, and it stopped matching reality during this stage — three findings labelled P2 or P3 were real defects: the inert `rounded-[--radius]`, the `reset()`/`retry()` error boundary, and the cross-posted filter below. Claude had been overriding the rule case by case for exactly those, which is the judgment-weighing the rule was written to prevent, so the user moved the line instead: **"lets take care of p2."** The four then-outstanding P2s were cleared in one pass:
      - **The "on both boards" filter counted memberships, not distinct boards.** The schema permits a cluster to hold two live memberships from the same source — transitive linking and manual reassignment both produce it, since the only uniqueness is one live membership per listing — so the filter could return a cluster whose row showed a single board. `crossPostedOnly` now counts `distinct sl.source_id`; mutation-checked, and the four real cross-posted clusters still return exactly four.
      - **The search placeholder used `--color-nontext`** (`#ffffff40`), which `design-direction.md` reserves for dividers and disabled controls. Measured ~2:1 on the black input, under the 4.5:1 floor. Now `--color-faint`, measured in the browser at **6.27:1**.
      - **Stage 6's completion criterion said only "the description".** Per-source descriptions and visible disagreements are now an explicit condition, because the concept requires the canonical view to surface disagreements and `anti-patterns.md` classes merged source text as lost provenance.
      - **Ranked results moved from Stage 10 to Stage 8.** It is read-only and unblocked, so sitting behind the first writing screen and behind duplicate review contradicted the plan's own two ordering constraints and let a missing `evidence` column stall a screen that was ready. Saved items becomes Stage 9 and remains the first writing screen.
  - **Stage 6 done (2026-09-08): opportunity detail.** `web/app/opportunities/[id]/`, plus `getOpportunity` in `src/browse/queries.ts` and `web/lib/opportunity-detail.ts`. The list answers "which vacancies match"; this answers "is this grouping right, and should I apply" — and it is the only screen where a reader can see why two listings were put together and disagree with it.
    - **The organising distinction is disagreement versus absence**, and it is not pedantry. Two boards stating different values is a conflict the reader must see; one board stating nothing is normal. jobs.ge records no location and no pay on any of its 310 listings, so treating "hr.ge says თბილისი, jobs.ge says nothing" as a conflict would mark every cross-posted cluster contradictory when the boards simply record different fields. Only distinct **present** values are marked, absent ones read "not stated", and a field no board states is not rendered at all. Measured against all four cross-posted clusters: all four disagree about state, **two of four** about the closing date, **none** about the posting date, and none is falsely marked on location.
    - **Descriptions are per board, attributed, never joined.** The ranking layer concatenates internally precisely so this does not have to; `anti-patterns.md` classes a merged block as lost provenance. Every live member also links back to its own listing, and the grouping decision is printed with its confidence, its ruleset version and who made it.
    - **hr.ge's `structuredAttributes` reach a screen for the first time** — hours, contract, seniority, languages, education, and the `specialty`/`industry` values that are the taxonomy work's input. Curated, not dumped: `listingSection: -1`, `isAnonymous`, `hideContactPerson` and `isPriority` describe how hr.ge runs its site, not the vacancy, and printing them would put raw internals on screen. Values are left exactly as the board wrote them, in Georgian, since normalising them here would be inventing data.
    - **The not-found page was returning HTTP 200, and the fix was structural.** `loading.tsx` applies to a segment *and everything nested under it*, so the list's loading fallback wrapped `[id]` too — and a fallback starts streaming the response, after which the status code has already been sent. A stale or mistyped link therefore rendered "No such opportunity" under a 200. The list now sits in a `(list)` route group, which changes no URL and scopes its fallback to itself, and the detail route has **no `loading.tsx` at all**: any Suspense boundary above the existence check reintroduces the bug. Measured, that trade is one-sided — the detail query runs in ~60ms against the live corpus, against the list's 3.8s. Confirmed: 404 for a malformed id and for an unknown uuid, 200 for a real one.
    - **A malformed id is a 404, not a 500.** The id arrives from a URL path and Postgres answers a non-uuid with `invalid input syntax for type uuid` — an error, not an empty result — so `getOpportunity` rejects it before the query rather than after.
    - **Browser QA against the live corpus found three defects the source could not show.** The comparison table stretched full width, putting the two values a reader is trying to compare 687px apart at 1600 — capped per column, and the cap scales with however many boards carry the vacancy. The provenance prose ran to 1533px, unconstrained by any measure. And two section headings a line apart both read "what each board says". Also verified: no raw enum, slug or uuid reaches the screen; no uppercase transform and no letter-spacing touches Georgian anywhere; and the mixed-script case renders in one typeface, confirmed by canvas measurement rather than by eye.
    - **The mobile answer is decided rather than reflowed.** At 375 CSS px — narrower than the gate's 390 — the page does not scroll sideways and the comparison table scrolls inside its own box instead. Stacking the boards was rejected: it puts the two values a screen apart, which removes the only reason the comparison is a table.
    - **A row's title is now the link into detail**, carrying the list's own query string, so returning lands on the filtered, sorted list rather than at the top of an unfiltered one. `buildQueryString` was extracted from `buildHref` for it, and the value is rebuilt rather than trusted — it is only ever used as the query part of this app's own list route. *(It originally carried the row's position too; the third gate round replaced that with the opportunity's id, for the reason recorded below.)*
    - **The `web-design-guidelines` pass found four**, all fixed: `toFixed(2)` hard-coded a decimal separator that is wrong for most of Europe (now `Intl`, and deliberately not a percentage — a dedupe confidence is a weighted evidence score, not a probability); the heading had no `text-pretty`; and the application address needed `translate="no"` and a wrap rule, being an identifier of unbounded length in a flex row.
    - **The Codex gate blocked the first attempt with two P1s and a P2, and the first was worse than reported.** All three were verified against the database before being accepted.
      - **Dates were rendered a day early, and that fabricated conflicts.** jobs.ge publishes calendar dates with no time and the adapter stores them as Tbilisi local midnight (`src/adapters/jobs-ge/dates.ts:99-101`), which Postgres returns as 20:00 UTC the previous day. Formatted in UTC, a deadline the board printed as **20 September** appeared as **19 Sept, 20:00 UTC** — the wrong day, on the field someone uses to decide whether it is too late to apply. The consequence Codex did not state is the serious one: comparing those instants made the screen **invent disagreements**. Measured over the four cross-posted clusters, it marked 4 of 4 closing dates and 4 of 4 posting dates as differing, where the truth is **2 and 0** — six of twelve markers invented, on the screen whose entire purpose is surfacing real ones. Source-stated dates now render as a calendar date in `Asia/Tbilisi` with the exact instant in a tooltip, and are compared by Georgian calendar day. Xtelo's own observations — first seen, last seen, fetched — stay in UTC, because they are this system's facts rather than a board's claims.
      - **The same defect was live in the Stage 5 list screen**, which is how a Stage 6 finding came to change a shipped screen: `toRow` distinct-ed deadlines by instant too, so the list had been marking all four cross-posted rows "boards differ" since Stage 5. Both screens now compare on the same key, and the false claim this file made about that measurement is retracted above rather than edited away. Verified in the browser: the list marks exactly 2 of 4, and the two detail screens agree with it.
      - **The stored membership evidence was dropped.** `opportunity_source_memberships.evidence` holds what the dedupe pass actually recorded — "shared vacancy-level application value (carried by 2 listings)", "titles agree (similarity 1.00)", "same normalized organization", plus the signal values — and `getOpportunity` did not select it, so the screen showed only this build's generic wording for the decision enum. Those diverge the moment a membership comes from a human reassignment or an older ruleset, which would let the screen built to explain a grouping misstate it. The recorded reasons are now rendered beneath the decision.
      - **"Apply on the employer's site" invented provenance** (P2, fixed under the revised rule). `applicationMethod.type === 'url'` establishes only that an external URL exists; in this corpus most are ATS vendors — `smrtr.io`, `cleverstaff.net`, `hel-ai.com`, `selfrecruit.ge` — not the employer's domain. The destination host is now named instead, which is both honest and more useful, since it says where the application is going.
    - **A second gate round found two more P1s and a P2, all verified before being accepted.**
      - **A detached opportunity was a page with nothing on it.** An opportunity whose last live membership is retired is kept deliberately — `resolveCanonicalOpportunity` preserves its final revision rather than writing an empty one — and this screen goes on rendering it as "a record of what was seen". With live members alone that record contained no source link, no title, no description and no evidence: an audit trail asserting itself while displaying nothing, which is the lost-provenance failure rather than a cosmetic gap. `getOpportunity` now returns retired memberships as `formerMembers` and the screen shows them as history — what was detached, from which board, when, and the reasons recorded at the time. That is also the shape Stage 10 needs, since reversibility is only auditable if the reversal is visible.
      - **The state note turned a suspicion into a takedown.** It read "This vacancy is still up on one board and not on the other", which asserts the listing is gone. `missing_suspected` means only that one crawl did not see it — the page may well still be there — and `labels.ts` says exactly that two lines away, so the screen contradicted its own vocabulary. `anti-patterns.md` forbids rounding a status; this was the same error pointing the other way. Now: "The boards report different states, and a missed crawl is a suspicion rather than a takedown."
      - **Application links were promoted to hrefs without checking the scheme** (P2, fixed). Neither the schema nor the adapters constrain `applicationMethod.value` to a web address, and both failure modes are silent: a relative value resolves against Xtelo's own origin, so the link points at this app instead of the source, and a custom scheme reaches the operating system's protocol handlers. Only absolute `http(s)` now becomes a link, and an address must be free of whitespace before it becomes a `mailto:` — a stored line break could otherwise append headers to the message a reader is about to send. **All 128 url routes in the corpus are already absolute https and all 225 addresses are well formed**, which is the reason to check rather than the reason not to.
    - **Two test sources and two opportunities were found stranded in the dev database**, rendering on the source-health screen as real sources named "Test source". They were left by this stage's own first test run: `afterEach` threw on an FK ordering before it reached `cleanupTestSource`, so cleanup that only works when nothing goes wrong did nothing in the case that needed it. Every cleanup step now runs independently and reports rather than aborting the rest. **The stranded rows themselves are still in the dev database** — removing them needs a permission this session does not have.
    - **A third gate round found two P1s and four P2s.** All six were real; the review got sharper as the screen did.
      - **A retired membership's evidence explains the grouping, not the removal.** `retireLiveMembership` only stamps `supersededAt` — it never rewrites `evidence` — so the reasons on a retired row are the ones that put the listing in the cluster. Rendering them under a detachment date attributed "shared application value" to an act it had nothing to do with. They are now labelled "originally grouped here because", which is what they are. The removal's own reason lives on the *new* membership for a reassignment and **nowhere at all** for a plain detach; that gap is real and is recorded here rather than papered over.
      - **The `mailto:` check was not enough, and the payload is a CV.** "No whitespace and one `@`" accepts `victim@example.ge?bcc=attacker%40example.ge` — percent-encoding hides the second address from any counting rule — producing a link that BCCs a stranger on a job application. Addresses now pass an allowlist that excludes URI delimiters by construction rather than detecting them. Measured: every address stored in the corpus still passes, and all six injection shapes are rejected.
      - **Retired members were resolved against the listing's CURRENT revision** (P2, fixed). A detached listing goes on being crawled, so its entry in this opportunity's history would silently rewrite itself to a title the cluster never contained — an audit trail describing something that was never detached. Retired memberships now resolve against the newest revision created at or before `supersededAt`. **Mutation-checked**: dropping that preference makes the new test read "Retitled after detachment" instead of the original title.
      - **Resilient cleanup had become silent cleanup** (P2, fixed). The previous round made every teardown step run independently, which fixed the stranding — and introduced a worse failure, since a broken cleanup would now leave the suite green while rows accumulated in the shared dev database. Failures are collected and rethrown as an `AggregateError` once every step has had its turn.
      - **The back link anchored on a row POSITION** (P2, fixed). A crawl running while the detail page is open adds, removes and reorders rows, so row 37 on the way back is a different vacancy from row 37 on the way in — the URL-state promise failing exactly while the corpus moves. The fragment is now the opportunity's own id, which the detail screen already knows, so the `at` parameter is gone entirely. The positional anchor survives on an inner span for "show more", where position is genuinely what is meant.
      - **Retired memberships were keyed by listing id** (P2, fixed). Detach, restore, detach again is a supported reversible workflow, so one listing can hold several retired rows in the same opportunity — which collapsed into one entry and duplicate React keys. Keyed on the membership id now, and `getOpportunity` returns it.
    - **A fourth gate round found two P1s, three P2s and a P3.** The review kept finding real things, so the rounds kept running rather than being declared sufficient.
      - **A stale canonical view was indistinguishable from a current one.** `resolveCanonicalOpportunity` runs during a dedupe pass, not after every crawl, so a listing can gain a revision while `opportunities.canonicalTitle` and `canonicalStatus` still describe the previous one — and this screen presented that as current with nothing to indicate it. `opportunity_revisions.sourceMembershipVersions` exists for precisely this comparison, and §12.4 requires "stale inputs" to be distinguishable from "changed ruleset"; it was being written and never read. The query now returns it, compares it against the live members' current revisions, and the screen says plainly when they diverge.
      - **The `confirmed_same` label named evidence it could not know.** It said the match rested on "a shared application link or address" — how the ruleset reaches that decision today, and not how a human reassignment does. With the stored evidence now rendered directly beneath it, the label could flatly contradict the record. None of the decision labels names a basis any more: the enum says what was decided, `evidence` says why.
      - **Live and retired members were read in two statements** (P2, fixed). A membership retired between them came back as both live and former at once — the audit screen contradicting itself during exactly the review operation it exists to explain. Both now come from one statement, with the revision each row resolves against chosen per row.
      - **The historical-revision pick is a reconstruction, not a binding** (P2, acknowledged rather than closed). A detail fetch that starts before a detachment and commits after it produces a revision whose `created_at` precedes `superseded_at` even though the cluster never held it, and no timestamp comparison can separate the two. Making the history immutable needs the retiring code to record the exact revision id — a column and a change to `membership-review.ts` — which belongs with the review screen that writes those rows (Stage 10). Written down in the code rather than quietly approximated.
      - **`#` was allowed in a mail recipient** (P2, fixed). It is legal in an address's local part and opens a fragment in a URI, so `mailto:foo#bar@example.com` addresses the message to `foo` — a link that silently sends to the wrong recipient rather than failing. Measured again after tightening: every stored address still passes and all seven injection shapes are rejected.
      - **A paragraph in this file still described the row-position link** a later round had replaced (P3, fixed above). Docs matching the code is a rule here, not a courtesy.
    - **A fifth gate round found three P1s and a P2**, every one of them a variation on the same theme: the screen stating things the data does not support.
      - **"No board currently carries this vacancy" was a fabricated takedown.** Detaching the last membership says nothing about the boards — the listing may still be live on jobs.ge, or reassigned to another record. What ended is its membership *here*. It now says no listings are currently grouped into this record, which is the only thing the data establishes.
      - **Retired memberships dropped their audit metadata.** The projection kept only `decision` (never rendered) and the reasons, discarding confidence, who decided, when, and the ruleset — and the observations section is built from LIVE members. So a fully detached record, the one case where retired rows are the only history that exists, had an audit section with nothing in it. They now carry and render the same line a live membership does.
      - **Grouping evidence was gated on cross-posting**, and a cluster can hold two live listings from the SAME board — transitive linking and manual reassignment both produce it, since the only uniqueness is one live membership per listing. A real merge was therefore uninspectable whenever both listings came from one source. Visibility now depends on whether more than one listing was grouped, which is what the section is about; `crossPosted` keeps its own meaning for the boards line.
      - **The 404 named a cause that cannot produce it** (P2, fixed). It said review might have split the grouping apart — but detach and split deliberately RETAIN the opportunity, and `getOpportunity` returns one with no live members rather than refusing it, so a reviewed cluster keeps resolving. Nothing deletes an opportunity in normal operation, which leaves a mistyped link as the honest explanation. Inventing a plausible-sounding cause is the same failure as inventing data.
    - **49 new tests** — 40 over the derivations against the shapes the corpus actually holds, including a real hr.ge attributes object, the two-precisions-of-one-day case, every rejected application-link scheme and mailto injection, and the twice-detached listing; and 9 over `getOpportunity`, one of them mutation-checked covering the malformed id, the unknown id, retired memberships, the canonical revision, and a cluster whose members have all been detached. The shared test cleanup also had to learn to drop an opportunity's revision pointer before its revisions, since the composite ownership FK refuses either order alone.
    - **`npm run lint` was already failing on this branch before Stage 5**, so CI was red: Biome could not parse `@theme static` (Tailwind v4 syntax, now enabled via `css.parser.tailwindDirectives`), flagged the `prefers-reduced-motion` `!important` block that is the whole point of that block, linted `icon.svg` as if it were JSX, and could not see `aria-hidden` through a spread in `status-chip.tsx`. Fixed at the config level except the last, which is now written out per element. Enabling the CSS parser also means Biome formats `globals.css` for the first time.
  - **The write gate is live, and as of Stage 9 it is load-bearing rather than precautionary.** `XTELO_WRITES_ENABLED` must be exactly `'true'`, so `npm run dev:web` against the live corpus cannot write no matter what is clicked, and `npm run dev:web:qa` runs against a disposable copy. This makes `browser-qa.md`'s procedural rule a mechanism — the rule that has already failed twice here. **The disposable database now exists** (`scraplify_qa`, seeded by `pg_dump | psql` from the live corpus) and `.env.qa.example` is committed as the written record of how it is made; `.env.qa` itself stays gitignored.
  - **Deferred or rejected:** **shadcn and the React Bits registry**, dropped 2026-09-06 after the Stage 1 spike. Its real value is accessible *behaviour* for primitives that are hard to hand-roll — focus-trapped dialogs, comboboxes, dropdown menus — and none of the planned screens need one: they are tables, links, plain inputs, custom status chips, and a review queue whose keyboard handling is bespoke either way. Since the palette is a custom dark system, shadcn's styling would be overridden anyway, so it amounted to a Radix dependency tree bought for behaviour nothing was using. Add it the day a real dialog or combobox appears; React Bits stays out for the reasons in `anti-patterns.md`. Also deferred: Figma MCP until a Figma file exists; Storybook entirely, since this is a single-user internal tool rather than a component library; and the Python `webapp-testing` skill, which would duplicate the already-connected Playwright MCP that §25 warns against stacking variants of.
- **Phase 1C items 1, 2 and 4** — closure has still never run against live data on either source, and neither source has had a full-coverage run (jobs.ge ≈ 7.9h, hr.ge ≈ 2.75h). Operational, not blocking: it needs elapsed time, not code. hr.ge's 100 listings also still carry stale `missing_suspected`/streak-2 residue that a real run would clear.
- **Phase 2 item 2 — taxonomy mapping.** *(Corrected 2026-09-16: this entry still described the schema as missing well after Phase 3C-2 built it.)* Done for hr.ge in Phase 3C-2: migration 0020, `seedTaxonomyTerms` + `classifyListings`, first real backfill 765 terms / 16,868 classifications. Still open: **jobs.ge carries no category data at all** (0 of 310 listings), so it stays uncategorized rather than inferred; and classification of newly crawled listings was never automated — being closed by the Phase 7A follow-ups below.
- **Phase 5 — CV parsing.** PDF/DOCX extraction, so profiles come from an uploaded CV rather than hand-entered JSON. *(Stale entry corrected 2026-09-23: this has been merged since 2026-09-16, PR #14 — see the completed-phases record.)*
- **Phase 6 — outreach assistance.** *(Stale entry corrected 2026-09-23: merged 2026-09-23, PR #17 — see "Earlier phase: Phase 6" above.)*
- **Phase 7 — operations and supervised repair.** *(Stale entry corrected 2026-09-23: 7A merged as PR #16, its taxonomy-classification follow-up merged as PR #18 — see "Earlier phase" sections above. Phase 7 as a whole is **not** closed: 7B remains open, below.)* **7B (supervised repair, pg-boss, hosting) stays open and deferred**, not started: evidence-gated on the 7A schedules actually running (concept §25), and they were found silently dead for a week on 2026-09-23 (see the incident above) — fixed the same day, but no multi-day evidence has accrued yet.
- **Phase 8 — hosted edition and private browser CV matching.** *(Stale entry corrected 2026-09-23: 8A is merged (PR #19, concludes with an honest lexical-first recommendation for its one tested candidate) and 8B is the current phase — see the current-phase section above, not "not started.")* Accepted direction recorded 2026-09-23 in `docs/scraplify-concept.md` §30 (amendment) and `change.md` (full implementation handoff, repository root). Sub-phases 8A (done) through 8E (hosted readiness), each its own branch — see concept §30.6 for the one-line exit criterion per sub-phase and `change.md` §13 for full deliverables. No public route, admin surface, migration, or production dependency exists yet outside what 8B is actively building. **Narrower than an earlier version of this line claimed:** 8A already built real embedding infrastructure — `src/matching/embed.ts`, committed golden vectors for synthetic profiles and real opportunities, and proven Node/browser-Worker parity (see "Earlier phase: Phase 8A" above) — what does *not* exist yet is a production opportunity-embedding pipeline, a matching bundle, browser CV parsing, or hosted auth (8B's own auth integration, in progress, is local/admin auth for the operator surface, not the public-facing hosted auth this line originally meant).

## Completed

### Phase 0 — policy and domain foundation

Merged to `main` via PR #1 (`a45332f`). `/codex:adversarial-review --base main` ran once and returned 3 P1 findings, all fixed. A second confirming run was intended but skipped by explicit user decision after the Codex CLI hung unresponsively twice (`/codex:cancel` found no job to cancel both times); the last commits before merge landed via `--no-verify` instead, verified by hand against a real local Postgres instance (seeded legacy/invalid data, confirmed both the intended rejections and successes) plus the full local gate (format, lint, typecheck, test, build, clean install). CI ran green on GitHub for the first time on the pre-merge push.

Delivered: TypeScript/Node tooling scaffold (package.json, tsconfig, Biome, Vitest), CI (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`), domain contracts as Zod schemas (`src/domain/`), source-policy records for jobs.ge and hr.ge (`src/policies/`), threat model and approval boundaries doc (`docs/THREAT_MODEL.md`), Postgres + Drizzle migrations 0000-0006 (`docker-compose.yml`, `drizzle.config.ts`, `src/db/`) including a composite ownership FK on `source_listings.currentRevisionId` added after adversarial review.

### Phase 1A — jobs.ge vertical slice

Merged to `main` via PR #2 (`c41d0b7`), squash-merged from `phase-1a-jobsge`. Milestone 1 (reconnaissance and fixture capture) is done — see `src/adapters/jobs-ge/RECON_NOTES.md` for the full findings and `src/policies/jobs-ge.ts` for the resulting policy/authorization update. Per §25: capture sanitized index/detail fixtures, implement VIP and standard discovery partitions, investigate filters/date ordering and define completeness, implement source-compliant HTTP fetching, store source listings/revisions/resources/attempts/crawl runs, implement incremental overlap and conservative closure logic, schedule local read-only runs.

The requirement carried forward from Phase 0's adversarial review — `source_listing_revisions` has no DB-level uniqueness on `(sourceListingId, meaningfulContentHash)` (removed deliberately — see `src/db/schema/source-listings.ts`; a lifetime-unique index would reject a listing whose content legitimately reverts to an earlier hash), so retry-safe idempotency has to be enforced in the write path itself — is now implemented: `src/db/write-source-listing-revision.ts`'s `writeSourceListingRevision` insert-or-ignores the listing row, then `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE`s it, re-reads its current revision's hash under that lock, and only inserts a new revision (then repoints `currentRevisionId`) if the freshly observed hash differs — all inside one transaction. Proven by a real concurrency test in `src/db/write-source-listing-revision.test.ts` (two `Promise.all`-raced calls for the same new listing collapse to one revision), which is why CI now runs a Postgres service (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`) rather than only verifying DB behavior by hand as Phase 0 did.

#### Exit gate

- [x] Sanitized index and detail HTML fixtures captured (read-only reconnaissance per concept §25's skill-adoption note — no sign-in, upload, submit, or send against jobs.ge) — 5 real fixtures saved to `src/adapters/jobs-ge/fixtures/`: the browse page, its last paginated page, and 3 detail pages chosen to represent 3 distinct application-method structures found (mailto, inline external link in description text, direct external ATS link). Full recon writeup in `src/adapters/jobs-ge/RECON_NOTES.md`
- [x] VIP and standard discovery partitions implemented — `src/adapters/jobs-ge/discovery.ts`'s `parseAdsPage` parses one `/ge/ads/?page=N` HTML page into its VIP and standard partitions (each listing's numeric ID + policy-checked detail URL + title). Partition membership comes only from which container a row is in (`.vipEntries` vs `#job_list_table`), never from the title anchor's own CSS class — confirmed against the real fixtures that jobs.ge reuses `class="vip"` on the title link in *both* sections, a genuine parsing trap. Verified against both real fixture pages: 10 VIP + 300 standard on page 1, 10 VIP + 247 standard on the last page, zero ID overlap, matching RECON_NOTES.md's counts exactly. Caught and fixed a real bug in the process: the 5 fixture files had been saved as JSON-escaped strings rather than raw HTML at capture time (see RECON_NOTES.md's 2026-09-04 correction) — string-search validation didn't catch it, but the actual cheerio parser did.
- [x] Filters/date ordering investigated; completeness defined and documented — confirmed via live recon (`src/adapters/jobs-ge/RECON_NOTES.md`): `#job_list_table` is itself date/ID-descending and VIP-independent, resolving concept §27's open question. Category/location filters are redundant for discovery (unfiltered page already covers both). Announcement-type filter (`jid`): project decision (2026-09-03) is to aggregate all types (vacancies/scholarships/trainings/tenders/other), not vacancies-only, matching the domain model's "Opportunity" naming — so `jid` is unused. Pagination bisected: 18 full pages of 300 + 1 partial page of 247 = 5,647 total current listings (site clamps out-of-range page numbers to the last real page rather than erroring)
- [x] Source-compliant HTTP fetching implemented (respects `src/policies/jobs-ge.ts`'s policy: allowed paths/query shape via `isJobsGeUrlAllowed`, rate limit, disallowed hosts) — `src/net/http-fetcher.ts`: GET-only, hand-rolled redirect loop (not undici's own redirect follower) so the caller's URL allow-list is re-checked before the initial request and before every redirect hop, per concept §16/§23.1's "reapply SSRF and policy checks after every redirect." Two independent SSRF enforcement points, since neither alone is sufficient: `src/net/ssrf-lookup.ts` hooks undici `Agent`'s `connect.lookup` to validate DNS-resolved addresses atomically with the real connection (closing the DNS-rebinding TOCTOU a separate pre-resolve step would leave open); `src/domain/ip-policy.ts` + a literal-IP-hostname pre-check in `http-fetcher.ts` cover the case DNS resolution never happens at all (Node's socket layer skips the `lookup` hook when the connection target is already an IP literal, confirmed empirically). Per-source pacing via `src/net/rate-limiter.ts` (concurrency + crawl-delay measured from the prior request's completion, not its start), injected rather than built internally so a future browser-based acquisition path for the same source can share its budget. Bounded redirects, per-request timeout, and response size. Wired into a real crawl end-to-end by the orchestrator below.
- [x] jobs.ge detail-page parser implemented — `src/adapters/jobs-ge/detail.ts`'s `parseJobsGeDetailPage` extracts title/organization/dates/application-method/description from a detail page's `.dtable` scaffold (confirmed, via label-text lookup rather than positional indexing, to be structurally stable across all 18 pages RECON_NOTES.md sampled even though the free-text description body varies wildly). Application-method extraction (mailto vs external URL vs unspecified) is scoped to the description cell only, specifically to avoid RECON_NOTES.md's documented gotcha — unrelated sidebar/ad links elsewhere on the page. `locations` and `sourceCategories` are deliberately left `[]` and `salaryRaw` null: none of the 18 sampled pages exposed a structured field for any of the three. Yearless "DD <Georgian month name>" dates (`src/adapters/jobs-ge/dates.ts`'s `parseYearlessGeorgianDate`) are resolved to a year by picking whichever of {year-1, year, year+1} lands closest to the fetch instant's own Asia/Tbilisi calendar date — handles both a recent-past `publishedDate` and a near-future `deadlineDate` without a hardcoded direction assumption, and is tested across real year boundaries (concept §24.1). Both proven against all 3 real detail fixtures (one per known application-method pattern) with exact golden-value assertions, per concept §24.2's "pin expected output to a parser version" (`JOBS_GE_DETAIL_PARSER_VERSION`). Wired into a real crawl end-to-end by the orchestrator below.
- [x] Source listings, revisions, resources, fetch attempts, and crawl runs stored via `src/db/schema/` — write path in `src/db/ingest.ts` (`upsertResource`, `startCrawlRun`/`finishCrawlRun`, `recordFetchAttempt`) and `src/db/write-source-listing-revision.ts`. Deliberately source-agnostic (takes domain-shaped input, no jobs.ge-specific code) so Phase 1B's hr.ge adapter reuses it rather than duplicating it. Wired into a real crawl end-to-end by the orchestrator below.
- [x] Crawl orchestrator implemented — `src/adapters/jobs-ge/crawl.ts`'s `runJobsGeCrawl` wires every piece above together for real: `startCrawlRun` → walk `/ge/ads/?page=N` to discovery's natural stop (or `MAX_DISCOVERY_PAGES`'s safety cap) → fetch and parse every discovered listing's detail page → `writeSourceListingRevision` → `expireOverdueListings` → `closeMissingListings` → `finishCrawlRun`. Every fetch (discovery pages and detail pages alike) is recorded as a resource + fetch attempt regardless of outcome, per concept §21.1. A per-listing fetch/parse failure never aborts the whole run (one bad detail page is routine, not catastrophic) — a fetch failure counts via `failedCount`, a parse failure via `quarantinedCount` (see below); an unexpected error from the write path itself aborts the run (marked `failed`) and rethrows, rather than continuing against what might be a broken DB connection. `fullCoverage` is asserted `true` at start (jobs.ge's Phase 1A crawl is always a full-corpus walk, never a deliberately-partial poll — §10.1's incremental-discovery optimization remains deferred, see below); whether that attempt actually *succeeded* is decided separately by `status` after the walk finishes (or doesn't), which is what `closeMissingListings`'s own persisted-row check actually gates on. Also surfaced and fixed a real, previously-unaddressed gap: nothing in this codebase had ever inserted jobs.ge's own `sources`/`source_policies` rows into the database (every prior DB test used a throwaway random-UUID source instead) — `ensureJobsGeSourceSeeded` now does this idempotently at the start of every run.

  First adversarial review of this orchestrator (2026-09-05) returned 4 P1s, all fixed before merge — each is a real correctness/safety property worth recording, not just a drafting note:
  - **Discovery's stop condition compared the wrong thing.** The original "a page contributes zero new IDs against everything accumulated so far" logic would treat a single broken/empty mid-corpus response (a challenge page, a transient empty render at, say, page 15 of 19) as a legitimate stop too, silently truncating discovery while still reporting `complete: true` — which, combined with a floor comparing only total count, could pass `DEFAULT_MIN_EXPECTED_DISCOVERED_LISTINGS` and let a truncated run drive real reconciliation. Fixed: `discoverAllListings` now compares each page's own ID set directly against the *immediately preceding* page's, requiring them to be non-empty **and** exactly equal — the actual signal RECON_NOTES.md documents for jobs.ge's clamp-to-last-page behavior. Any other case (empty, different, broken) simply never satisfies the stop condition, leaving `complete: false` and the run `partial` — the safe direction to be wrong in. `DEFAULT_MIN_EXPECTED_DISCOVERED_LISTINGS` (100, ~56x margin below the real 5,647) remains as defense-in-depth against a total collapse this stronger check wouldn't itself catch (e.g. two consecutive identical small responses).
  - **A detail-fetch/parse failure could make a still-listed opportunity look missing.** A listing present in discovery but whose detail fetch or parse failed never had `writeSourceListingRevision` called, so its `lastSeenAt` never advanced — `closeMissingListings` has only `lastSeenAt` to judge "observed this run" by, so repeated transient failures would eventually close a listing that was never actually gone from the index.
